# Verified Results

Results listed here have cleared all gates in `CHARTER.md`:
1. Exact statement quoted from the primary source (with version/section) and confirmed by a checker.
2. Mechanical verification (`verify.py` / exact computation or lemma check) where applicable.
3. Independent Pro audit (`AUDIT.md`), without seeing the author's commentary.
4. Third novelty gate passed in the hour before reporting.
5. Coordinator verification run and inspected.

Allowed labels:
- `counterexample, verified by exact computation`
- `written proof, internally audited`
- `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`)

None of these labels constitutes peer review, and each entry states what is not established.

### `ferrero-2511.16335-conj1.8` — Counterexample to Ferrero–Hall–Hogben–Hunnell–Small Conjecture 1.8

- **Summary**: Ferrero, Hall, Hogben, Hunnell, and Small conjectured that every connected graph whose upper standard zero forcing propagation time equals 1 must be a "standard fast join" (either a complete graph or a join of edgeless graphs and double cliques). We refuted this conjecture by exhibiting the 12-vertex, 24-edge circulant graph $C_6[2K_1] \cong \mathrm{Circ}(12, \{1, 5\})$, which is connected and satisfies $\overline{\mathrm{PT}}(G) = 1$ yet has a connected complement and zero forcing number $8 < n - 2$, so it is not a standard fast join. We further proved that the entire infinite family of cycle blowups $C_6[r_0 K_1, \dots, r_5 K_1]$ with $r_i \ge 2$ gives counterexamples.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Daniela Ferrero, H. Tracy Hall, Leslie Hogben, Mark Hunnell, Ben Small, *"Zero forcing propagation time intervals and graphs with fixed propagation time"*, `arXiv:2511.16335v1` (`NewFixedPropTime.tex`, Section 1, Definition 1.5 lines 275–276 and Conjecture 1.8 `\label{c:std}` lines 293–296; verified verbatim in *Australasian Journal of Combinatorics*, Vol. 96(2), 2026, pp. 374–394, revised 2 June 2026).
  - Definition 1.5: *"A graph $G$ of order $n\geq 2$ is a standard fast join if $G\cong K_n$ or $G=\bigvee_{i=1}^t G_i$ where $t\geq 2$ and each graph $G_i$ satisfies $G_i\cong n_iK_1$ or $G_i\cong K_{n_i}\dot{\cup} K_{m_i}$ with $n_i,m_i\geq 2$."*
  - Conjecture 1.8: *"Let $G$ be a connected graph. If $\overline{\mathrm{PT}}(G)=1$, then $G$ is a standard fast join."*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, and no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`; formalizes both the explicit 12-vertex witness $C_6[2K_1] \cong \mathrm{Circ}(12, \{1, 5\})$ in `refutes_conjecture_1_8_circ12` / `not_ferrero_conjecture_1_8` and the infinite family $C_6[r_0 K_1, \dots, r_5 K_1]$, $r_i \ge 2$, in `refutes_conjecture_1_8_infinite_family`; file: `lean/OpenMath/Ferrero251116335Conj18.lean`, audited for statement fidelity in board `#200` and coordinator-verified). Also supported by `counterexample, verified by exact computation` (`notes/check-check-2.py`) and `written proof, internally audited` (`AUDIT.md`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake build OpenMath.Ferrero251116335Conj18` (and Python witness check: `.venv/bin/python targets/ferrero-2511.16335-conj1.8/notes/check-check-2.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-26T20:20:58Z`–`20:21:30Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**`, live `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing` (issue `#8371` closed `2026-09-16` as terminal OPEN with no resolution), GitHub search, live arXiv API (`2511.16335v1` unchanged), OpenAlex `W4416550551` (`cited_by_count: 0`), AJC Vol. 96(2) revised PDF, and web search; 0 prior resolutions found.
- **What is not established**: Global minimality of $n = 12$ across all connected graphs of order $n \le 11$ is not proved (only minimality within the $C_6[r_0 K_1, \dots, r_5 K_1]$ with $r_i \ge 2$ family). Internal auditing and exact computation are not external peer review.

### `wei-2310.17337-conj1.5` — Counterexample to Wei–Zhang–Zhao Conjecture 1.5

- **Summary**: Wei, Zhang, and Zhao conjectured that for every connected graph $G \ncong C_k$ of size $m$, the $C_k$-isolation number satisfies $\iota(G, C_k) \le (m + 1)/(k + 2)$, having proved $k = 4$ and building on Borg–Fenech–Kaemawichanurat's $k = 3$ theorem. We refuted this conjecture at $k = 8$ using the 12-vertex, 18-edge truncated tetrahedral graph, for which deleting the closed neighborhood of any single vertex leaves an 8-cycle subgraph so $\iota(G, C_8) = 2 > 19/10 = (m+1)/(k+2)$. The generalized Petersen graphs $GP(9, 2)$ (at $k = 14$, $\iota = 2 > 7/4$) and $GP(11, 2)$ (at $k = 18$, $\iota = 2 > 17/10$) also refute the bound.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Xiaohua Wei, Gang Zhang, Biao Zhao, *"On the $C_4$-isolation number of a graph"*, `arXiv:2310.17337v1` (`C_4-isolation.tex`, Section 1, Conjecture 1.5 `\label{conj1.5}`, lines 119–121, and Abstract line 67).
  - Statement: *"If $G \ncong C_k$ is a connected graph of size $m$, then $\iota(G, C_k) \le \frac{m+1}{k+2}$."*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, no `native_decide`, and no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`; formalizes the 12-vertex, 18-edge truncated tetrahedral graph at $k = 8$ in `refutes_wei_conjecture_1_5_witness` and `not_wei_conjecture_1_5`; file: `lean/OpenMath/Wei231017337Conj15.lean`, audited for statement fidelity in board `#227` and coordinator-verified). Also supported by `counterexample, verified by exact computation` (`notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `cd lean && export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && lake build OpenMath.Wei231017337Conj15` (and Python witness check: `.venv/bin/python targets/wei-2310.17337-conj1.5/notes/check-check-1.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-26T20:23:09Z`–`20:24:00Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**`, live `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, GitHub code/repo/issue searches, live arXiv API (`2310.17337v1` unchanged), OpenAlex `W7163506673` (`cited_by_count: 3`: Borg–Scicluna `10.1016/j.dam.2026.08.006`, Liang–Wu `10.1016/j.dam.2025.06.058`, `10.12677/aam.2025.149398`), Crossref `10.1142/s1793830926500679`, and web search; no prior refutation or settlement found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Published DMAA journal version caveat (`AUDIT.md` §4):** `arXiv:2310.17337v1` was later published in *Discrete Mathematics, Algorithms and Applications* (online `2026-07-21`, DOI `10.1142/s1793830926500679`). The published DMAA abstract replaces the closing statement of Conjecture 1.5 with *"For general $k$, it is worth noting that the problem on $C_k$-isolation remains open,"* and because the DMAA full-text PDF is paywalled, it is unverified whether Section 1 of the published journal text retained, modified, or dropped Conjecture 1.5; this refutation applies strictly to `arXiv:2310.17337v1` (Section 1, Conjecture 1.5).
  - Does not settle whether $\iota(G, C_k) \le (m+1)/(k+2)$ holds for the small individual values $k \in \{5, 6, 7\}$, and does not refute the girth-restricted bound $\iota(G, \mathcal{C}) \le (m+1)/(g+2)$ studied by Liang & Wu (2026) (the truncated tetrahedron has girth $3 < 8$ and $GP(9,2)$ has girth $5 < 14$).
  - Internal audit and exact computation are not external peer review.

### `oeis-A100691` — Proof of Mathar's 2022 Formula for OEIS `A100691` and Triangular-Strip SAW Discrepancy ($s(5) = 160 \ne 158$)

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in 2022 that OEIS `A100691(n)` (listed as counting $n$-step self-avoiding paths on the triangular lattice strip $\mathbb{Z} \times \{0,1\}$) equals `A193641(n+2) - 3` for all $n > 0$. We resolved both readings of the entry: first, we proved the empirical recurrence `A193641(n) = 2*A193641(n-1) + A193641(n-3)` ($n \ge 3$) via an exact 3-state partition and showed it implies `a_OEIS(n) = A193641(n+2) - 3` for all $n \ge 1$ for the stored OEIS `A100691` sequence. Second, under the combinatorial definition in `A100691`'s title (rooted $n$-step self-avoiding walks from $(0,0)$ on $\mathbb{Z} \times \{0,1\}$), we showed the actual count $s(n)$ satisfies $s(5) = 160 \ne 158 = \text{A100691}(5)$ and proved via an exact 3-edge-cut column transfer matrix that $F_{\mathrm{SAW}}(z) - G_{\mathrm{OEIS}}(z) = 2z^5 / ((1-z)^2 (1-2z-z^3))$, so $s(n) > \text{A193641}(n+2) - 3$ for all $n \ge 5$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A100691` (revision 11, 2022-07-22, Emeric Deutsch / R. J. Mathar) and OEIS `A193641` (revision 48, 2026-09-16, R. H. Hardin / Alois P. Heinz).
  - `A100691` title & data: *"Number of self-avoiding paths with n steps on a triangular lattice in the strip Z x {0,1}."* (`1,4,12,30,70,158,352,780,1724,3806,...`, G.f. $(1+z^2)(1+z+z^2)/((1-z)(1-2z-z^3))$).
  - Mathar's 2022 conjecture (`A100691`, Formula): *"Conjecture: a(n) = A193641(n+2)-3, n>0 - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 22 2022"*.
  - `A193641` title & empirical recurrence: *"Number of arrays of -1..1 integers x(1..n) with every x(i) in a subsequence of length 1 or 2 with sum zero."* (`1,1,3,7,15,33,73,161,355,...`; `Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-3).`).
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, no `native_decide`, and no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`; formalizes Part 1 for all $n \ge 1$ in `A193641_recurrence`, `aOEIS_gf_identity`, and `aOEIS_eq_A193641_sub_three`, plus Part 2's combinatorial SAW refutation $s(5) = 160 \ne 158$ in `sawCount_eq_length_sawPaths`, `sawCount_five`, `refutes_combinatorial_reading_at_five`, and `not_mathar_combinatorial_conjecture`; file: `lean/OpenMath/OeisA100691.lean`, coordinator-verified). Also supported by `written proof, internally audited` (`AUDIT.md`, including the all-$n \ge 5$ transfer-matrix proof) and exact symbolic/enumeration checks (`notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `cd lean && export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && lake build OpenMath.OeisA100691` (and Python transfer-matrix/enumeration check: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A100691/notes/check-check-3.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-26T21:12:06Z`–`21:14:45Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**`, live OEIS `A100691` (rev 11) and `A193641` (rev 48), live OEIS searches for `s(n)`, live arXiv API, GitHub code/repo/issue searches (`1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing`), Zenodo API, Crossref, and web search; 0 prior proofs of `A193641`'s recurrence or reports of `s(5) = 160` found (Gate 2 OpenAlex check clear at `20:18Z`; Gate 3 OpenAlex returned HTTP 429).
- **What is not established**:
  - **Labelle (1996) print-only caveat (`AUDIT.md` §3.3):** J. Labelle, *"Paths in the Cartesian, triangular and hexagonal lattices"*, *Bull. ICA* 17 (1996), 47–61 is print-only and was not directly inspected, so we cannot attribute whether the single `-z^5` vs `+z^5` sign discrepancy originated in Labelle (1996) or in Deutsch's 2004 OEIS submission (though sibling sequences `A038577`, `A100692`, and `A100693` confirm Labelle's rooted-SAW strip definition).
  - Internal auditing and exact symbolic/finite computation are not external peer review.

### `dris-2202.08116-conj5.1` — Proof of Dris Conjecture 5.1 (`AsymptoticDensity2`)

- **Summary**: Jose Arnaldo Bebita Dris conjectured that the set $A$ of positive integers $m$ satisfying $\gcd(m, \sigma(m^2)) = \gcd(m^2, \sigma(m^2))$ has asymptotic density $0$, after proving an upper bound of $7/9$ and citing an unproved heuristic of Aaron Meyerowitz. We proved this conjecture completely by combining a prime-power divisibility criterion for primes $p \equiv 1 \pmod 6$ with a finite CRT truncation separating main residue exclusions from bad sets where fewer than two root-of-unity primes $q \in \mathcal{Q}_p$ exactly divide $m$. Using Dirichlet's theorem on primes in arithmetic progressions and Chebyshev's inequality on finite CRT blocks to show each bad set has density $0$, and taking $k \to \infty$ via $\sum_{p \equiv 1 \pmod 6} 1/p = \infty$, we established $\overline{d}(A) = 0$ and hence $d(A) = 0$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Jose Arnaldo Bebita Dris, *"A new approach to odd perfect numbers via GCDs"*, `arXiv:2202.08116v1` (`main.tex`, Section 5, Conjecture 5.1 `\label{AsymptoticDensity2}`, lines 386–391; also Abstract lines 57–59; published in *Octogon Mathematical Magazine* 30(2), Oct 2022, pp. 883–895).
  - Statement: *"The asymptotic density $\mathscr{A}$ of positive integers $m$ with $\gcd(m,\sigma(m^2))=\gcd(m^2,\sigma(m^2))$ satisfies $\mathscr{A} = 0$."*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, no `native_decide`, and no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`; formalizes `DrisSet`, `densityRatio`, and `dris_conjecture_5_1 : Filter.Tendsto (densityRatio DrisSet) Filter.atTop (nhds 0)` in `lean/OpenMath/Dris220208116Conj51.lean`, audited for statement fidelity in board `#690` and coordinator-verified via `lake build OpenMath.Dris220208116Conj51` and `lake env lean`). Also supported by `written proof, internally audited` (`AUDIT.md` and `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake build OpenMath.Dris220208116Conj51` (and Python check: `.venv/bin/python targets/dris-2202.08116-conj5.1/notes/check-check-2.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-26T21:27:55Z`–`21:29:00Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**`, live `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, GitHub code/repo/issue searches, live arXiv API (`2202.08116v1` unchanged), Zenodo API, and web search (with Gate 2 live Stack Exchange API check of MO `Q369441` / Meyerowitz heuristic Answer `369521` and OpenAlex `W4226357996` `cited_by_count: 0`; Gate 3 OpenAlex returned HTTP 429); 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - Proves qualitative asymptotic density $d(A) = 0$ only, not an explicit quantitative decay rate for $|A \cap [1, X]| / X$ (which decays very slowly, $\approx 92.35\%$ at $X = 10^6$).
  - Does not settle Dris's Section 7 conjecture that $\gcd(\sigma(q^k), \sigma(n^2)) \neq \gcd(n^2, \sigma(n^2))$ for odd perfect numbers $N = q^k n^2$.
  - Internal audit and small-case exact checks are not formal peer review.

### `oeis-A250917` — Proof of Mathar's 2020 D-Finite Recurrence for OEIS `A250917`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in 2020 that the coefficients $a(n)$ of the exponential generating function $F(x) = \exp(x C(x)^3)$, where $C(x) = (1 - \sqrt{1-4x})/(2x)$ is the Catalan generating function, satisfy an explicit order-5 polynomial-coefficient recurrence. We proved that this recurrence holds for all $n \ge 5$ (and also at $n = 3, 4$, while failing at $n = 0, 1, 2$) by translating it into a linear differential operator $L = \sum_{r=0}^5 Q_r(\theta) (d/dx)^{5-r}$ acting on $F(x)$. Working in the differential field $\mathcal{A} = \mathbb{Q}(x)[S]/(S^2 - (1-4x))$ with $S = \sqrt{1-4x}$, we showed symbolically in exact rational arithmetic that $L[F](x)/F(x) = 0$ identically on $|x| < 1/4$, forcing every Taylor coefficient of $L[F]$ to vanish.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A250917` (revision 20, 2025-03-15).
  - Name: *"Expansion of e.g.f. exp( x*C(x)^3 ) where C(x) = (1 - sqrt(1-4*x))/(2*x) is the g.f. of the Catalan numbers, A000108."* (offset `0,3`).
  - Conjecture (Formula section): *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: +2*a(n) +(-11*n+20)*a(n-1) +(n^3+9*n^2-116*n+164)*a(n-2) +(-4*n^4+35*n^3+n^2-317*n+342)*a(n-3) -6*(n-3)*(6*n^3-50*n^2+147*n-176)*a(n-4) +12*(n-5)*(2*n-9)*(n-3)*(n-4)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 25 2020"* (proved for its natural domain $n \ge 5$, as well as $n = 3, 4$; fails at $n = 0, 1, 2$).
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact SymPy rational/polynomial verification of $L[F]/F = 0$ in $\mathcal{A}$ and series check through $n = 25$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A250917/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-26T21:37:27Z`–`21:56:30Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**`, live OEIS `A250917` (rev 20), live `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` & `trureturing`, GitHub code/repo/issue searches, live arXiv API, Zenodo API, and web search (with Gate 2 OpenAlex check confirming 0 hits; Gate 3 OpenAlex/Crossref/S2 returned HTTP 429); 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Symbolic-computation & `joeis`/`gfis/OEIS-mat` near-miss caveats (`AUDIT.md` §4):** The algebraic cancellation $R(x, S) = (0, 0)$ in $\mathbb{Q}(x)[S]/(S^2 - (1-4x))$ relies on exact computer-algebra verification (`notes/check-check-2.py`) rather than a term-by-term hand expansion in `PROOF.md`. `gfis/OEIS-mat` (`holrec/rectopost.man`) and `archmageirvine/joeis` (`A250917.java`) record an unproved Maple `sumrecursion` order-3 recurrence guess on the OEIS binomial sum formula (of which Mathar's order-5 recurrence is a $\mathbb{Q}(n)$-left-multiple) without proof, creative-telescoping certificate, or equivalence proof to $\exp(x C(x)^3)$.
  - Internal audit and SymPy exact symbolic verification are not formal peer review or a formal Lean proof.

### `oeis-A108430` — Proof of Mathar's 2022 D-Finite Recurrence for OEIS `A108430`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in 2022 that OEIS `A108430(n)` (the total number of $d = (1,-1)$ steps in first-quadrant lattice paths from $(0,0)$ to $(3n,0)$ using steps $u = (2,1)$, $U = (1,2)$, and $d = (1,-1)$) satisfies an explicit order-3 polynomial-coefficient recurrence. Using the bivariate path decomposition $G(t,z) = 1 + t z G(t,z)^2(1 + t G(t,z))$ and Lagrange inversion to recover the generating function $A(z) = \left.\frac{\partial G}{\partial t}\right|_{t=1}$, we translated the recurrence into a differential operator acting on $A(z)$ inside the degree-3 algebraic field $\mathbb{Q}(z)[G_1]/(z G_1^3 + z G_1^2 - G_1 + 1)$. Exact symbolic reduction shows $\Phi(z) = -42291 z - 49573 z^2$, which forces every coefficient $[z^n]\Phi(z) = 0$ and thus proves Mathar's recurrence for all $n \ge 3$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A108430` (revision 14, 2025-11-05).
  - Name: *"Number of d steps in all paths from (0,0) to (3n,0) that stay in the first quadrant (but may touch the horizontal axis), consisting of steps u=(2,1), U=(1,2), or d=(1,-1)."* (offset `1,1`).
  - Conjecture (Formula section): *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence n*(2*n+1)*(23982*n-28681)*a(n) +(-640736*n^3+1168048*n^2-901220*n+247035)*a(n-1) +(1196488*n^3-6448608*n^2+10992587*n-5911365)*a(n-2) +2*(2*n-5)*(28283*n-6993)*(n-3)*a(n-3)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 24 2022"* (proved for all $n \ge 3$, with boundary terms $a(0)=a(-1)=a(-2)=0$ and $c_3(3)=0$).
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact combinatorial/Lagrange inversion check and exact differential-algebraic reduction in $\mathbb{Q}(z)[G_1]/(z G_1^3 + z G_1^2 - G_1 + 1)$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A108430/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-26T21:46:56Z`–`22:18:00Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**`, live OEIS `A108430` (rev 14), live `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` & `trureturing`, GitHub code/issue/repo searches, live arXiv API, Zenodo API, Crossref, and web search (Gate 3 OpenAlex/S2 returned HTTP 429); 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Exact symbolic reduction caveat (`AUDIT.md` §3.3):** The reduction of $\Phi(z) = \sum_{r=0}^3 c_r(\theta)[z^r A(z)]$ to $-42291 z - 49573 z^2$ in $\mathbb{Q}(z)[G_1]/(z G_1^3 + z G_1^2 - G_1 + 1)$ relies on exact computer-algebra verification (`notes/check-check-1.py`) rather than an expanded hand derivation of the intermediate 3-component rational expressions in `PROOF.md`.
  - **Deutsch (2000) print JSTOR caveat (`TARGET.md` & Gate 3 receipts):** The print-only JSTOR full text of Emeric Deutsch (*Amer. Math. Monthly* 107, 2000, Problem 10658, pp. 368–370), which established the path enumeration and binomial sum formula prior to Mathar's 2022 recurrence conjecture, was not downloaded.
  - Internal audit and SymPy exact symbolic verification are not formal peer review or a formal Lean proof.

### `linusson-2512.24346-conj1` — Proof of Linusson–Özdemir Conjecture 1 (`conj:complement`)

- **Summary**: Svante Linusson and Alperen Özdemir conjectured that the stationary distribution $\pi$ of the finite $k$-Plancherel Markov chain on $\mathcal{R}_k$ satisfies the complement symmetry $\pi(\lambda) = \pi(\lambda^{\mathrm{comp}})$ for all $\lambda \in \mathcal{R}_k$. We proved this conjecture for all $k \ge 1$ by establishing an explicit product formula $\pi(\lambda) \propto \frac{d_\lambda^{(k)} d_{\lambda^{\mathrm{comp}}}^{(k)}}{|\lambda|! |\lambda^{\mathrm{comp}}|!}$ and proving exact $R$-reversibility under the involution $R(\lambda) = \lambda^{\mathrm{comp}}$. Using a Maya-diagram bijection with $k$-TASEP configurations and Lapointe–Morse's $k$-rectangle theorem, we showed that every weak cover translates into an adjacent swap whose complement reverses the transition with identical $k$-rectangle weights, while constructive 3-phase paths prove irreducibility.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Svante Linusson and Alperen Özdemir, *"The $k$-Plancherel Measure and a Finite Markov Chain"*, `arXiv:2512.24346v1` (`Kplancherel.tex`, Section 3.4, Conjecture 1 `\label{conj:complement}`, lines 641–645).
  - Statement: *"For a $\lambda\in\mathcal{R}_k$, let $\lambda^{\mathrm{comp}}\in\mathcal{R}_k$ be such that $\lambda \cup \lambda^{\mathrm{comp}} = (k-1,(k-2)^2,\ldots,i^{k-i},\ldots,2^{k-2},1^{k-1})$, that is $l_i(\lambda)+l_i(\lambda^{\mathrm{comp}})=k-i$. Conjecture (Symmetry of complements): For all $\lambda\in\mathcal{R}_k$, $\pi(\lambda)=\pi(\lambda^{\mathrm{comp}})$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact integer/`Fraction` verification of Lemmas 1–4, $R$-reversibility, stationarity, and $k=1..5$ tables in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/linusson-2512.24346-conj1/notes/check-check-2.py`

### `oeis-A006255` — Proof of Corneth/Padhi Conjecture on Graham's Sequence (`A006255`)

- **Summary**: On OEIS `A006255`, David A. Corneth (2016) and Jaideep Padhi (2026) conjectured that for Graham's sequence $a(n)$ and $b(n) = \mathrm{A006530}(\mathrm{A007913}(n))$, whenever $b(n)^2 \ge 2n$ one has $a(n) = n + b(n)$ except at $n \in \{2, 3, 10, 171\}$. We proved this conjecture unconditionally for all $n \ge 1$ by reducing $b(n)^2 \ge 2n$ to $n = kp$ with $p$ prime and $1 \le k \le (p-1)/2$, giving a short 4-element proof for $k \le (p-3)/2$, and handling the boundary $n = p(p-1)/2$ via a 78-leaf polynomial odd-cycle covering tree modulo $2520$ plus $10$ explicit finite witnesses. We also proved directly that $a(n) > n + b(n)$ at the four exceptional values $n \in \{2, 3, 10, 171\}$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A006255` (revision 106, 2026-06-19, Comments section), referencing `A007913` (rev. 213) and `A006530` (rev. 248).
  - Statement: *"Conjecture: Let b(n) = A006530(A007913(n)). If b(n)^2 >= 2 * n then a(n) = n + b(n) except for n = 2, 3, 10, and 171. [Corrected by Jaideep Padhi_, Jun 15 2026]"*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact integer/`Fraction` verification in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A006255/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-26T23:05:24Z`–`23:10:00Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**`, live OEIS `A006255` (rev. 106), `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, live arXiv API, Zenodo, Crossref, and web search (OpenAlex/S2 returned HTTP 429); 0 prior proofs found.

### `oeis-A386251` — Counterexample to Hohn's 3-Segment Closed Walk Conjecture (`A386251`)

- **Summary**: Charles L. Hohn conjectured on OEIS `A386251` that for all dimensions $n \ge 6$, the shortest closed walk of noncongruent primitive all-nonzero lattice diagonals in $\mathbb{Z}^n$ is always achieved by a 3-segment walk. We disproved this conjecture for an infinite family of dimensions $n = m^2 - 30$ ($m \ge 92$, starting at $n = 8434$) by constructing an explicit 4-segment non-self-intersecting closed walk of four pairwise noncongruent primitive vectors of length $m$ each, giving total length $4m$. We then proved elementarily that every valid 3-segment closed walk in dimension $n = m^2 - 30$ ($m \ge 92$) has length at least $4m + 2$, so $a(m^2 - 30) = 4m$ is never realized by a 3-segment walk.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A386251` (revision 22, 2025-08-06, Charles L. Hohn).
  - Name & Comment: *"Consider the graph whose vertices are the points of the n-dimensional cubic lattice with points connected by all integer-length diagonals that traverse all n dimensions and do not intersect intermediate points. a(n) is the total length of the shortest possible closed walk in this graph via noncongruent diagonals."* / *"It is provable that all such walks must be even in total length. It is also provable that 3-segment closed walks are impossible for n < 6, and conjectured that a(n) for all n >= 6 are produced by 3-segment walks."*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, no `native_decide`, and no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`; formalizes `ValidDiagonal`, `AreCongruent`, `ClosedWalk`, `witnessWalk4_simple_cycle`, `a386251_eq_four_mul`, `refutes_a386251_conjecture_infinite_family`, `refutes_a386251_at_8434`, and `not_a386251_conjecture` in `lean/OpenMath/OeisA386251.lean`, imported in `lean/OpenMath.lean`, audited for statement fidelity in board `#804` and coordinator-verified via `lake build OpenMath.OeisA386251 && lake env lean OpenMath/OeisA386251.lean`). Also supported by `written proof, internally audited` (`AUDIT.md` and `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake build OpenMath.OeisA386251 && lake env lean OpenMath/OeisA386251.lean` (and Python check: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A386251/notes/check-check-3.py`)

### `yip-2309.10950-rem3.1` — Proof of Yip's $d = 2$ van Lint–MacWilliams Conjecture (`arXiv:2309.10950v2` Theorem 1.7 / Remark 3.3)

- **Summary**: Chi Hoi Yip conjectured in Section 3.2 (Remark 3.3) of `arXiv:2309.10950v2` (*Canad. J. Math.* 78 (2026), 594–618) that Theorem 1.7 extends to $d = 2$ for every odd square $q \ge 49$: any $A \subset \mathbb{F}_q$ satisfying $A \hat{+} A \subset S_2 \cup \{0\}$ has $|A| \le \sqrt{q}$, with equality if and only if $A = \alpha \mathbb{F}_{\sqrt{q}}$ for some $\alpha \in S_2$. We proved this conjecture completely for all odd squares $q = r^2 \ge 49$, settling the sumset case via spectral bounds and Blokhuis (1984) and handling restricted sumsets via a weighted Stepanov polynomial family $H_k(Y)$ forcing non-squares onto a norm circle or affine line. Applying Hasse's theorem to the resulting genus-1 curves closes all $r \ge 11$ unconditionally, and explicit orbit and characteristic-3 witness reductions rule out non-canonical equality sets at $r = 7$ ($q = 49$) and $r = 9$ ($q = 81$).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Chi Hoi Yip, *"Restricted sumsets in multiplicative subgroups"*, `arXiv:2309.10950v2` (`main.tex`, Section 1 Theorem 1.7 `\label{thm:VLM}` lines 105–107 and Section 3.2 Remark 3.3 lines 405–407; published in *Canad. J. Math.* 78(2) (2026), 594–618, DOI `10.4153/S0008414X24000920`).
  - Theorem 1.7 & Remark 3.3: *"Let $d \geq 3$. Let $q \equiv 1 \pmod d$ be an odd prime power and a square. If $A \subset \mathbb{F}_q$ such that $A\hat{+}A \subset S_d \cup \{0\}$, then $|A| \leq \sqrt{q}$, with the equality holds if and only if $d \mid (\sqrt{q}+1)$ and $A=\alpha \mathbb{F}_{\sqrt{q}}$, where $\alpha \in S_d$."* / *"Nevertheless, we conjecture that when $q$ is an odd square and $q \geq 49$, the statement of Theorem~\ref{thm:VLM} also extends to the case $d=2$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact finite-field checks of Lemma 2.2–2.3, norm-circle sums, Hasse bounds, and the $r = 7, 9$ witnesses in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/yip-2309.10950-rem3.1/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-26T23:27:07Z`–`23:32:30Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**` (356 files), `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, live arXiv API (`2309.10950v2` sole version), OpenAlex (`cited_by_count: 0` in Gate 3 snapshot), Zenodo, Crossref, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

### `oeis-A379818` — Proof of Kurkov's Binomial Formula Conjecture on OEIS `A379818`

- **Summary**: Mikhail Kurkov conjectured on OEIS `A379818` that a recursive sequence defined via 2-adic valuations $f(n) = v_2(n)$ and `A025480` satisfies $a(2^m(2k+1)) = \sum_{j=0}^m (\binom{m+2}{j+1} - \binom{m}{j}) a(2^j k)$ for all $m, k \ge 0$. We proved this conjecture unconditionally by first proving the auxiliary shift identity $\mathrm{A025480}(2^r(2k+1) - 1) = k$ for all $r, k \ge 0$ by induction on $r$. We then proved the main identity by induction on $m$ uniformly over all $k \ge 0$, combining the four terms of the even recurrence at $n = 2^{m-1}(2k+1)$ via Pascal's identity.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A379818` (revision 8, 2026-02-26, Mikhail Kurkov), referencing `A025480` (rev. 235) and `A007814`.
  - Name & Formula: *"a(2n+1) = a(n) for n >= 0, a(2n) = a(n) + a(n - 2^f(n)) + a(2n - 2^f(n)) + a(A025480(n-1)) for n > 0 with a(0) = 1 where f(n) = A007814(n)."* / *"Conjecture: a(2^m*(2k+1)) = Sum_{j=0..m} (binomial(m+2, j+1) - binomial(m, j))*a(2^j*k) for m >= 0, k >= 0 with a(0) = 1."*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, no `native_decide`, and no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`; formalizes `a007814_eq_factorization`, `a025480`, `a379818`, `kurkov_conjecture_A379818`, `kurkov_conjecture_A379818_int`, and the axiomatic `kurkov_conjecture_A379818_of_recurrence` in `lean/OpenMath/OeisA379818.lean`, audited for statement fidelity in `targets/oeis-A379818/AUDIT.md` §5 and coordinator-verified). Also supported by `written proof, internally audited` (`AUDIT.md` and `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake build OpenMath.OeisA379818 && lake env lean OpenMath/OeisA379818.lean` (and Python check: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A379818/notes/check-check-1.py`)

### `oeis-A061313` — Proof of Zabolotskii's Hirzebruch Continued Fraction Conjecture on OEIS `A061313`

- **Summary**: Andrei Zabolotskii conjectured in 2020 on OEIS `A061313` that $a(n) + 2$, where $a(n)$ is the minimal number of $\{-1, \times 2\}$ steps from $1$ to $n$, equals the sum of the terms of the Hirzebruch (negative) continued fraction of the Stern–Brocot tree fraction $\mathrm{A007305}(n)/\mathrm{A007306}(n)$. We proved this conjecture for all $n \ge 2$ and characterized $n = 1$ as the sole exception (where $1/1 = [[1]]$ has sum $1 \neq 2 = a(1) + 2$) by encoding the Stern–Brocot ancestor intervals in $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z})$ and conjugating the left/right tree matrices to Hirzebruch matrices $H(c) = \begin{pmatrix} c & -1 \\ 1 & 0 \end{pmatrix}$. Structural induction on $n \ge 2$ shows that the Hirzebruch term sum satisfies the exact recurrence $S(2m-1) = S(m) + 2$, $S(2m) = S(m) + 1$ with $S(2) = 3$, matching $a(n) + 2$ identically (and simultaneously settling companion `A008687` for $n \ge 2$).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A061313` (revision 58, 2026-11-01, Henry Bottomley / Andrei Zabolotskii), referencing `A007305` (rev. 117) and `A007306` (rev. 342).
  - Name, Formula & Comment: *"Minimal number of steps to get from 1 to n by (a) subtracting 1 or (b) multiplying by 2."* (`a(2n) = a(n)+1; a(2n+1) = a(n+1)+2; a(1) = 0.`) / *"Conjecture: a(n)+2 is the sum of the terms of the Hirzebruch (negative) continued fraction for the Stern-Brocot tree fraction A007305(n)/A007306(n). - _Andrei Zabolotskii_, Apr 17 2020"* (proved for all $n \ge 2$, with $n = 1$ explicitly established as the sole boundary exception).
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, no `native_decide`, and no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`; formalizes `a061313_is_min_steps`, `sternBrocotFrac`, `IsCanonicalHirzebruch`, `zabolotskii_conjecture_A061313`, `zabolotskii_sum_eq`, and sole exception `zabolotskii_boundary_exception_at_one` in `lean/OpenMath/OeisA061313.lean`, audited for statement fidelity in `targets/oeis-A061313/AUDIT.md` §5 and coordinator-verified). Also supported by `written proof, internally audited` (`AUDIT.md` and `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake build OpenMath.OeisA061313 && lake env lean OpenMath/OeisA061313.lean` (and Python check: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A061313/notes/check-check-1.py`)

### `chang-2201.02781-conj2` — Proof of Chang–Yang–Yin–Yao Conjecture 2 (`arXiv:2201.02781v1`)

- **Summary**: Jing-zhi Chang, Chao Yang, Zhi-xiang Yin, and Bing Yao conjectured in `arXiv:2201.02781v1` that every connected graph $G \neq K_2$ admits a non-proper $3$-total coloring whose neighbor full sums $\phi(x) = f(x) + \sum_{e \ni x} f(e) + \sum_{y \in N(x)} f(y)$ distinguish every pair of adjacent vertices ($\mathrm{fgndi}_{\sum}(G) \le 3$). Fixing $f(v) = 2$ on all vertices reduces this to an even-offset $1$-$2$-$3$ edge weighting theorem for all connected graphs with $|V| \ge 3$ (while $K_1$ holds vacuously with $\mathrm{fgndi}_{\sum}(K_1) = 1$). We proved the even-offset theorem completely by induction on $|V|$ via maximum bipartite cut-flow balancing, parity-tree reweighting, conflict elimination on an independent set $R$, and an exhaustive analysis of leaf-pair reductions.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Jing-zhi Chang, Chao Yang, Zhi-xiang Yin, Bing Yao, *"An Extension on Neighbor Sum Distinguishing Total Coloring of Graphs"*, `arXiv:2201.02781v1` (`An_extension_on_nsdt-_coloring_of_graphs-arXiv.tex`, Section 1, Definition 1 lines 146–151 and Conjecture 2 line 162; standing convention line 125 restricts to finite, undirected, simple, connected graphs).
  - Statement: *"For every connected graph $G$ and $G$ is not $K_2$, $\mathrm{fgndi}_{\sum}(G)\leq 3$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exhaustive exact verification on $K_1, K_2$, all $4 + 38 + 728 + 26{,}704$ labeled connected graphs on $n = 3..6$, and $283$ larger structured graphs $n = 7..16$ triggering all 18 proof branches and 6 Lemma 6/7 sub-branches in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/chang-2201.02781-conj2/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T01:38:15Z`–`02:02:00Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**` (369 files), live `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, live arXiv API (`2201.02781v1` sole version), OpenAlex (`citing_count: 0`), Zenodo (`total: 0`), and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

### `li-2505.12035-conj7` — Counterexample to Li–Lu–Luo Conjecture 7 (`conj:ore`)

- **Summary**: Li, Lu, and Luo conjectured in `arXiv:2505.12035v1` that every $n$-vertex $[3]$-graph with $n \ge 6$ in which every non-adjacent pair satisfies $d_{\partial\mathcal{H}}(u) + d_{\partial\mathcal{H}}(v) \ge n + 1$ contains a Hamiltonian Berge cycle. We refuted this conjecture with two explicit connected $3$-uniform hypergraphs: a $6$-vertex, $5$-edge hypergraph $\mathcal{H}_1$ whose $2$-shadow is $K_{2,2,2}$ (every non-adjacent pair has degree sum $4 + 4 = 8 = n + 2 > n + 1$), and a $7$-vertex, $6$-edge linear hypergraph $\mathcal{H}_2$ obtained from the Fano plane $\mathrm{STS}(7)$ by deleting one line (every non-adjacent pair has degree sum $4 + 4 = 8 = n + 1$). In both witnesses $|E(\mathcal{H}_i)| < n$, making a Hamiltonian Berge cycle (which requires $n$ distinct hyperedges) impossible.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Yupei Li, Linyuan Lu, Ruth Luo, *"An Ore-type theorem for $[3]$-graphs"*, `arXiv:2505.12035v1` (`main_arxiv.tex`, Section 1, lines 94–113 and 152–156, Conjecture 7 `\label{conj:ore}`).
  - Statement: *"Let $n\geq 6$, and let $\mathcal{H}$ be a $[3]$-graph on $n$ vertices such that every pair $u,v \in V(\mathcal{H})$ of non-adjacent vertices satisfies $d_{\partial \mathcal{H}}(u) + d_{\partial \mathcal{H}}(v) \geq n+1$. Then $\mathcal{H}$ contains a Hamiltonian Berge cycle."*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, no `native_decide`, and no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`; formalizes `Hypergraph3`, `IsUniform3`, `IsLinear`, `shadowAdj`, `shadowDeg`, `SatisfiesOre`, `ShadowDiamLeTwo`, `HasHamiltonianBergeCycle`, `witness1_no_hamiltonian_berge_cycle`, `witness2_no_hamiltonian_berge_cycle`, `not_li_lu_luo_conjecture_7`, and `not_li_lu_luo_conjecture_7_linear_uniform` in `lean/OpenMath/Li250512035Conj7.lean`, imported in `lean/OpenMath.lean`, audited for statement fidelity in board `#828` and coordinator-verified via `lake build OpenMath.Li250512035Conj7 && lake env lean OpenMath/Li250512035Conj7.lean`). Also supported by `counterexample, verified by exact computation` (`notes/check-check-3.py` and `AUDIT.md`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake build OpenMath.Li250512035Conj7 && lake env lean OpenMath/Li250512035Conj7.lean` (and Python check: `.venv/bin/python targets/li-2505.12035-conj7/notes/check-check-3.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T01:26:04Z`–`02:34:54Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**` (up to 413 files), live `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, live arXiv API (`2505.12035v1` sole version), Crossref, and Zenodo (`total: 0`); 0 prior refutations or resolutions found (OpenAlex/Semantic Scholar hit unauthenticated HTTP 429 during the `watch-5` re-check).

### `campbell-2509.16034-conj1` — Solution to Campbell–Currie–Rampersad Section 3 Open Problems `(c1)` and `(c2)`

- **Summary**: In `arXiv:2509.16034v1` (rescoped strictly per `notes/contest-ruling-audit-3.md` to Open Problems `(c1)` and `(c2)`), Campbell, Currie, and Rampersad left open how to evaluate the nonzero sign of $\rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(4n+2) - \rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(4n)$ and how to obtain a recursion for $\rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(4n)$ (and a full recursion for $\rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(n)$). Using their exact run-length interval decomposition, we derived the closed form $\rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(n) = \frac{3}{2}(M_n - m_n + 1) + \frac{(-1)^{m_n} + (-1)^{M_n}}{4}$, proved explicit recurrences $\rho(4n) = \rho(n+1) + 1 + a_{n+1}$ and $\rho(4n+2) = \rho(n+1) + 1 + b_{n+1}$ (where $a_n = m_n \bmod 2$, $b_n = M_n \bmod 2$) alongside an explicit $8$-state LSD $2$-automatic DFAO for $(a_{k+1}, b_{k+1})$, deduced $2$-regularity of $\rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(n)$, and showed that in the nonzero case $\mathrm{sgn}(\rho(4n+2) - \rho(4n)) = 2b_{n+1} - 1 \in \{-1, +1\}$ is given explicitly by the same $8$-state automaton.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: John M. Campbell, James Currie, Narad Rampersad, *"Reduced complexities for sequences over finite alphabets"*, `arXiv:2509.16034v1` (`ReducedComplexityV85.tex`, Section 1 lines 75–113 and Section 3 lines 675–699; published in *INTEGERS* 26 (2026), `#A34`), rescoped per `notes/contest-ruling-audit-3.md`.
  - Statements (`(c1)` and `(c2)`): explicit evaluation of $\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(4n+2) - \rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(4n))$ when nonzero (lines 691–694), and explicit recursion for $\rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(4n)$ and full recursion for $\rho^{\mathrm{ab},\mathrm{red}}_{\mathbf{t}}(n)$ (lines 680–682, 695–696).
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact enumeration for $1 \le n \le 120$, recurrence/closed-form checks for $1 \le n \le 20{,}000$, exact $8$-state $2$-kernel closure, and sign-automaton checks for $1 \le n \le 4999$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/campbell-2509.16034-conj1/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T02:00:53Z`–`03:09:00Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**` (up to 413 files), live `the-omega-institute/trureturing*` (PR `#6106` / commits `832f82f782`, `96baee8682` settle odd recurrence, eq. ### `oeis-A360949` — Proof of Hanna's 2023 Mod-4 Thue–Morse Conjecture for OEIS `A360949`

- **Summary**: Paul D. Hanna conjectured in 2023 on OEIS `A360949` that the coefficients of the formal power series $A(x)$ satisfying $1 = \sum_{n \ge 0} (-x/2)^n (A(x)^n + (-1)^n)^n$ obey $a(0) = 1$, $a(2\,\mathrm{A161674}(k) + 1) \equiv 2 \pmod 4$ for $k \ge 1$, and $a(n) \equiv 0 \pmod 4$ otherwise. Writing $A(x) = 1 + 2 B(x)$, we proved by strong induction that $B(x) \in x\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$, reduced the defining functional equation modulo $2$ in $\mathbb{F}_2[[x]]$ to $(1+x^2) B(x) = x + x^3 B(x)^2$ (forcing $B(x) = x C(x^2)$ and hence $a(2k) \equiv 0 \pmod 4$), and showed that $C(y)$ and $\widetilde{C}(y) = \sum_{m \ge 0} (t_m + t_{m+2}) y^m \in \mathbb{F}_2[[y]]$ (where $t_m = \mathrm{A010060}(m)$ is the Thue–Morse sequence) satisfy the same uniquely-determining quadratic equation $y^2 C(y)^2 + (1+y) C(y) + 1 = 0$ over $\mathbb{F}_2[[y]]$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A360949` (author: Paul D. Hanna, rev. 15, `2023-03-05`) and cross-referenced `A161674` (rev. 21, `2021-01-30`), `A010060` (rev. 619).
  - Name: *"G.f. A(x) satisfies: 1 = Sum_{n>=0} (-x/2)^n * (A(x)^n + (-1)^n)^n."* (offset `0,2`).
  - Conjecture (Comment): *"Conjecture: a(0) = 1, a(2*A161674(k) + 1) == 2 (mod 4) for k >= 1, otherwise a(n) == 0 (mod 4). A161674 lists positions n such that A010060(n) + A010060(n+2) = 1, where A010060 is the Thue-Morse sequence."*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (Lean 4 formalization `lean/OpenMath/OeisA360949.lean` — theorems `existsUnique_a360949`, `coeff_ASeries`, `hanna_conjecture_A360949`, `hanna_conjecture_A360949_A161674` — verified with zero `sorry`/`native_decide` and standard axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`, `AUDIT.md` §6 statement-fidelity & hygiene PASS; also supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` §§1–5 PASS, and `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake build OpenMath.OeisA360949 && lake env lean OpenMath/OeisA360949.lean` (plus Python check `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A360949/notes/check-check-3.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T02:00Z`–`03:09Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**` (up to 413 files), live OEIS `A360949` rev. 15 (still marked `Conjecture:`), `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, arXiv, Zenodo, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

### `cooper-2302.00757-conj11.2` — Proof of Cooper's Conjecture 11.2 (Mod-9 and Mod-25 Supercongruences for $T_{11}(n)$ / OEIS `A284756`)

- **Summary**: Shaun Cooper conjectured in `arXiv:2302.00757v2` (Section 11.1, Conjecture 11.2) that the level-11 Apéry-like sequence $T_{11}(n) = \mathrm{A284756}(n)$ satisfies $T_{11}(3n) \equiv T_{11}(n) + 3n \pmod{3^2}$ and $T_{11}(5n) \equiv T_{11}(n) \pmod{5^2} \iff 5 \mid n$. Using Cooper's modular parametrization $U_1(q) = \sum_{n \ge 0} T_{11}(n) X_1(q)^n$ on $\Gamma_0(11)$ and the $V_p$-shifted forms on $\Gamma_0(11p)$, we established the mod-3 and mod-5 Lucas congruences and homogenized the supercongruence errors into explicit weight-30 modular forms $G_3 \in M_{30}(\Gamma_0(33)) \cap \mathbb{Z}[[q]]$ and weight-66 modular forms $G_5 \in M_{66}(\Gamma_0(55)) \cap \mathbb{Z}[[q]]$. Verifying $q$-expansion vanishing modulo $9$ through $q^{130} > B(30,33) = 120$ and modulo $25$ through $q^{405} > B(66,55) = 396$ via Sturm's bound (proved valid for $p \mid N$ and prime powers $p^2$) yields both congruences and exact non-vanishing mod $25$ when $5 \nmid n$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Shaun Cooper, *"Apéry-like sequences defined by four-term recurrence relations"*, `arXiv:2302.00757v2` (`main.tex`, Section 5 lines 1611–1632 and Section 11.1 lines 2557–2565; published in *Contemporary Mathematics* **818**, AMS 2025, pp. 137–179, DOI `10.1090/conm/818/16373`).
  - Statement (Conjecture 11.2): *"$T_{11}(3n) \equiv T_{11}(n) + 3n \pmod {3^2}$ and $T_{11}(5n) \equiv T_{11}(n) \pmod {5^2}$ if and only if $5 \mid n$."* (for all $n \ge 1$, and trivially at $n = 0$).
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact recurrence, $q$-series, and Sturm-bound verification through $q^{130}$ on $\Gamma_0(33)$ and $q^{405}$ on $\Gamma_0(55)$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`, plus independent SymPy weight/quotient checks in `AUDIT.md`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/cooper-2302.00757-conj11.2/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T02:00Z`–`03:09Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `watch/snapshots/**` (up to 413 files), live arXiv `2302.00757v2` (and OpenAlex citing check confirming `2604.14219v2` is unrelated level-8), live OEIS `A284756`, `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, Zenodo, and Crossref; 0 prior proofs found.

Depends on Cooper's Theorem 3.1 + Tables 3–5 parametrization, Sturm's theorem on $\Gamma_0(33)$ / $\Gamma_0(55)$, and exact $q$-series computations up to $q^{130} / q^{80} / q^{405}$. ### `oeis-A396911` — Proof of Irvine's 2026 Vogel Phyllotaxis Walk Bound (`a(n) <= (3/2) * n^2`)

- **Summary**: Sean A. Irvine conjectured in June 2026 on OEIS `A396911` that the greedy nearest-unvisited walk index $a(n)$ on Vogel's golden-angle phyllotaxis spiral $p_k = (\sqrt{k}\cos(k\theta), \sqrt{k}\sin(k\theta))$ ($\theta = 2\pi\phi^{-2}$) satisfies $a(n) \le \frac{3}{2}n^2$. Combining exact Fibonacci Diophantine gap bounds ($\|F_m\phi^{-2}\|_{\mathbb{Z}} = \phi^{-m}$ and minimal spacing outside $\{F_{m-1}, F_m\}$) with monotonicity and plateau-dominance estimates for $D^2(k, k + F_m)$, we proved an exact inductive plateau structure for all $m \ge 17$ in which step differences lie exclusively in $\{+F_m, +F_{m+1}\}$ with transition thresholds $k^*_m$ satisfying $k^*_{m+1}/k^*_m \to \phi^2$. Summing the resulting Fibonacci geometric series yields $a(n)/n^2 < 1.43 < 3/2$ for all $n \ge 460$ (and $\limsup_{n \to \infty} a(n)/n^2 = \pi/\sqrt{5} \approx 1.40496$), while a certified 60-digit interval-arithmetic simulation covers $0 \le n \le 460$ (and confirms strict monotonicity and Fibonacci first differences unconditionally).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A396911` (created by Witold Tatkiewicz, rev. 48, `2026-06-22`) and `A131988` (rev. 20).
  - Name & Comments: *"Shortest path indices during walk over golden ratio phyllotaxis starting at the center."* Walk on $p_k = (\sqrt{k}\cos(k\theta), \sqrt{k}\sin(k\theta))$ ($\theta = \text{A131988}$, $k \ge 0$) starting at $p_0 = (0,0)$ and moving at each step to a closest unvisited point.
  - Conjecture (Formula): *"Conjecture: a(n) <= (3/2) * n^2. - _Sean A. Irvine_, Jun 21 2026"*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (analytic & Diophantine proof for $m \ge 17$, combined with certified 60-digit `mpmath.iv` interval-arithmetic verification of the finite base case $0 \le n \le 500$ with strict nearest-neighbor separation $> 2.5 \times 10^{-3}$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A396911/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T02:44:00Z`–`03:18:32Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all discovered repo snapshots (595 repos), live OEIS `A396911` rev. 48 (still conjectured), `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean` (`IrvineConjecture.lean` verified unrelated), `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, `CrocSwap/integer-mult-bounds`, arXiv, and Zenodo; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Interval-arithmetic base-case & lemma-constant caveat (`AUDIT.md` §3.2–3.3):** The finite base case $0 \le n \le 460$ (verified through $500$ with `min_gap = 0.002514 > 2.5e-3`) and intermediate numerical constants in Lemmas 2–4 rely on 50- to 60-digit `mpmath.iv` interval arithmetic rather than hand-expanded rational bounds or a Lean formalization (with potential ties at $k = k^*_m$ for $m \ge 17$ handled unconditionally under either choice).

### `daugherty-2610.29291-conj6.1` — Counterexample to Daugherty et al. Conjecture 6.1 (`conj:box`)

- **Summary**: Daugherty, Fahnestock, Huxtable, Larson, Al Munayes, and Peacock conjectured in `arXiv:2610.29291v1` (Section 6, Conjecture 6.1) that adding a single box to any non-bottom row $i \in [\ell(\lambda)-1]$ of a wide partition $\mu$ to obtain a wide partition $\lambda$ preserves or increases the number of Latin tableaux, $LT(\lambda) \ge LT(\mu)$. By exact enumeration from Definition 2.5 (cross-checked via Lemma 5.4 integer Ryser permanents in `AUDIT.md`), we refuted Conjecture 6.1 at $n = 22 \to 23$, $i = 5$ on the authors' own motivating pair $\mu = (6,5,5,3,2,1)$ and $\lambda = (6,5,5,3,3,1)$: both partitions are wide across all 63 nonempty subpartitions, yet $LT(\lambda) = 32 < 56 = LT(\mu)$. Two further wide counterexample pairs occur at $(6,6,4,3,2,1) \to (6,6,4,3,3,1)$ ($88 \to 64$) and $(6,6,5,3,2,1) \to (6,6,5,3,3,1)$ ($2080 \to 1984$).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Spencer Daugherty, Michaela Fahnestock, Shawn Huxtable, Michael Larson, Nourah Al Munayes, Storey Peacock, *"Counting and constructing Latin tableaux"*, `arXiv:2610.29291v1` (`final.tex`, Section 2 Definitions 2.2/2.5 & Proposition 2.4 lines 263–308, Section 6 Conjecture 6.1 `\label{conj:box}` lines 717–719).
  - Statement: *"If $\lambda$ and $\mu$ are wide partitions where $\lambda_i = \mu_i + 1$ for some $i \in [\ell(\lambda)-1]$ and $\lambda_j = \mu_j$ when $j \neq i$, then $LT(\lambda) \geq LT(\mu)$."*
- **Label**: `counterexample, verified by exact computation` (direct Definition 2.5 tableau enumeration and wide-subpartition verification across all 63 nonempty subpartitions for all three pairs in `notes/check-check-3.py`, cross-checked against Ryser permanents in `AUDIT.md`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/daugherty-2610.29291-conj6.1/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T03:16Z`–`03:55Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all 595 repo snapshots, live `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*` (`#20269` targets Conj 6.3 only, `#26377` Conj 6.2 only), `rainwangphy/aftd-platform` / `valyuAI/research` (Conj 6.2/6.3 and $n=6\to 7$ positive case only), `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, `CrocSwap/integer-mult-bounds`, `small-true-things`, live arXiv (`2610.29291v1` sole version), OpenAlex `W7223232483` (`cited_by_count: 0`), Crossref, and Zenodo (`total: 0`); 0 prior refutations of Conjecture 6.1 found.
- **What is not established**:
  - Does not refute monotonicity for $i = 1$ (proved in Proposition 5.5 of `arXiv:2610.29291v1`) or characterize which sub-families of wide partitions and non-bottom rows satisfy box-addition monotonicity beyond $n \le 24$.

### `gaetz-2512.08168-conj1` — Proof of Gaetz–Gao Conjecture 1.3 (`conj:union-intersection`)

- **Summary**: Christian Gaetz and Yibo Gao conjectured in `arXiv:2512.08168v2` that for any Coxeter group $(W, S)$ and any $w \in W$, the set $\mathrm{BP}(w) = \{J \subseteq S \mid \mathrm{Supp}(w^J) \cap J \subseteq D_L(w_J)\}$ of Billey–Postnikov parabolic decompositions of $w$ is closed under intersection and union, having proved this only for finite Weyl groups and Coxeter groups of rank at most $3$. Using the $0$-Hecke/Demazure monoid action on subwords to identify $[e, w] \cap W_A = [e, \mathrm{Dem}(\mathbf{w}|_A)]$ for arbitrary (even infinite) $A \subseteq S$ and proving a comparison lemma inside $W_{J \cup K}$ showing $u \le v$ whenever $J, K \in \mathrm{BP}(u)$ and $u_J \le v_J, u_K \le v_K$, we proved $J \cap K, J \cup K \in \mathrm{BP}(w)$ for all Coxeter groups $(W, S)$ and all $w \in W$, establishing that $(\mathrm{BP}(w), \subseteq)$ is a distributive sublattice of $2^S$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Christian Gaetz, Yibo Gao, *"Billey--Postnikov posets, rationally smooth Schubert varieties, and Poincar\'e duality"*, `arXiv:2512.08168v2` (`main.tex`, Section 1.1 Conjecture 1.3 `\label{conj:union-intersection}` lines 113–116, Section 2.1 Definition 2.1 `\label{def:BP}` lines 192–205).
  - Statement: *"Let $W$ be any Coxeter group and let $w \in W$. If $J, K \in \mathrm{BP}(w)$, then $J \cap K, J \cup K \in \mathrm{BP}(w)$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact Tits-representation verification of Lemmas 1–5 and Theorem 1 across $A_3, B_3, H_3, \widetilde{C}_2 (\ell \le 7)$, $\mathrm{RACG}_{C_4} (\ell \le 6)$, and rank-4 hyperbolic $(\ell \le 6)$ in `notes/check-check-3.py`, plus independent non-reduced-word and rank-4/5 stress tests in `AUDIT.md` §3).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/gaetz-2512.08168-conj1/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T03:34Z`–`04:10Z`, refreshed `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, plus prior `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `470` repo snapshots, live arXiv (`2512.08168v2` sole latest version; Gaetz–Gao `2610.22857v1` and `2611.33572v1` unrelated), OpenAlex (`citing_count: 0`), `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*` (`SCREENED-OUT-R52.md` refers only to Conj 1.9), `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, `CrocSwap/integer-mult-bounds`, Crossref, Zenodo, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Minor citation nit & scope (`AUDIT.md` §1.3, §4):** `TARGET.md` line 29 parenthetically cites `"Theorem 3.7 / Section 3.3"` for the rank-$\le 3$ case whereas `main.tex` places it at **Theorem 3.15** in **Section 3.5** (`PROOF.md` does not repeat this slip). Does not address type-$E$ Lehmer codes or claim finite-poset Birkhoff representation when $|S|$ is infinite.
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite-ball exact computations are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `oeis-A208425` — Proof of Zhi-Wei Sun's Supercongruence Conjecture (ii) for $p \equiv 1 \pmod 3$ (`conjecture_2`)

- **Summary**: On OEIS `A208425` (and `FormalConjectures/OEIS/208425.lean`), Zhi-Wei Sun conjectured in 2016 that $a(n) = [x^n]\sum_{k \ge 0}\frac{(3k)!}{k!^3}\frac{x^{2k}}{(1-x)^{3k+1}} = \sum_{k=0}^n \binom{n}{k}\binom{n-k}{k}\binom{n+k}{k}$ satisfies $\sum_{k=0}^{p-1} a(k) \equiv \binom{2(p-1)/3}{(p-1)/3} \pmod{p^2}$ for all primes $p \equiv 1 \pmod 3$ (`conjecture_2`). Writing $p = 6n + 1$ ($n \ge 1$), hockey-stick summation isolates the unique $p$-singular term at $k = 2n$ to give $\binom{4n}{2n} \pmod{p^2}$ plus $p\sum_{k \neq 2n}\binom{2k}{k}/(3k+1) \pmod{p^2}$. We proved that the residual sum modulo $p$ vanishes via Adamchuk's congruence $\sum_{k=0}^{4n}\binom{2k}{k} \equiv 1 \pmod{p^2}$ (derived self-containedly from Laurent coefficient extraction and a $3$-orbit cancellation $\sigma(x) = 1/(1-x)$ over $\mathbb{F}_p \setminus \{0, 1, \zeta, \zeta^{-1}\}$) together with a polynomial differential telescoping identity on $\mathbb{Q}[X]$ evaluated at a root $\zeta$ of $\Phi(X) = X^2 - X + 1$ in $\mathbb{F}_p$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A208425` (revision 60, `2025-11-05`, comment by Zhi-Wei Sun, Nov 12, 2016) and `google-deepmind/formal-conjectures` `FormalConjectures/OEIS/208425.lean` (`conjecture_2`).
  - Statement (`conjecture_2`): *"For any prime $p \equiv 1 \pmod 3$, $\sum_{k=0}^{p-1} a(k) \equiv \binom{2(p-1)/3}{(p-1)/3} \pmod{p^2}$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact verification of OEIS definitions, Lemmas 1–3 on 45 primes $p < 500$, Lemmas 4–5 on $p \in \{7, 13, 19, 31, 37, 43\}$, and `conjecture_2` across all 80 primes $p \equiv 1 \pmod 3$, $p < 1000$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A208425/notes/check-check-2.py`

### `ragunathan-2603.29978-conj6.1` — Proof of Ragunathan–Van Tuyl Conjecture 6.1 (WLP of van der Waerden Simplicial Complexes)

- **Summary**: Ragunathan and Van Tuyl conjectured in `arXiv:2603.29978v2` (Section 6, Conjecture 6.1) that over a field $K$ of characteristic $0$, for fixed $k \ge 3$, the Artinian algebra $A(\mathrm{vdw}(n, k))$ of the van der Waerden simplicial complex fails to have the Weak Lefschetz Property (WLP) for all sufficiently large $n \ge m$ when $k$ is odd, and has the WLP for all $n > k$ when $k$ is even. Reducing to the incidence matrices $M_r$ of $\ell_1 = \sum x_j$ in characteristic $0$, we proved Part (2) completely for all even $k = 2m \ge 2$ and all $n > k$ via Boolean-algebra $DU - UD = (N - 2s)I$ injectivity on facets ($r \le m$) and extremal jump-isolation into a single arithmetic progression ($m+1 \le r \le 2m$), and proved Part (1) completely for all odd $k \ge 3$ with explicit thresholds $m = 2k + 2$ for odd $k = 2m - 1 \ge 5$ (exhibiting a 4-facet nonzero cokernel witness in $\ker(M_m^T)$ and proving $|F_m| > |F_{m+1}|$ via Catalan-weighted jump-sum differences) and $n \ge 40$ self-contained ($n \ge 7$ via Theorem 4.2 of `2603.29978v2`) for $k = 3$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Naveena Ragunathan, Adam Van Tuyl, *"The van der Waerden Simplicial Complex and its Lefschetz Properties"*, `arXiv:2603.29978v2` (`van_der_Waerden_complex_and_the_Lefschetz_Properties_final.tex`, Section 6 Conjecture 6.1 `\label{conjecture}` lines 1384–1393, Theorem 4.2 lines 916–920).
  - Statement: *"Suppose $\mathrm{char}(K) = 0$. Fix an integer $k \ge 3$. (1) If $k$ is odd, then there exists an $m$ such that $A(\mathrm{vdw}(n,k))$ fails to have the WLP for $n \ge m$. (2) If $k$ is even, then $A(\mathrm{vdw}(n,k))$ has the WLP for all $n > k$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact rational/integer verification of $DU - UD = (N-2s)I$, even-case WLP for $k \in \{2,4,6\}, n \le 9$, $k=3$ WLP characterization for $n \le 25$ and $n \ge 40$, and odd $k \ge 5$ Proposition 7–8 identities in `notes/check-check-3.py`, plus independent brute-force face-enumeration checks in `AUDIT.md` §3).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/ragunathan-2603.29978-conj6.1/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T03:34Z`–`04:10Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `470` repo snapshots, live arXiv (`2603.29978v2` sole latest version), OpenAlex (`W7147123883` `cited_by_count: 0`; Ragunathan thesis `W7214395421` cited solely by `2603.29978`), `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, `chy4pro/automath` (raw triage listing only), Zenodo, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Minimal failing $n$, $\mathrm{char}(K) > 0$, & minor Lemma 6 wording nit (`AUDIT.md` §2, §4):** Establishes WLP failure for all $n \ge 2k+2$ when $k \ge 5$ is odd (and self-contained $n \ge 40$ / Theorem 4.2 $n \ge 7$ for $k = 3$), not the exact minimal failing $n$ suggested in the paper's Remark (lines 1406–1409) for $k \equiv 3 \pmod 4$ (e.g. $2k+1$) nor WLP behavior on $k < n < 2k+1$. Assumes $\mathrm{char}(K) = 0$ as stated in Conjecture 6.1 (does not treat $\mathrm{char}(K) > 0$ or SLP). Lemma 6 states $\gcd = 1$ as a hypothesis though its proof never uses it (making the Proposition 8 invocation on $(0,2,\dots,2m-2)$ valid).

### `petyuk-2210.14762-conj1` — Counterexample to Petyuk Conjecture 1 (`S(4, 3)` is Word-Representable)

- **Summary**: Anthony V. Petyuk conjectured in `arXiv:2210.14762v2` (Section 4, Conjecture 1) that the simplified de Bruijn graph $S(n, k)$ is non-word-representable for all $n \ge 4$ and $k \ge 3$. We refuted Conjecture 1 at $(n, k) = (4, 3)$ by constructing an explicit balanced, $\mathbb{Z}_3$-shift-equivariant proper $3$-coloring $c : \{0,1,2\}^4 \to \mathbb{Z}_3$ ($27$ vertices per color class, hence word-representable by Halldórsson–Kitaev–Pyatkin 2016) and explicitly writing down a length-$4536$ representing word $w = \prod_{t=1}^{28}(P_t^{(01)} P_t^{(20)} P_t^{(12)})$ over the $81$ vertices of $S(4, 3)$ ($237$ edges).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Anthony V. Petyuk, *"On word-representability of simplified de Bruijn graphs"*, `arXiv:2210.14762v2` (`simplified_de_Bruijn_graphs__4_.tex`, Section 1.2 lines 65–67, Section 4 Conjecture 1 line 123).
  - Statement: *"$S(n,k)$ is non-word-rerpesentable for $n\geq 4$ and $k\geq 3$."* (typo in original for "non-word-representable").
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (Lean 4 formalization `lean/OpenMath/Petyuk221014762Conj1.lean` — theorems `threeColorable_isWordRepresentable`, `s43_coloring_proper`, `s43_has_triangle`, `s43_wordRepresentable`, `not_petyuk_conjecture_1` — verified with zero `sorry`/`native_decide` and standard axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`, `AUDIT.md` §6 statement-fidelity & hygiene PASS; also supported by explicit length-$4536$ representing word verified across all $3240$ vertex pairs in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake build OpenMath.Petyuk221014762Conj1 && lake env lean OpenMath/Petyuk221014762Conj1.lean` (plus Python check `.venv/bin/python targets/petyuk-2210.14762-conj1/notes/check-check-1.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T03:34Z`–`04:10Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `470` repo snapshots, live arXiv (`2210.14762v2` sole latest version; Huang–Kitaev–Pyatkin `2312.10377v1` / *DAM* 346 (2024) and Roy–Hariharasubramanian `2605.02268v2` leave Petyuk Conj 1 open), OpenAlex citing cache, `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, `CrocSwap/integer-mult-bounds`, Crossref, Zenodo, and web search; 0 prior word-representability proofs or refutations of Petyuk Conj 1 found.

  - **Prior-art disclosure (`AUDIT.md` §4):** Under the $\alpha$-overlap graph notation $G(a, k, k-1) = S(k, a)$, Debra Knisley, Yared Nigussie, and Attila Pór (*J. Combin. Math. Combin. Comput.* **73**, 2010, pp. 3–13) already proved $\chi(G(3, 4, 3)) = \chi(S(4, 3)) = 3$ (and $\chi(G(3, n, n-1)) = 3$ for all $n \ge 4$). However, Knisley–Nigussie–Pór (2010) predates Halldórsson–Kitaev–Pyatkin (2016) and Petyuk (2022) and was never connected in print or public repositories to word-representability or Petyuk's Conjecture 1; `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md` also constructs an independent $\mathbb{Z}_3$-shift-equivariant $3$-coloring and explicit length-$4536$ representing word.
  - **Minor typo & $k \ge 4$ scope (`AUDIT.md` §2.3, §4):** `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md` lines 72–73 write `$(ab)^{28}$`, `$(bc)^{28}$`, `$(ac)^{28}$` where $28$ four-letter blocks `abab`, `bcbc`, `acac` yield `$(ab)^{56}$`, `$(bc)^{56}$`, `$(ac)^{56}$` (harmless to alternation, $w$, and HKP Theorem 2). Word-representability of $S(n, k)$ for $k \ge 4, n \ge 4$ remains open (e.g., $S(4, 4)$ is not $3$-colorable).

### `blanco-2511.16959-conj5` — Proof of Blanco et al. Conjecture 5 (`con:F_k(n)`)

- **Summary**: Blanco, Golubyatnikov, Konstantinova, Maslova, and Nikiforov conjectured in `arXiv:2511.16959v3` (*DMTCS* 28:1, 2026) that $F_k(n) = \{m : k < m < n,\, \langle r_n, r_m, r_k \rangle = \mathrm{Sym}_n\}$ for prefix reversals $r_i$ satisfies $\max_{n > k+1} |F_k(n)| = k + 1$ for even $k \ge 2$ and $(k+1)/2$ for odd $k \ge 3$. Giving a self-contained proof of the upper bound (Proposition 1.1: $m + k \ge n$ when $n$ is even and $m + k \ge n - 1$ when $n$ is odd via acyclicity of $E_m \cup E_k$, plus a parity/bipartite obstruction eliminating odd $m$ when $k$ is odd), and constructing prime degrees $n \equiv 3 \pmod 4$ via CRT and Dirichlet's theorem where $G = \langle r_n, r_m, r_k \rangle$ is transitive, $2$-transitive by Burnside's prime-degree theorem, contains $\mathrm{Alt}_n$ via Jordan/minimal-support arguments, and contains odd permutation $r_n$, we proved the exact maximum for all $k \ge 2$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Saúl A. Blanco, Mikhail P. Golubyatnikov, Elena V. Konstantinova, Natalia V. Maslova, Luka A. Nikiforov, *"Generating the symmetric group by three prefix reversals"*, `arXiv:2511.16959v3` (`Final-DMTCS-Format.tex`, Section 4.1 Conjecture 5 `\label{con:F_k(n)}` lines 948–960; *DMTCS* **28:1** (2026), Paper `#7`, DOI `10.46298/dmtcs.16975`).
  - Statement: *"Let $F_k(n) = \{m \,:\, k < m < n,\, \langle r_n, r_m, r_k \rangle = \mathrm{Sym}_n\}$, then $\max_{n > k+1} |F_k(n)| = k + 1$ if $k$ is even, and $\frac{k+1}{2}$ if $k$ is odd."* (for all integers $k \ge 2$).
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exhaustive Schreier–Sims verification of $F_k(n)$ and Propositions 1.1–1.2 for $4 \le n \le 10$, Section 2 commutator/$\mathrm{AGL}_1(p)$ checks, and Section 3 CRT/Dirichlet constructions for $2 \le k \le 12$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/blanco-2511.16959-conj5/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T03:40:57Z`–`04:10:00Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all 596 repo snapshots, live arXiv (`2511.16959v3` sole latest version; follow-up `arXiv:2610.30892v1` proves Conjectures 2–4 only and does not address Conjecture 5), OpenAlex citing works, `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*` (`#20814` is an open preregistration with 0 comments/PRs), `rainwangphy/aftd-platform` (`PrefixReversal*` covers Conjs 1–4 only), `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, `CrocSwap/integer-mult-bounds`, `small-true-things`, `chy4pro/automath`, and Zenodo; 0 prior proofs of `con:F_k(n)` found.

### `biswas-2603.04876-conj5.5` — Proof of Biswas–Mandal–Saha Conjecture 5.5 ($\mathcal{RV}_{\mathrm{chordal}}(n)$)

- **Summary**: Prativa Biswas, Mousumi Mandal, and Kamalesh Saha proved $A(n) \subseteq \mathcal{RV}_{\mathrm{chordal}}(n)$ in `arXiv:2603.04876v1` (Theorem 3.5) and conjectured equality for all connected chordal graphs on $n \ge 3$ vertices, where $A(n) = \{(r, v) \in \mathbb{N}^2 : 1 \le r < n/2,\ 1 \le v \le r - \lceil r/(n-2r) \rceil + 1\}$. Using a clique-tree greedy bottom-up clique cover $\{B_1, \dots, B_r\}$ paired with a maximum induced matching $M = \{e_1, \dots, e_r\}$ ($r = \mathrm{im}(G) = \mathrm{reg}(G)$) and modifying only the root clique if needed so that every cover clique meets $X = V(G) \setminus V(M)$, a pigeonhole count over the $m = n - 2r \ge 1$ vertices of $X$ produces $x^* \in X$ covering $t \ge \lceil r/m \rceil$ cliques; deleting $N_G[x^*]$ and applying $\mathrm{v}(H) \le \mathrm{im}(H)$ for arbitrary chordal $H$ establishes $\mathrm{v}(G) \le r - \lceil r/(n-2r) \rceil + 1$ (and we also give a self-contained proof of $A(n) \subseteq \mathcal{RV}_{\mathrm{chordal}}(n)$).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Prativa Biswas, Mousumi Mandal, Kamalesh Saha, *"Lattice points arising from regularity and $\mathrm{v}$-number of Graphs: Whisker and Cameron-Walker"*, `arXiv:2603.04876v1` (`Arxiv.tex`, Section 2 Definition 2.4 / Proposition 2.9 / Theorem 2.11 lines 249–346, Section 5 Conjecture 5.5 lines 980–983).
  - Statement: *"For the class of connected chordal graphs on $n\geq 3$ vertices, we have $\mathcal{RV}_{\mathrm{chordal}}(n)=\left\{(r,v)\in \mathbb{N}^{2} \mid 1\leq r<\frac{n}{2}, 1\leq v\leq r-\left\lceil\frac{r}{n-2r}\right\rceil+1\right\}$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exhaustive exact verification of all 352 non-isomorphic connected chordal graphs on $n = 3..7$, all 42 lattice points in $A(3..11)$, and 84 stress graphs on $n = 8..14$ in `notes/check-check-3.py`, plus independent Subcase B2 and Part 2 boundary verification in `AUDIT.md` §3).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/biswas-2603.04876-conj5.5/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T04:32Z`–`05:01Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all 470+ repo snapshots, live arXiv (`2603.04876v1` sole version), OpenAlex (`W7134037417`, `cited_by_count: 0`), `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, `CrocSwap/integer-mult-bounds`, `small-true-things`, `chy4pro/automath`, Crossref, Zenodo, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

Does not address non-chordal graph classes in `arXiv:2603.04876v1` (`PROOF.md` line 174 also makes a passing editorial mention of "Saha–Sengupta witnesses $A$" that does not affect the self-contained proof).

### `oeis-A400888` — Proof of Lafitte's Surjectivity Conjecture for OEIS `A400888`

- **Summary**: M. Jeremie Lafitte (Levitas) conjectured in October 2026 on OEIS `A400888` that the sequence $a(n) = \max\{m \ge 1 : u_n \equiv (0,0)^T \pmod m\}$ (equivalently $\gcd(p_n - e_n, q_n + f_n)$) of quarter-turn corrected Stern vector contents on the Gosper–Lafitte triangle columns is surjective onto $\mathbb{Z}_{\ge 1}$. Using the periodicity $V(4n+3) = V(n)$ and an explicit column-word family $V(n(r,t)) = (0^r 1^r)^{t-1} 0^r 1^{r-1}$, we proved $a(n(r,t)) = r \gcd(X_t, Z_t)$ where $\gcd(X_t, Z_t) \mid t((t-1)r - 2)$. Elementary choices $t \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ yield $a(n(r,t)) = r$ for $r = 1$ ($n = 2$) and all $r \not\equiv 27 \pmod{30}$, while for odd $r$ with $3 \mid r$, combining a quadratic norm bound over $\mathbb{Z}[\alpha]$ with a CRT/Montgomery–Vaughan Brun–Titchmarsh sieve and the Prime Number Theorem in Arithmetic Progressions produces infinitely many prime-indexed $t = (2p + r + 2)/r$ with $\gcd(X_t, Z_t) = 1$, giving $a(n(r,t)) = r$ for every integer $r \ge 1$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A400888` (author: M. Jeremie Lafitte (Levitas), rev. 16, `2026-10-15`) and cross-referenced `A000361` (rev. 53), `A000360` (rev. 154), `A284556` (rev. 28), `A002487` (rev. 719).
  - Name & Conjecture (Comment): *"Contents of the quarter-turn corrected Stern vectors associated with the column words of the Gosper-Lafitte triangle."* / *"It is conjectured that the sequence is surjective onto the positive integers."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by exact verification of all 96 live OEIS `A400888` terms against both PARI `w(n)` and `A000361`, Lemmas 1–5, and $\ge 5$ explicit prime witnesses for every $r \equiv 27 \pmod{30}$ up to $r = 147$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`, plus Step 4 Case 1 parameter checks in `AUDIT.md` §3).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A400888/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T04:53Z`–`05:51Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `470+` repo snapshots, live OEIS `A400888` rev. 16 (still conjectured), `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`, `joaquimmj/oeis-conjectures`, `aliromman/math`, `KnowledgeSense/math`, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform`, `valyuAI/research`, `facebookresearch/atlas-lean`, `JENW1N/complete-math-solutions`, `TheJustinSunPrize/awards`, `open-math-agents`, `ProofPlaygrond`, `CrocSwap/integer-mult-bounds`, `small-true-things`, `chy4pro/automath`, arXiv, Crossref, OpenAlex, Zenodo, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **External analytic theorems & scope (`AUDIT.md` §2.6):** Lemma 6 relies on the Prime Number Theorem in Arithmetic Progressions and Montgomery–Vaughan's (1973, Theorem 2) unconditional Brun–Titchmarsh inequality $\pi(X; M_q, a_q) < \frac{2X}{\varphi(M_q)\log(X/M_q)}$ for $1 \le M_q < X$. Does not determine the smallest index $n$ achieving each value $r$, or whether every odd $r \equiv 27 \pmod{30}$ already has a small $t$ without invoking PNT.

### `oeis-A125790` — Proof of Kurkov's May 2025 Row Generating Function Conjecture for OEIS `A125790`

- **Summary**: Mikhail Kurkov conjectured in May 2025 on OEIS `A125790` that for every row $n > 0$ of Paul D. Hanna's array $T(n,k) = T(n,k-1) + T(n-1,2k)$ ($T(0,k) = T(n,0) = 1$), the row ordinary generating function equals $\bigl(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1} x^i \sum_{j=0}^i \binom{n+1}{j} T(n,i-j)(-1)^j\bigr) / (1-x)^{n+1}$. Using the invertibility of the backward difference operator $\nabla f(t) = f(t) - f(t-1)$ from $\{f \in \mathbb{Q}[t] : \deg f \le n,\ f(0) = 0\}$ to $\{g \in \mathbb{Q}[t] : \deg g \le n - 1\}$, we proved by induction that $T(n,k) = P_n(k)$ for a polynomial $P_n \in \mathbb{Q}[t]$ of degree $\le n$ satisfying $P_n(-1) = P_n(0) - P_{n-1}(0) = 0$. Consequently $(\nabla^{n+1} P_n)(i) = 0$ for all $i \in \mathbb{Z}$, forcing $[x^i](1-x)^{n+1} \sum_{k \ge 0} T(n,k) x^k = 0$ for every $i \ge n$ (including $i = n$ via $P_n(-1) = 0$).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A125790` (author: Paul D. Hanna, rev. 47, `2026-03-22`).
  - Formula & Conjecture: *"T(n,k) = T(n,k-1) + T(n-1,2*k) for n>0, k>0, with T(0,n)=T(n,0)=1 for n>=0."* / *"Conjecture: g.f. for n-th row is (Sum_{i=0..n-1} x^i Sum_{j=0..i} binomial(n+1,j)*T(n,i-j)*(-1)^j)/(1-x)^(n+1) for n > 0. - _Mikhail Kurkov_, May 03 2025"*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (no `sorry`, no `native_decide`, and no axioms beyond `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`; formalizes `a125790_rec`, `rowGF`, `kurkovNum`, `kurkov_row_gf_mul`, `kurkov_row_gf`, and `kurkov_row_gf_of_recurrence` in `lean/OpenMath/OeisA125790.lean`, audited for statement fidelity in `targets/oeis-A125790/AUDIT.md` §5 and coordinator-verified). Also supported by `written proof, internally audited` (`AUDIT.md` and `notes/check-check-1.py`).
