### `oeis-A394511` — Counterexample Refuting Exley's 2026 Truncated-Square (`4.8.8`) Enclosing Shell Conjecture

- **Summary**: Peter Exley conjectured in June 2026 on OEIS `A394511` that the minimum connected enclosing shell size $a(n)$ around an $n$-cell connected hole on the `4.8.8` (truncated-square) Archimedean tiling equals $2\lceil (n+17)/5 \rceil$ for $n \ge 6$ (equivalently $a(n) = a(n-5)+2$ for $n \ge 11$) with optimal shell equal to the corona. We refuted both the closed formula and the recurrence by constructing rectangular octagon-and-square blocks $H(R,C)$ ($R,C \ge 2$) of $n = 2RC + R + C - 3$ cells whose exact corona $C(H(R,C))$ is a valid connected enclosing shell of size $2R + 2C + 4 = O(\sqrt{n})$. In particular, $H(3,5)$ gives $a(35) \le 20 < 22 = 2\lceil (35+17)/5 \rceil$ (and $a(20) = 16$ from OEIS would force $a(35) = 22$), and the diagonal family $R = C = k \ge 4$ yields infinitely many counterexamples $n_k = 2k^2 + 2k - 3$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A394511` (author: Peter Exley, May 05 2026, rev. `47`, `2026-06-06T04:04:55-04:00`).
  - Name & Conjecture (Comment): *"Minimum number of cells in a connected enclosing shell of an n-cell connected hole on the truncated-square (4.8.8) Archimedean tiling."* / *"Conjectured (UNVERIFIED beyond n = 23): the minimum shell equals the corona of the optimal hole, |S| = |C(H)|; and a(n) = 2*ceiling((n + 17)/5) for n >= 6, equivalently a(n) = a(n - 5) + 2 for n >= 11. - _Peter Exley_, Jun 05 2026"* (with `a(20) = 16` in OEIS Data).
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (upgraded from `counterexample, verified by exact computation`; Lean 4 formalization in [`lean/OpenMath/OeisA394511.lean`](lean/OpenMath/OeisA394511.lean), imported in [`lean/OpenMath.lean`](lean/OpenMath.lean) L13, statement-fidelity audit PASS by `audit-1` in `AUDIT.md` §5; coordinator verified `.lake/build/lib/lean/OpenMath/OeisA394511.olean` and `cd lean && lake env lean OpenMath/OeisA394511.lean` exiting `0` with 0 `sorry`/warnings and axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`, proving `SRect_isValidEnclosingShell`, `a394511_35_le_20`, `not_exley_conjecture_formula`, `exley_infinite_family_refutation`, and `not_exley_conjecture_recurrence`; also supported by `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact integer verification in `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A394511/notes/check-check-3.py` and `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake env lean OpenMath/OeisA394511.lean`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T08:48Z`–`08:52Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`; plus `2026-11-27T07:01:00Z` `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `586` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 22), live OEIS `A394511` rev. `47` (unchanged), live `gh search issues`/`code`, `the-omega-institute/trureturing`, `629` discovered repo snapshots, arXiv, and Zenodo API; 0 prior disproofs or proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - Establishes upper bounds $a(35) \le 20$, $a(34) \le 20$, $a(37) \le 20$ and $a(2k^2 + 2k - 3) \le 4k + 4$ ($k \ge 4$) rather than the exact minimum value of $a(n)$ for all $n \ge 24$, and does not determine the smallest $n \ge 24$ where $a(n) < 2\lceil (n+17)/5 \rceil$.

### `ziller-2310.15628-conj6.2` — Proof of Ziller Conjecture 6.2 (Injectivity of $\varphi(x)/x^2$)

- **Summary**: Mario Ziller conjectured in `arXiv:2310.15628v2` (Section 6, Conjecture 6.2) that all values of $\varphi(x)/x^2$ for $x \in \mathbb{N}$ are pairwise distinct, after proving only the squarefree/coprime special case. We proved this conjecture completely by writing $\varphi(n)/n^2 = \prod_{q \mid n} (q-1)/q^{e_q+1}$ and analyzing the $p$-adic valuation at the largest prime factor $p$ of $xy$ for a minimal-product counterexample $x \neq y$. Because every prime $q \mid xy$ satisfies $q - 1 < p$, $v_p(q - 1) = 0$, forcing $v_p(x) = v_p(y) = e \ge 1$ and descending via multiplicativity to $f(x/p^e) = f(y/p^e)$ with strictly smaller product, a contradiction.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Mario Ziller, `arXiv:2310.15628v2` (`Ziller_2023_v2.tex` line 116, `Examples.tex` line 296, `Conclusions.tex` Section 6 "Concluding remarks", Conjecture 6.2 lines 89–91, updated `2026-04-15`).
  - Statement: *"All function values of $\frac{\varphi(x)}{x^2}$ for $x\in\mathbb{N}$ are pairwise distinct."* (where $\mathbb{N} = \{1, 2, 3, \dots\}$ and $\varphi(x) = x \prod_{p \mid x}(1 - 1/p)$).
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (Lean 4 formalization `lean/OpenMath/Ziller231015628Conj62.lean` — theorems `ziller_conjecture_6_2`, `ziller_conjecture_6_2_pnat`, `ziller_conjecture_6_2_reciprocal`, `totient_mul_sq_inj` — verified with zero `sorry`/`native_decide` and standard axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`, `AUDIT.md` §5 statement-fidelity & hygiene PASS; also supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` §§1–4 PASS, and `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake build OpenMath.Ziller231015628Conj62 && lake env lean OpenMath/Ziller231015628Conj62.lean` (plus Python check `.venv/bin/python targets/ziller-2310.15628-conj6.2/notes/check-check-3.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T08:48Z`–`08:52Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`; plus `2026-11-27T06:36:49Z`–`06:48:42Z` `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md`): checked all `586` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 22), live arXiv (`2310.15628v2` latest; only citing paper `arXiv:2604.19646v1` checked and confirmed not to resolve Conjecture 6.2), live OEIS (`A373318`, `A373319`), `gh search` (`trureturing#24177` is unrelated OEIS `A349824`), competing repo trees, Zenodo, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Scholarly disclosure on sibling exponent (`AUDIT.md` §3):** The same largest-prime $p$-adic valuation descent is well known in the literature for the sibling integer-valued function $n \mapsto n\varphi(n) = \varphi(n^2)$ (e.g., P. Giblin, *Primes and Programming*, CUP 1993, p. 116 Ex. 1.10; OEIS `A002618` F. Vrabec 2012; MSE `#68120` & `#539558`; Gabdullin–Iudelevich–Luca, `arXiv:2201.09287v2` / *Int. J. Number Theory* 19(5), 2023, §4). None of those sources state or prove injectivity of $\varphi(x)/x^2$ (or general $n^\alpha \varphi(n)$), which Ziller left open as Conjecture 6.2 in April 2026 (`2310.15628v2`).
  - Does not generalize beyond $\varphi(x)/x^2$ (and its reciprocal $x^2/\varphi(x)$); OpenAlex/Semantic Scholar live endpoints were rate-limited (`429`) during audit/Gate 3, though the sole OpenAlex citation (`arXiv:2604.19646v1`) was verified directly. Internal audit and Lean formalization are not external journal peer review.

### `oeis-A092920` — Proof of Kurkov's 2023 Binary-Valuation / Binomial Sum Conjecture for OEIS `A092920`

- **Summary**: Mikhail Kurkov conjectured in April 2023 on OEIS `A092920` that the number $a(n)$ of strongly monotone partitions of $[n]$ equals $\sum_{k=0}^{2^{n-1}-1} b(k)$ for $n > 0$, where $b(2^m(2n+1)) = \sum_{k=0}^{[m>0](m-1)} \binom{m-1}{k} b(2^k n)$ with $b(0) = 1$. We proved this identity completely by first establishing an explicit FIFO-queue bijection from strongly monotone partitions of $[n]$ to labeled Motzkin paths $\mathcal{M}_n$, yielding the standard J-fraction $A(x) = (1 - x - x^2 B_0(x))^{-1}$ with $B_m(x) = (1 - (m+1)x - x^2 B_{m+1}(x))^{-1}$. We then translated the $b$-recurrence into an exponential generating function PDE $x u \, \partial_u^2 H(x,u) + (x - 1)\partial_u H(x,u) + x H(x,u) = 0$ ($u = e^z$) whose hypergeometric solution satisfies the exact same J-fraction $x$-adically, proving $H(x, 1) = A(x)$ coefficientwise.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A092920` (author: Ralf Stephan, Apr 17 2004; latest rev. `56`, `2026-03-14T12:38:00-04:00`), offset `0,3`.
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Number of strongly monotone partitions of [n]."* / *"Conjecture: a(n) = Sum_{k=0..2^(n-1) - 1} b(k) for n > 0 with a(0) = 1 where b(2^m*(2n+1)) = Sum_{k=0..[m > 0]*(m-1)} binomial(m-1, k)*b(2^k*n) for m >= 0, n >= 0 with b(0) = 1. - _Mikhail Kurkov_, Apr 24 2023"* (with $\binom{-1}{0} = 1$ at $m=0$, giving $b(2n+1) = b(n)$).
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact rational/combinatorial verification of the bijection for $n \le 7$, J-fraction matching all $28$ OEIS terms $n \le 27$, direct Kurkov sums for $n \le 15$, and Sections 2–3 formal power series identities through degree $13$ in `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A092920/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T08:48Z`–`08:52Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `586` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 22), live OEIS `A092920` rev. `56` (still marked `Conjecture:`), live arXiv API (`arxiv_submissions.json` 1,000 entries + live query), `gh search issues`/`code`, Zenodo API, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - Establishes the formula for OEIS `A092920` ($n > 0$) without giving an asymptotic formula for $a(n)$ or a direct non-generating-function combinatorial bijection between strongly monotone partitions and $b(k)$ summands.
  - Closed-access venues and private repositories are not covered; internal adversarial audit and finite exact checks ($n \le 27$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `cassaigne-2602.21895-conjC4` — Proof of Dekking Conjecture `(C_4)` for Factor Frequencies in $\mathbf{t}_{3/2}$ and $\mathbf{t}'$

- **Summary**: Cassaigne, Espinoza, Rigo, and Stipulanti restated Dekking's Conjecture `(C_4)` in `arXiv:2602.21895v1` (Section 1.4 & Section 7), asking whether every finite binary word $w$ has a well-defined frequency in $\mathbf{t}' = \varphi(\mathbf{t}_{3/2})$ (`OEIS A357448`) and in the base-$3/2$ Thue–Morse word $\mathbf{t}_{3/2}$, and whether $w$ and its reversal $w^R$ always have equal frequency. Starting from their single-letter ($|w| = 1$) equidistribution theorem ($\mu_n(a,k) = 2^{-n-1}$) and using exact $3$-to-$2$ desubstitution preimages $\psi_0, \psi_1, \psi_2$ on residue-filtered counts $C_n(w,k,N)$ together with a diagonal limit-contraction argument on $|w| \ge 2$, we proved that all factor frequencies $\mathrm{freq}_{\mathbf{t}_{3/2}}(w)$ and $\mathrm{freq}_{\mathbf{t}'}(w)$ exist and are explicitly computable rational numbers (including $\mathrm{freq}_{\mathbf{t}_{3/2}}({\tt 00}) = 3/10$, $\mathrm{freq}_{\mathbf{t}_{3/2}}({\tt 01}) = 1/5$, and $\mathrm{freq}_{\mathbf{t}'}({\tt 01}) = 2/5$). By establishing involution symmetries $S(3M+1-m) = 2M-S(m)$ and $\varepsilon(3M+1-m) = \varepsilon(m)$ and lifting through the palindromic morphism $\varphi$, we further proved reversal invariance $\mathrm{freq}(w) = \mathrm{freq}(w^R)$ and bit-complement invariance $\mathrm{freq}(w) = \mathrm{freq}(\bar{w})$ for all $w \in \{{\tt 0}, {\tt 1}\}^*$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Julien Cassaigne, Bastián Espinoza, Michel Rigo, Manon Stipulanti, *"Symbols frequencies in the Thue--Morse word in base $3/2$ and related conjectures"*, `arXiv:2602.21895v1` (`2026-02-25`, `merge_version.tex`, Section 1.3–1.4 lines 411–446 & 493–495, Section 5 `thm:existence_freqs_iff_mun_exist` lines 1265–1273, Section 7 lines 2090–2121).
  - Statement (Conjecture `(C_4)`): *"(1) It is conjectured that frequencies of the words $w \in \mathcal{A}^*$ occurring in $\mathbf{t}'$ exist. (2) It is also conjectured that $w$ and its reversal have the same frequency. Similar questions can also be asked for $\mathbf{t}_{3/2}$."* (where $t_0 = {\tt 0}$, $t_{3n} = t_{3n+1} = t_{2n}$, $t_{3n+2} = 1 - t_{2n+1}$, and $\mathbf{t}' = \varphi(\mathbf{t}_{3/2})$ with $\varphi({\tt 0}) = {\tt 010}$, $\varphi({\tt 1}) = {\tt 101}$).
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, `notes/contest-ruling-audit-2.md` `STILL-OPEN`, and exact rational/counting verification of prefixes, Lemma 1 ($2 \le L \le 50$), Lemma 2 ($2 \le L \le 5$, $N \le 10{,}000$), exact rational frequencies and symmetries ($1 \le L \le 9$ in $\mathbf{t}_{3/2}$, $1 \le L \le 7$ in $\mathbf{t}'$), and empirical densities on $\mathbf{t}_{3/2}[0..100000)$ and $\mathbf{t}'[0..300000)$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/cassaigne-2602.21895-conjC4/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T08:48Z`–`08:52Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`; plus `2026-11-27T06:31:37Z`–`06:53:31Z` `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-3.md` and contest ruling `notes/contest-ruling-audit-2.md` `2026-11-27T08:09Z`): checked all `586` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 22), live arXiv (`2602.21895v1` sole version; citing `arXiv:2611.06878v1` concerns the Kolakoski shift), live OEIS `A357448` rev. `20`, `gh search`, competing repo trees, and Zenodo API; no prior settlement of full Conjecture `(C_4)` found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Transparent attribution for competing Part 1 work (`notes/contest-ruling-audit-2.md` §3):** `the-omega-institute/trureturing#24005` (`2026-10-30`, open) publicly posted a 4-step Toeplitz-skeleton proof plan — and `#29075` (`2026-11-11`, open) announced an unpushed/unmerged Lean 4 completion on internal branch `lane/math/op-dekking2301` (HTTP 404 publicly) — covering **Part 1 alone** (existence of real factor frequencies $\mu[w] \in \mathbb{R}$ in $\mathbf{t}'$ and $\mu[00]=1/10, \mu[01]=4/10$, i.e. Dekking Journal Conjecture 7). Neither `#24005`/`#29075` nor any other public source addresses **Part 2** (reversal invariance $\mathrm{freq}(w) = \mathrm{freq}(w^R)$, Dekking Journal Conjecture 9), complement invariance (Dekking Journal Conjecture 8), or explicit rationality $\mathrm{freq}(w) \in \mathbb{Q}$, all of which `PROOF.md` settles via an independent desubstitution-contraction proof for both $\mathbf{t}_{3/2}$ and $\mathbf{t}'$.
  - **Dependencies & scope (`AUDIT.md` §4):** Relies on `thm:existence_freqs_iff_mun_exist` and `lem:morphic_image` of `arXiv:2602.21895v1` for the $|w|=1$ base case and morphism definition; does not prove uniform convergence (unique ergodicity of the subshift) or characterize which binary words have nonzero frequency. Internal audit and finite exact checks are not external peer review or a Lean 4 formalization.

### `oeis-A350963` — Proof of Sloane's 2022 Conjectured Generating Function for OEIS `A350963` (`XXOXX` Tiling)

- **Summary**: N. J. A. Sloane conjectured in February 2022 on OEIS `A350963` that the coordination sequence $a(n)$ of the planar `XXOXX` polyomino tiling with respect to a type-R tile has ordinary generating function $-(2t^7 - 6t^6 - 24t^5 - 33t^4 - 33t^3 - 28t^2 - 9t - 1)/((1-t^2)(1-t^4))$. We proved this formula in full by constructing an explicit periodic graph representation over $\Lambda = \mathbb{Z}(4,-1) + \mathbb{Z}(0,6)$ with a half-turn automorphism exchanging the two type-R orbits, and showing that the exact graph distance from the root equals $D^*(v) = \max_{1 \le s \le 8} F_s(v) + \mathbf{1}_{v \in E_{\mathrm{exc}}}$ across all tiles via a $1$-Lipschitz lower bound and collar descent outside a bounded core $B_{\mathrm{core}}$. Exact octagonal shell enumeration then yields $a(0) = 1$, $a(1) = 9$, and $a(4m+r) = 17(4m+r)-5$ ($r \in \{0,2\}$), $16(4m+1)-5$, $16(4m+3)-6$ for all $n \ge 2$, whose generating function equals Sloane's rational expression verbatim.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A350963` (author: N. J. A. Sloane, Feb 25–26 2022; rev. `54`, `2025-11-05T15:22:48-05:00`) and Rémy Sigrist's OEIS-linked PARI/GP definition `a350963.gp.txt`.
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Coordination sequence for the XXOXX tiling with respect to a tile of type R."* / *"Conjectured g.f.: -(2*t^7-6*t^6-24*t^5-33*t^4-33*t^3-28*t^2-9*t-1)/((1-t^2)*(1-t^4)). - _N. J. A. Sloane_, Feb 26 2022"*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS including symbolic verification of all Case B collar descent cells and Section 3 shell polynomials, and independent verification of the mod-$\Lambda$ tiling, 50 edges, $1$-Lipschitz property, $B_{\mathrm{core}}$ 5,166 vertices, BFS through radius 55, all 54 OEIS terms, and generating function expansion to $t^{100}$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/oeis-A350963/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T08:52Z`–`08:53Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `601` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 23), live OEIS `A350963` rev. `54` (still marked `Conjectured g.f.`), live `gh search issues`/`code`, `1,000` recent arXiv entries + live arXiv query, and Zenodo API; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - Settles `A350963` (type-R root) only; the companion `XXOXX` coordination sequences `A351966` and `A351967` (types G and B) are not proved here.
  - Closed-access venues and private repositories are not covered; internal adversarial audit and finite exact checks (radius $\le 55$, $t^{100}$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `oeis-A286044` — Proof of Yanev's 2026 Nested-Recurrence Formula for OEIS `A286044`

- **Summary**: Velin Yanev conjectured in February 2026 on OEIS `A286044` that the $\{011 \to 0\}$-transform $a(n)$ of the Thue–Morse word (`A010060`) satisfies $a(n) = b(b(n-2)) - b(b(n-3))$ for all $n > 3$, where $b$ is Hofstadter-style nested recurrence `A003160` ($b(1)=b(2)=1$, $b(n) = n - b(b(n-1)) - b(b(n-2))$). We proved this identity completely by showing that all `011` blocks in Thue–Morse are disjoint and the surviving `1`s occur at $1$-based positions $2v(m) + m$, matching the indicator $a(n) = \mathbf{1}_{v(n)\text{ even}}$ of even vile numbers ($V = \{k \ge 1 : 2 \mid v_2(k)\}$, `A003159`). We then proved by level-set block induction that $b(n) = v(n+1) - (n+1)$ for all $n \ge 1$ and deduced $b(b(n-2)) - b(b(n-3)) = 1 \iff v(n)$ is even for every $n > 3$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A286044` (author: Clark Kimberling, May 07 2017; rev. `8`, `2026-02-19T22:02:10-05:00`, offset `1`), OEIS `A003160` (rev. `61`, `2026-03-10`), and OEIS `A010060` (Thue–Morse).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"{011->0}-transform of the Thue-Morse word A010060."* / *"Conjecture: a(n) = b(b(n - 2)) - b(b(n - 3)) for n > 3, where b is A003160. - _Velin Yanev_, Feb 16 2026"*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact verification of all 86 OEIS `A286044` prefix terms, `{011->0}` transform through 160,138 terms, vile-number indicator, `A003160` closed form $v(n+1)-(n+1)$, and $a(n) = b(b(n-2)) - b(b(n-3))$ for all $4 \le n \le 120{,}000$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/oeis-A286044/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T08:52Z`–`08:53Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `601` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 23), live OEIS `A286044` rev. `8` (Yanev's Feb 16 2026 formula remains marked `Conjecture:`), live `gh search issues`/`code`, `1,000` recent arXiv entries + live arXiv query, and Zenodo API; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - Closed-access venues and private repositories are not covered; internal adversarial audit and finite exact checks ($4 \le n \le 120{,}000$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `oeis-A190969` — Proof of Zhi-Wei Sun's Supercongruence Conjectures for OEIS `A190969` (`conjecture_1` mod $p^2$ and `conjecture_2` mod $p^3$)

- **Summary**: Zhi-Wei Sun conjectured in March 2013 on OEIS `A190969` (formalized in `google-deepmind/formal-conjectures` as `FormalConjectures/OEIS/190969.lean`) that for $a(n) = 5a(n-1) - 8a(n-2)$ ($a(0)=0, a(1)=1$), the sum $S(p) = \sum_{k=0}^{p-1} a(4k)\binom{2k}{k}^3 / (-4096)^k$ satisfies $S(p) \equiv 0 \pmod{p^2}$ for every odd prime $p$ (`conjecture_1`) and $S(p) \equiv 0 \pmod{p^3}$ for every odd prime $p \equiv 1, 2, 4 \pmod 7$ (`conjecture_2`). Using Binet's formula over $K = \mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-7})$ to write $S(p) = (F_p(t_2) - F_p(t_1))/\sqrt{-7}$ for $F_p(t) = \sum_{k=0}^{p-1} \binom{2k}{k}^3 t^k$ and the conjugate CM parameters $t_{1,2} = (47 \pm 45\sqrt{-7})/8192$ with $j = -15^3$, we proved both conjectures completely. For split primes $p \equiv 1,2,4 \pmod 7$, Beukers' CM supercongruence theorem (`arXiv:2403.03301v3`, Theorem 1.15) at level $N=4$ equates $F_p(\iota(t_1)) \equiv F_p(\iota(t_2)) \equiv \iota(x+ys)^2 \pmod{p^3\mathbb{Z}_p}$; for inert primes $p \equiv 3,5,6 \pmod 7$, a self-contained finite Clausen identity reduces $F_p(t_i)$ mod $p^2$ to the Hasse invariant $H_p(u_i) \equiv 0 \pmod p$ of a supersingular elliptic curve with $j = -3375$ via Deuring's criterion; and $p = 7$ is checked directly mod $49$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A190969` (author: Zhi-Wei Sun, Mar 13 2013; rev. `48`, `2025-11-05T15:22:20-05:00`) and `google-deepmind/formal-conjectures` (`FormalConjectures/OEIS/190969.lean`, `conjecture_1` and `conjecture_2`).
  - Name & Comment (Conjecture): *"a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 8*a(n-2), with a(0)=0, a(1)=1."* / *"Let S(p):=Sum_{k=0..p-1} a(4k)*binomial(2k,k)^3/(-4096)^k. Zhi-Wei Sun conjectured that S(p) == 0 (mod p^2) for every odd prime p, and also S(p) == 0 (mod p^3) for any odd prime p == 1,2,4 (mod 7). - _Zhi-Wei Sun_, Mar 13 2013"*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact integer/rational verification of Sections 2–5 lemmas, all 61 odd primes $\le 300$ for `conjecture_1`, and all 27 split primes $\le 300$ for `conjecture_2` in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A190969/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:00Z`–`09:01Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `601` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 23 and `epoch-research/LeanOpenProblems-results` whose `oeis_a190969` runs failed SafeVerify `sorryAx`), live OEIS `A190969` rev. `48`, live arXiv, `gh search repos`/`issues`, Zenodo API, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **External literature dependencies:** Relies on F. Beukers, *"Supercongruences using modular forms"*, `arXiv:2403.03301v3` (*Alg. Number Th.* 20 (2026), 1727–1768, Theorem 1.15 and Remark 1.14(2)/Appendix C) for `conjecture_2`, and Deuring's / Hasse invariant criteria (Lang, *Elliptic Functions*, Ch. 13 §4 Thm. 12; Silverman, *The Arithmetic of Elliptic Curves*, Ch. V Thm. 4.1) for inert primes in `conjecture_1`. Also shows $S(7) \equiv 294 \not\equiv 0 \pmod{343}$ at the ramified prime $p = 7$.
  - Live `gh search code` hit GitHub rate limit (`HTTP 429`) at Gate 3; closed-access venues and private repositories are not covered. Internal adversarial audit and finite exact checks ($p \le 300$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `haugland-2411.00721-ques2` — Negative Resolution of Haugland–Omland Question 2.3 and Existence in Open Problem (iii) (No Degree-2 Proper Liftings)

- **Summary**: Jan Kristian Haugland and Tron Omland asked in `arXiv:2411.00721v2` (Section 2 Question 2.3 and Section 4 Open Problem (iii)) whether there exists a proper lifting $f \colon \mathbb{F}_2^k \to \mathbb{F}_2$ of algebraic degree $2$ (equivalently, a reversible binary cellular automaton with a quadratic local rule), after verifying none exist for diameter $k \le 7$. We answered this existence question completely in the **negative** by proving that for every $k \ge 2$ and every degree-$2$ Boolean function $f \colon \mathbb{F}_2^k \to \mathbb{F}_2$, the induced shift-invariant map $F_N \colon \mathbb{F}_2^N \to \mathbb{F}_2^N$ at the single power-of-two window size $N = 2^{\lceil \log_2 k \rceil + 1} \in [2k, 4k-4]$ is non-bijective. The proof combines a circulant directional-derivative lemma in $\mathbb{F}_2[z]/((z+1)^N)$ with a double induction on residue sums modulo $2^s$ ($0 \le s \le \lceil \log_2 k \rceil$), forcing all quadratic coefficients $q_{ij}$ of $f$ to vanish whenever $F_N$ is bijective.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Jan Kristian Haugland, Tron Omland, *"New classes of reversible cellular automata"*, `arXiv:2411.00721v2` (updated `2026-11-01`, DOI `10.1007/s10623-026-01869-z`, `haugland-omland-reversible-v2.tex`, Section 1 lines 89–101, Section 2 Question 2.3 lines 327–331, Section 4 Open Problem (iii) line 455).
  - Question 2.3 & Open Problem (iii): *"Does there exist a proper lifting of degree $2$?"* / *"Do proper liftings of algebraic degree $2$ exist, or more generally, what is the minimal possible degree as a function of diameter?"*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact verification of $N$ bounds/Lemma 3 hypotheses for $k \in [2,500]$, Eq. (3) in $\mathbb{F}_2[w]$, exhaustive checks for all $k=2$ ($8$ functions) and $k=3$ ($112$ functions), all $1{,}008$ sampled $k=4$ functions, $800$ diameter-$k$ functions for $k \in [5,12]$, and sharpness family $f_m$ for $m \in \{1,2,3\}$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/haugland-2411.00721-ques2/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:00Z`–`09:01Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `601` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 23), live arXiv (`2411.00721v2` latest; OpenAlex `cited_by_count: 5`, with `arXiv:2609.35921v1` covering only $k=5, 4 \mid n$), `gh search repos`/`issues`, Zenodo API, and web search; 0 prior proofs for general diameter $k$ found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Strict scope (`AUDIT.md` §2):** Settles Question 2.3 and the existence part of Open Problem (iii) completely in the negative (proving every non-affine proper lifting has algebraic degree $\ge 3$); the secondary part of Open Problem (iii) (*"what is the minimal possible degree as a function of diameter?"* for arbitrary $k$) remains open and is outside the scope of this proof.
  - Relies on Proposition 1.2 of `arXiv:2411.00721v2` (affine proper liftings have diameter $1$) and Kari's Theorem 7 (cellular automaton equivalence) for the concluding corollaries; live `gh search code` hit GitHub rate limit (`HTTP 429`) at Gate 3. Internal adversarial audit and finite exact checks are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `oeis-A091524` — Proof of Kurkov's 2024 Graham–Pollak / Beatty Ratio Formula for OEIS `A091524`

- **Summary**: Mikhail Kurkov conjectured in April 2024 on OEIS `A091524` that the multiplier $a(n)$ of $\sqrt{2}$ in the binary constant $\alpha(n) = a(n)\sqrt{2} - b(n)$ determined by the Graham–Pollak sequence starting at $u_1^{(n)} = n$ equals $\sqrt{\mathrm{A028982}(n)/\mathrm{A006337}(n)}$ for all $n \ge 1$. Partitioning $\mathbb{Z}_{\ge 1}$ via the complementary Beatty sequences $B_1(k) = k + \lfloor k/\sqrt{2} \rfloor$ and $B_2(k) = k + \lfloor k\sqrt{2} \rfloor$ corresponding to the ordered set $S = \mathbb{Z}_{\ge 1} \cup \sqrt{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\ge 1}$, we proved by induction on $r$ that $u_r^{(m)} = \lfloor \tau_m (2^{(r-1)/2} + 2^{(r-2)/2}) \rfloor$ for all $r \ge 1$, giving $\alpha(m) = \{k\sqrt{2}\}$ and $a(B_1(k)) = a(B_2(k)) = k$. Evaluating $\mathrm{A028982}(B_1(k)) = k^2$, $\mathrm{A028982}(B_2(k)) = 2k^2$, $\mathrm{A006337}(B_1(k)) = 1$, and $\mathrm{A006337}(B_2(k)) = 2$ yields $\sqrt{\mathrm{A028982}(n)/\mathrm{A006337}(n)} = k = a(n)$ for every $n \ge 1$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A091524` (author: Eric W. Weisstein, Jan 18 2004; rev. `27`, `2025-02-16T08:32:52-05:00`, offset `1,3`), OEIS `A001521` (rev. `105`), OEIS `A028982` (rev. `156`), and OEIS `A006337` (rev. `104`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"a(m) is the multiplier of sqrt(2) in the constant alpha(m) = a(m)*sqrt(2) - b(m), where alpha(m) is the value of the constant determined by the binary bits in the recurrence associated with the Graham-Pollak sequence."* / *"Conjecture: a(n) = sqrt(A028982(n)/A006337(n)). - _Mikhail Kurkov_, Apr 25 2024"*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2})$ verification of the Beatty partition, Graham–Pollak recurrence equivalence, all $75$ OEIS `A091524` terms, $54$ `A028982` terms, $105$ `A006337` terms, and Kurkov's identity for $1 \le n \le 5{,}000$ in `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A091524/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:04Z`–`09:05:15Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `605` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 23 and `434` discovered repo snapshots), live OEIS `A091524` rev. `27` (still marked `Conjecture:`), live arXiv, `gh search repos`/`issues`, Zenodo API, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - Live `gh search code` was skipped/rate-limited (`HTTP 429`) at Gate 3 (though checked at Gate 1); closed-access venues and private repositories are not covered. Internal adversarial audit and finite exact checks ($n \le 5{,}000$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `chervov-2509.19162-conj9` — Disproof of Chervov et al. Conjecture 9 (`diam(U(n, Z/2Z)) = A136184(n)`)

- **Summary**: Chervov, Fedoriaka, Konstantinova, Naumov, Kiselev, et al. conjectured in `arXiv:2509.19162v2` (Conjecture 9) that the diameter of the upper unitriangular group $U(n, \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})$ with respect to the fundamental root transvections $I + E_{k,k+1}$ equals the number $M(n)$ of isomorphism classes of metacyclic groups of order $2^n$ (`OEIS A136184`). We refuted this conjecture immediately at $n = 2$ ($\mathrm{diam}(U(2, \mathbb{F}_2)) = 1 < 2 = M(2)$) and asymptotically for $n = 176$ and **all** $n \ge 178$. Explicitly, row-by-row conjugation factorization proves $\mathrm{diam}(U(n, \mathbb{F}_2)) \le 2n^2 - 6n + 5$ for all $n \ge 2$, whereas an explicit $\Theta(n^3)$ family of metacyclic presentations $G(a, c, s)$ distinguished by $(G^{\mathrm{ab}}, Z(G))$ gives $M(n) \ge |\mathcal{F}_n| > 2n^2 - 6n + 5$ at $n = 176$ ($61{,}404 > 60{,}901$) and for every $n \ge 178$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: A. Chervov, D. Fedoriaka, E. V. Konstantinova, A. Naumov, I. Kiselev, et al., *"CayleyPy Growth: Efficient growth computations and hundreds of new conjectures on Cayley graphs (Brief version)"*, `arXiv:2509.19162v2` (`2026-04-01`, `mainv2.tex`, Subsection *"Bounds on the diameters of unitriangular groups"*, lines 2203–2231, Conjecture 9) and OEIS `A136184` (rev. `31`, `2026-09-17`).
  - Statement (Conjecture 9): *"The diameter of $U(n,\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})$ with respect to the fundamental root transvections is equal to the number of metacyclic groups of order $2^n$ (OEIS A136184)."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`, `AUDIT.md` §4 PASS, and exact-arithmetic verification of BFS diameters for $n = 2..5$ [and $n = 6$ in audit], explicit word factorizations up to $n = 176$, metacyclic group laws/invariants for $n = 6, 7, 8$, and the cubic-vs-quadratic threshold inequality for $n = 176$ and $178 \le n \le 500$ in `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/chervov-2509.19162-conj9/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:04Z`–`09:05:15Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `605` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 23 and `434` discovered repo snapshots), live arXiv (`2509.19162v2` latest; OpenAlex citing papers `2610.22571`, `2610.14373`, `2610.08385`, `2607.12026`, `2511.16959` do not address Conjecture 9), live OEIS `A136184` rev. `31`, `gh search repos`/`issues`, Zenodo API, and web search; 0 prior refutations found.
- **What is not established**:
  - Proves failure at $n = 2$, $n = 176$, and all $n \ge 178$ (while matching $M(n)$ on $3 \le n \le 6$); does not determine whether equality fails at any intermediate values $7 \le n \le 175$ or $n = 177$, nor whether the true asymptotic growth of $\mathrm{diam}(U(n, \mathbb{F}_2))$ is smaller than $2n^2 - 6n + 5$.
  - Live `gh search code` was rate-limited (`HTTP 429`) at Gate 3; closed-access venues and private repositories are not covered. Internal adversarial audit and finite exact checks are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `dawsey-2107.14284-conj5.1` — Proof of Dawsey–Just–Schneider Conjecture 4.1 (Prime-Index Cesàro–Abelian Theorem)

- **Summary**: Madeline Locus Dawsey, Matthew Just, and Robert Schneider conjectured in `arXiv:2107.14284v2` (Section 4 `\label{sect5}`, Conjecture 4.1 `\label{conj1}`) that whenever an arithmetic function $f \colon \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{C}$ has a finite Cesàro average $L_f = \lim_{N \to \infty} \frac{1}{N} \sum_{k=1}^N f(k)$, the prime-indexed ratio $\bigl(\sum_{p \in \mathbb{P}} f(\mathrm{idx}(p)) p^{-s}\bigr) / \bigl(\sum_{p \in \mathbb{P}} p^{-s}\bigr)$ converges to $L_f$ as $s \to 1^+$. Using Abel summation by parts with $p_k \ge k$ and $|S_k| \le Ck$, we proved that the ordered Dirichlet series $\lim_{M \to \infty} \sum_{k=1}^M f(k) p_k^{-s}$ converges for every real $s > 1$ and equals $\sum_{k=1}^\infty S_k(p_k^{-s} - p_{k+1}^{-s})$. Splitting $\sum_{k=1}^\infty (S_k/k - L_f) k (p_k^{-s} - p_{k+1}^{-s}) / P(s)$ at a finite tail cutoff $K$ and invoking Euler's theorem $\lim_{s \to 1^+} P(s) = +\infty$ yields the exact limit $L_f$ as $s \to 1^+$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Madeline Locus Dawsey, Matthew Just, Robert Schneider, *"A 'supernormal' partition statistic"*, `arXiv:2107.14284v2` (`main.tex`, Section 4 `\label{sect5}` lines 954–1086, Conjecture 4.1 `\label{conj1}` lines 1083–1086; also published in *J. Number Theory* 241 (2022), 120–141; folder slug `dawsey-2107.14284-conj5.1` retains `conj5.1` from the TeX label `\label{sect5}`).
  - Statement (Conjecture 4.1 `\label{conj1}`): *"For $f(n)$ an arithmetic function, if the limit of the average value $L_f=\lim_{N\to \infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N} f(k)$ exists, then $\lim_{s\to 1^+}\ \frac{\sum_{p\in \mathbb P} f\left(\mathrm{idx}(p)\right)p^{-s}}{\sum_{p\in \mathbb P} p^{-s}}\ =\ L_f$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` §4 PASS, and exact integer/`Fraction`/interval checks over the first $1{,}200$ primes, Abel summation telescoping identities, and $s \to 1^+$ error bounds in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/dawsey-2107.14284-conj5.1/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:06Z`–`09:07Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `605` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 23 and `434` discovered repo snapshots), live arXiv (`2107.14284v2` latest; OpenAlex `cited_by_count: 15`, incl. `2511.20829v1`, `2501.00580`, `2410.00941`, `10.1007/s11139-026-01350-4`), `gh search repos`/`issues`, Zenodo API, and web search; 0 prior proofs of Conjecture 4.1 found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Ordered Dirichlet series & real-limit scope (`AUDIT.md` §2):** For unbounded $f$ with finite Cesàro mean, convergence of $\sum_{p \in \mathbb{P}} f(\mathrm{idx}(p)) p^{-s}$ on $1 < s \le 2$ holds in the sense of the ordered Dirichlet series $\lim_{M \to \infty} \sum_{k=1}^M f(k) p_k^{-s}$ (not necessarily unconditionally/absolutely). Proves the stated real limit $s \to 1^+$ of Conjecture 4.1 (`\label{conj1}`), not complex Stolz-sector limits (`main.tex` footnote, line 1054) nor Conjecture 4.2 (`\label{conj2}`).
  - Paywalled *J. Number Theory* 241 (2022), 120–141 PDF and `10.1007/s11139-026-01350-4` full text were not downloaded, and live `gh search code` was rate-limited at Gate 3 (checked at Gate 1); internal adversarial audit and finite exact checks are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `oeis-A158826` — Proof of Mathar's 2021 D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture for OEIS `A158826`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in August 2021 on OEIS `A158826` that the third compositional iterate $y(x) = f(f(f(x)))$ of the shifted Catalan series $f(x) = x C(x)$ satisfies a 5-step D-finite linear recurrence of order $4$ with degree-$4$ polynomial coefficients for all $n \ge 6$. Using $F(f(x)) = x$ for $F(t) = t(1-t)$, we showed $P_3(y(x)) = x$ where $P_3(y) = F(F(F(y)))$ has degree $8$, and cleared $P_3'(y)^7$ to verify an exact polynomial identity $U(y) = 0$ in $\mathbb{Z}[y]$ proving that $y(x)$ satisfies a fourth-order inhomogeneous linear ODE $\sum_{d=0}^4 c_{d+1}(x) y^{(d)}(x) + c_0(x) = 0$. Extracting $[x^{n-4}]$ for all $n \ge 6$ yields Mathar's exact 2021 recurrence unconditionally.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A158826` (revision `54`, `2026-05-25T10:12:49-04:00`, offset `1,2`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Third iteration of x*C(x) where C(x) is the Catalan function (A000108)."* / *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence 1053*n*(n-1)*(n-2)*(n-3)*a(n) -36*(n-1)*(n-2)*(n-3)*(634*n-1367)*a(n-1) +24*(n-2)*(n-3)*(7966*n^2-43500*n+61181)*a(n-2) -8*(n-3)*(96128*n^3-957424*n^2+3221878*n-3665189)*a(n-3) +16*(91904*n^4-1446528*n^3+8575792*n^2-22703688*n+22652013)*a(n-4) -256*(8*n-35)*(8*n-41)*(8*n-39)*(8*n-37)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Aug 30 2021"*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact symbolic verification of $P_3(y)$, implicit derivatives, $U(y) = 0$ in $\mathbb{Z}[y]$, Step 3 coefficient extraction in $\mathbb{Z}[n]$, 21 OEIS prefix terms, $P_3(y(x)) \equiv x \pmod{x^{100}}$, and Mathar's recurrence for $6 \le n \le 99$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A158826/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:08Z`–`09:09:30Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `605` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 23 and `434` discovered repo snapshots), live OEIS `A158826` rev. `54` (still marked `Conjecture`), live arXiv, `gh search repos`/`issues` (and Gate 2 `watch-6` `gh search code`), Zenodo API, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

### `bui-2508.04602-conj8.1` — Proof of Bui's Acyclicity Conjecture (`arXiv:2508.04602v1`, §8.2 Conjecture 8.1)

- **Summary**: Hong Duc Bui conjectured in `arXiv:2508.04602v1` (Section 8.2, Conjecture 8.1) that for any general-position planar order type $Q$ of $2n$ points with $n$ convex hull vertices $Q_1, \dots, Q_n$ and $n$ interior points $B_1, \dots, B_n$, the directed swap graph $G$ on $S_n$ connecting $\pi$ to $\rho$ whenever two nontrivially intersecting triangles $\triangle Q_i B_{\pi_i} Q_{i+1}$ and $\triangle Q_j B_{\pi_j} Q_{j+1}$ swap $\pi_i \leftrightarrow \pi_j$ is acyclic. Choosing a realization of $Q$ in $\mathbb{R}^2$ and proving that nontrivial triangle intersection is equivalent to nonempty interior intersection, we showed that any point $Z \in \mathrm{int}(T_i(\pi)) \cap \mathrm{int}(T_j(\pi))$ yields $\ell_i(B_{\pi_j})\ell_j(B_{\pi_i}) < \ell_i(B_{\pi_i})\ell_j(B_{\pi_j})$ for the positive oriented edge-height functions $\ell_k(X) = \det(Q_{k+1} - Q_k, X - Q_k)$. Consequently the positive product potential $\Phi(\pi) = \prod_{k=1}^n \ell_k(B_{\pi_k})$ strictly decreases along every directed edge of $G$, proving $G$ is a DAG and the swap algorithm terminates in at most $n! - 1$ steps (with the two swapped triangles becoming disjoint).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Hong Duc Bui, *"On existence of a compatible triangulation with the double circle order type"*, `arXiv:2508.04602v1` (`2025-08-06`, `article.tex`, Section 8.2 *"Matching Problem and Acyclicity of State Graph"*, lines 1188–1217, Conjecture 8.1).
  - Statement: *"The algorithm described above always terminates; equivalently, the directed graph $G$ is acyclic for all order types $Q$ in general position (with $|Q|=2n$ and $|\mathrm{CH}(Q)|=n$)."*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (upgraded from `written proof, internally audited`; Lean 4 formalization [`lean/OpenMath/Bui250804602Conj81.lean`](lean/OpenMath/Bui250804602Conj81.lean), imported in [`lean/OpenMath.lean`](lean/OpenMath.lean) L17; `AUDIT.md` §5 statement-fidelity & kernel audit PASS by `@audit-2` at `2026-11-27T10:53Z` verifying `trianglesIntersect_iff_open`, `area_product_strict_decrease`, `swapped_triangles_disjoint`, `potential_strict_decrease`, `bui_conjecture_8_2_acyclic`, and `bui_conjecture_8_2_terminates` with standard axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`, zero `sorry`/warnings; supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact integer verification across $34$ instances for $n = 3..7$ totaling $96{,}940$ directed swap edges in `notes/check-check-1.py`).

### `riener-2605.25743-conjHermite` — Proof of Riener–Shapiro Conjecture 6.5 (`conj:hermite`) and Conjecture 1.1 (`conj:sos`)

- **Summary**: Cordian Riener and Boris Shapiro conjectured in `arXiv:2605.25743v3` (Sections 1 & 6) that for every $n \ge 2$ and $0 \le k \le n - 2$, the ordinary Hermite matrix $H(P^{(k)}/\mathrm{lc}(P^{(k)}))$ is a sum-of-squares (SOS) matrix over $\mathbb{R}[x_1,\dots,x_n]$ (`conj:hermite`), and consequently the derivative discriminant $\mathcal{D}_k^{(n)} = \mathrm{disc}(P^{(k)})$ is a sum of squares of polynomials in the root differences $x_i - x_j$ (`conj:sos`). We proved both conjectures in full for all $n \ge 2$ and $0 \le k \le n - 2$ ($r = n - k \in [2, n]$) by conjugating the Hermite matrix via a unimodular Bezoutian congruence $H(J_{n,r}) = T(J_{n,r})^{-1} B(J_{n,r}) (T(J_{n,r})^{-1})^{\mathsf T}$ and expressing $B(J_{n,r}) = C(x)^{\mathsf T} \mathbf{W}_{n,r} C(x)$ in the subset polynomial basis $\{P_U\}_{|U|=r-1}$ with an explicit constant matrix $\mathbf{W}_{n,r} \in \mathbb{Q}^{N \times N}$. A Walsh–Hadamard rank-1 integral decomposition proves $\mathbf{W}_{n,r} \succ 0$ with positive rational weights, yielding an explicit positive-rational-weighted SOS-matrix factorization over $\mathbb{Q}[x_1,\dots,x_n]$ (and an $N$-row SOS matrix over $\mathbb{R}[x_1,\dots,x_n]$), and hence by centering and Cauchy–Binet an explicit SOS representation of $\mathcal{D}_k^{(n)}$ in $\mathbb{Q}[\{x_i - x_j\}]$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Cordian Riener, Boris Shapiro, *"Discriminants of derivatives: square graphs and sums of squares"*, `arXiv:2605.25743v3` (submitted `2026-05-25`, updated `2026-10-21`, `article.tex`, Section 1 Conjecture 1.1 `\label{conj:sos}` lines 123–126 and Section 6 Conjecture 6.5 `\label{conj:hermite}` lines 1983–1987).
  - Conjecture 1.1 (`conj:sos`): *"For every $n\ge 2$ and every $0\le k\le n-2$, the polynomial $\mathrm{D}_k^{(n)}$ is a sum of squares of polynomials in the differences $x_i-x_j$."*
  - Conjecture 6.5 (`conj:hermite`): *"For every $n\ge 2$ and $0\le k\le n-2$, the ordinary Hermite matrix of $P^{(k)}/\mathrm{lc}(P^{(k)})$ is an SOS matrix over $\mathbb{R}[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact symbolic verification of Lemma 1 ($2 \le n \le 6$), Lemma 2 ($r = 2..6$), Lemma 3 ($2 \le r \le n \le 6$), Lemma 4 ($2 \le r \le n \le 8$), and Theorem 1 & Corollary 2 symbolically for $2 \le r \le n \le 5$, full Cauchy–Binet $(6,2)$, and open cases up to $(9,4)$ in `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/riener-2605.25743-conjHermite/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:07Z`–`09:07:30Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `605` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` Sweep 23 and `434` discovered repo snapshots), live arXiv (`2605.25743v3` latest, OpenAlex `cited_by_count: 0`), `gh search repos`/`issues` (`trureturing#25877` is unrelated Breloer–Riener `2606.18895v2`), Zenodo API, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

### `oeis-A177259` — Proof of Mathar's 2022 D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture for OEIS `A177259`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in July 2022 on OEIS `A177259` that the count $a(n) = \sum_{s=0}^n \sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor(n-s)/3\rfloor} (-1)^{s+t} (n-2t)!/(s!\,t!)$ of derangements of $\{1,\dots,n\}$ with no adjacent $3$-cycles satisfies $a(n) = (n-1)a(n-1) + (n-1)a(n-2) + a(n-3) + (n-1)a(n-4) + 2a(n-6)$ for all $n \ge 6$. Reindexing the double sum by $k = n - 2t$ and $j = s + t$ collapses the ordinary generating function in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$ to $y(x) = \sum_{m=0}^\infty m!\, x^m / (1+x+x^3)^{m+1}$, so $(1+x+x^3)y(x) = F(x/(1+x+x^3))$ where $F(u) = \sum_{m=0}^\infty m!\, u^m$ satisfies $u^2 F'(u) + (u-1)F(u) + 1 = 0$. Differentiating and clearing denominators yields the first-order linear ODE $x^2(1+x+x^3)y'(x) + (-1+x^2+x^3+3x^4+2x^6)y(x) + (1-2x^3) = 0$, whose $[x^n]$ coefficient gives Mathar's recurrence for every $n \ge 6$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A177259` (revision `28`, `2025-11-05T15:22:18-05:00`, offset `0,5`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Number of derangements of {1,2,...,n} having no adjacent 3-cycles (an adjacent 3-cycle is a cycle of the form (i,i+1,i+2))."* / `a(n) = Sum_{s=0..n} Sum_{t=0..floor((n-s)/3)} (-1)^(s+t)*(n-2*t)!/(s!*t!).` / *"Conjecture: D-finite with recurrence a(n) = (n-1)*a(n-1) + (n-1)*a(n-2) + a(n-3) + (n-1)*a(n-4) + 2*a(n-6). - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 26 2022"*
- **Label**: `Lean-checked` (upgraded from `written proof, internally audited`; Lean 4 formalization [`lean/OpenMath/OeisA177259.lean`](lean/OpenMath/OeisA177259.lean), imported in [`lean/OpenMath.lean`](lean/OpenMath.lean) L15; `AUDIT.md` §5 statement-fidelity & kernel audit PASS by `@audit-1` proving `a177259_cast_eq`, `a177259_initial`, `mathar_conjecture_A177259_rat`, and `mathar_conjecture_A177259` for all `n ≥ 6` with standard axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`, zero `sorry`/warnings; supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact integer/`Fraction` verification in `notes/check-check-2.py`).

### `iverson-2506.20778-conjPaley` — Proof of Iverson–Mixon Asymmetric SIC Conjecture (`arXiv:2506.20778v1`, Conjecture 22 / `conj:Paley`)

- **Summary**: Joseph W. Iverson and Dustin G. Mixon conjectured in `arXiv:2506.20778v1` (Section 4, Conjecture 22 `\label{conj:Paley}`) that for the Paley type I Hadamard matrix $H$ of order $d = q + 1$ ($q > 11$, $q \equiv 3 \bmod 4$), the graphical Hadamard matrix $\tilde{H}$ satisfies $\mathrm{Aut}_s(\tilde{H}) \cong P\Gamma L(2, q)$ with exactly two orbits on $[d]\times[d]$ (diagonal $\Delta$ and its complement), and that for every prime $p \equiv 3 \bmod 4$ dividing $d - 8$, the Theorem 2 SIC $\{x_{ij}\}$ in $\mathbb{F}_{p^2}^d$ has $\mathrm{Aut}_s(\{x_{ij}\}) = \iota(\mathrm{Aut}_w(H))$ and $\mathrm{Aut}_w(\{x_{ij}\}) = \mathrm{Aut}_s(\tilde{H})$, yielding infinitely many non-transitive SICs over finite fields. For Part (a), combining Hasse's elliptic-curve bound $|\langle v_{ij}, v_{uv} \rangle| \le 2\sqrt{q} + 4 < d - 4$ on $\Delta$-signature inner products with a $d$-clique permutation-graph characterization (and finite elimination of $q = 19$ over $17$ square-scaling orbits) proves $\mathrm{Aut}_s(\tilde{H})$ preserves $\Delta$, reducing strong automorphisms via Kantor's theorem ($\mathrm{Aut}_w(H) \cong P\Sigma L(2, q)$) to $\psi(P\Gamma L(2, q))$. For Part (b), an explicit coordinate-swap/Galois element $\pi = \psi(g_0)$ ($g_0 \in P\Gamma L(2, q) \setminus P\Sigma L(2, q)$) induces a weak anti-unitary symmetry $\bigl(x_{\pi(i,j)}, x_{\pi(k,l)}\bigr) = \omega_{i,j}\omega_{k,l}\overline{(x_{i,j}, x_{k,l})}$ (so $\mathrm{Aut}_w(\{x_{ij}\}) = \mathrm{Aut}_s(\tilde{H})$), yet fails strong equivalence because triple inner products evaluate to both $64 \in \mathbb{F}_p^\times$ and $\pm 64\mathrm{i}$, forcing $\mathrm{Aut}_s(\{x_{ij}\}) = \iota(\mathrm{Aut}_w(H))$ (with Dirichlet's theorem and $q = 7^r$, $r \ge 3$ odd supplying infinitely many non-transitive pairs $(q, p)$).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Joseph W. Iverson and Dustin G. Mixon, *"Asymmetric SICs over finite fields"*, `arXiv:2506.20778v1` (`2025-06-25`, sole version, `2025.06.25.tex`, Section 4 Conjecture 22 `\label{conj:Paley}`, lines 829–840).
  - Statement: *"Given a prime power $q > 11$ with $q \equiv 3 \bmod 4$, consider the ``Paley I'' Hadamard matrix $H$ of order $d:=q+1$. Then the following hold: **(a)** $\mathrm{Aut}_s(\tilde{H}) \cong P\Gamma L(2,q)$ has exactly two orbits on $[d] \times [d]$, namely, $\{(i,i) : i \in [d]\}$ and its complement, and **(b)** for any prime $p \equiv 3 \bmod 4$ that divides $d-8$, the SIC $\{x_{ij}\}$ in $\mathbb{F}_{p^2}^d$ constructed by Theorem~\ref{thm.main} has $\mathrm{Aut}_s(\{x_{ij}\}) = \iota(\mathrm{Aut}_w(H))$ and $\mathrm{Aut}_w(\{x_{ij}\}) = \mathrm{Aut}_s(\tilde{H})$. In particular, there exist non-transitive SICs in infinitely many dimensions over various finite fields."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` re-audit PASS, and exact finite-field/integer verification of Lemma 1 for $q \in \{19, 23, 27, 31\}$, Lemma 2 & Lemma 4 exhaustive $q=19$ square-scaling orbits and $190$ column-pair eliminations, and Theorem 2 SIC Gram/span/automorphism separation for $(q,p) \in \{(19,3), (31,3), (83,19), (343,7)\}$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/iverson-2506.20778-conjPaley/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:16Z`–`09:18Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` `08:49Z` rebuild and `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`), live arXiv (`2506.20778v1` sole version, OpenAlex `cited_by_count: 0` in `watch-5`), Zenodo API, and web search; 0 prior proofs found.
- **What is not established**:
  - Depends on Kantor (1969) for $\mathrm{Aut}_w(H) \cong P\Sigma L(2, q)$ ($q > 11, q \equiv 3 \bmod 4$) and Hasse's elliptic-curve bound over $\mathbb{F}_q$.
  - Live `gh search issues/prs` (`HTTP 403`) and OpenAlex (`HTTP 429` in `watch-1`, succeeded in `watch-5`) had rate limits during Gate 3; closed-access venues and private repositories are not covered. Internal adversarial audit and finite exact checks are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

Reindexing the double sum by $k = n - 3t$ and $j = s + t$ collapses the ordinary generating function in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$ to $y(x) = \sum_{m=0}^\infty m!\, x^m / (1+x+x^4)^{m+1}$, so $(1+x+x^4)y(x) = F(x/(1+x+x^4))$ where $F(u) = \sum_{m=0}^\infty m!\, u^m$ satisfies $u^2 F'(u) + (u-1)F(u) + 1 = 0$. ### `benfield-2407.20048-conj5.3` — Refutation of Benfield–Lippard Conjecture 5.3, Clause (vi) (`arXiv:2407.20048v2`)

- **Summary**: Brennan Benfield and Oliver Lippard conjectured in `arXiv:2407.20048v2` (Section 5, Conjecture 5.3 `\label{finite-orders}`) a six-part classification of the range of the Pisano zero-order $\omega_{(a,b)}(m)$ ($m > 1$) for $(a,b)$-Fibonacci sequences ($U_0 = 0, U_1 = 1, U_n = a U_{n-1} + b U_{n-2}$), with clause (vi) claiming the range is *"infinite in all other cases"* outside clauses (i)–(v). **Scope & Prior Credit (`CONTESTED.md`, `notes/contest-ruling-audit-1.md`):** Clause (v) ($b \neq \pm 1, |a|-|b|=1 \implies \{0,1,2\}$) was previously refuted by `the-omega-institute/trureturing#9448` / PR `#9451` (`Problems/pisano-order-range-refutation.md`, merged `2026-09-22`) at $(a,b) = (3,2), m = 13$ ($\omega_{(3,2)}(13) = 3 \notin \{0,1,2\}$), which explicitly left clause (vi) open; this result claims **zero novelty on clause (v)** and is rescoped strictly to **clause (vi)**. Because $(a,b) = (3,4)$ satisfies $b = 4 \notin \{0,\pm 1\}$ and $|3| - |4| = -1 \neq 1$, it falls strictly under clause (vi), yet $U_n = (4^n - (-1)^n)/5$ proves $\omega_{(3,4)}(m) = 0$ for all even $m > 1$ and $\omega_{(3,4)}(m) \in \{1, 2\}$ for all odd $m > 1$ (specifically $1$ when $\mathrm{ord}_{5m}(-4)$ is even and $2$ when odd), giving exact finite range $\{\omega_{(3,4)}(m) : m > 1\} = \{0, 1, 2\}$ and refuting clause (vi).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Brennan Benfield and Oliver Lippard, *"Connecting Zeros in Pisano Periods to Prime Factors of $K$-Fibonacci Numbers"*, `arXiv:2407.20048v2` (`2025-01-30`, `fiborder.tex`, Section 5 *"Final Remarks"*, lines 692–696, 724, and Conjecture 5.3 `\label{finite-orders}` lines 729–741, rescoped strictly to clause (vi)).
  - Statement (Conjecture 5.3): *"For an $(a,b)$-Fibonacci sequence, the range of $\omega_{(a,b)}(m)$ for $m>1$ is given by (i) $\{1\}$ if ...; (ii) $\{2\}$ if ...; (iii) $\{1,2,4\}$ if $b=1$ and $a\neq 0$; (iv) $\{1,2\}$ if $b=-1$ and $a\not\in\{-2,-1,0,1,2\}$; (v) $\{0,1,2\}$ if $b \neq \pm 1$ and $|a|-|b| = 1$; **(vi) infinite in all other cases.**"*
- **Label**: `counterexample, verified by exact computation` (supported by `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, exact integer modular verification of $U_n = (4^n - (-1)^n)/5$ for $0 \le n \le 60$ and exhaustive modular period/order computation for $m = 2..200$ showing exact range $\{0,1,2\}$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`, plus universal proof for all $m > 1$).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/benfield-2407.20048-conj5.3/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS for rescoped clause (vi) (`2026-11-27T09:17Z`–`09:18Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` `08:49Z` rebuild and `the-omega-institute/trureturing`), live GitHub issues/PRs on `trureturing` (`#9448` / PR `#9451` settles clause (v) only; `#23317` is on Question 4.34), live arXiv (`2407.20048v2` latest), OpenAlex/S2 citations, and Zenodo API (`0` hits); 0 prior refutations of clause (vi) found.
- **What is not established**:
  - **Prior credit & rescoped boundary:** Clause (v) was already refuted in `the-omega-institute/trureturing#9448` / PR `#9451` (`2026-09-22`); only clause (vi) is claimed here.
  - **Empirical observation in `fiborder.tex` L724 (`AUDIT.md` §1 transparency note):** In the informal paragraph immediately preceding Conjecture 5.3 (`fiborder.tex` L724), the authors wrote empirically that *"the order of the $(3,4)$-Fibonacci sequence modulo $m$ is always 0, 1, or 2 for $m < 20000$"* without proving it for all $m$, and then formulated clause (v) with `$b \neq \pm 1$ and $|a|-|b| = 1$`. Because $|3| - |4| = -1 \neq 1$, $(3,4)$ falls strictly inside clause (vi) of Conjecture 5.3 as published; `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md` proves $\omega_{(3,4)}(m) \in \{0,1,2\}$ for **all** $m > 1$ (closing the unproved empirical gap as well as refuting verbatim clause (vi)).
  - Journal version *The Fibonacci Quarterly* 63(2) (2025), 240–258 (`doi:10.1080/00150517.2025.2460555`) was paywalled (`HTTP 403`) and unread beyond `arXiv:2407.20048v2`; closed-access venues and private repositories are not covered. Internal audit and finite exact checks ($m \le 200$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `navarra-2511.22346-conj2.3` — Counterexample to Navarra–Qureshi–Rinaldo Conjecture 2.3 (`arXiv:2511.22346v1`)

- **Summary**: Francesco Navarra, Ayesha Asloob Qureshi, and Giancarlo Rinaldo conjectured in `arXiv:2511.22346v1` (Section 2, Conjecture 2.3 `\label{conj}`) that for every collection of cells $\mathcal{P}$, the switching rook polynomial $\tilde{r}_{\mathcal{P}}(t)$ equals the $h$-polynomial of the inner $2$-minor coordinate ring $K[\mathcal{P}]$ and the rook number $r(\mathcal{P})$ equals $\mathrm{reg}(K[\mathcal{P}])$, after reporting in Theorem 2.2 that all collections of rank $|\mathcal{P}| \le 10$ satisfy the conjecture. We constructed an explicit weakly connected $10$-cell collection $\mathcal{P} \subset \{1,2,3,4\}^2$ (formed by removing the four corners and $(2,2),(3,3)$, with $|V(\mathcal{P})| = 21$ and $18$ inner intervals) whose inner $2$-minor ideal $I_{\mathcal{P}}$ lies inside the monomial prime ideal $\mathfrak{p} = (x_v : v \in \{2,3,4\}^2)$. Consequently $\dim K[\mathcal{P}] \ge 21 - 9 = 12$ over every field $K$, forcing the linear $h$-coefficient $h_1 = 21 - \dim K[\mathcal{P}] \le 9 < 10 = \tilde{r}_1(\mathcal{P})$ (with exact Hilbert $h$-polynomial $1 + 9t + 27t^2 + 25t^3 - 16t^4 - 33t^5 - 11t^6 - t^7 \neq 1 + 10t + 37t^2 + 62t^3 + 46t^4 + 12t^5 + t^6 = \tilde{r}_{\mathcal{P}}(t)$), refuting both Conjecture 2.3 and Theorem 2.2's rank $\le 10$ claim over every field $K$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Francesco Navarra, Ayesha Asloob Qureshi, Giancarlo Rinaldo, *"Switching rook polynomial of collections of cells"*, `arXiv:2511.22346v1` (`2025-11-27`, `HILBERT_Series_NEW.tex`, Section 2, Conjecture 2.3 `\label{conj}`, lines 645–647; Theorem 2.2, lines 525–531).
  - Statement: *"Let $\mathcal{P}$ be a collection of cells. Then the switching rook polynomial of $\mathcal{P}$ coincides with the $h$-polynomial of $K[\mathcal{P}]$, and the rook number of $\mathcal{P}$ equals the regularity of $K[\mathcal{P}]$."*
- **Label**: `counterexample, verified by exact computation` (supported by `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, exact `sympy` reduced grevlex Gröbner basis ($46$ binomials with coefficients in $\{\pm 1\}$, hence field-independent), exact Hilbert series $\dim K[\mathcal{P}] = 12$ and $h_{K[\mathcal{P}]}(t)$, and $\tilde{r}_{\mathcal{P}}(t)$ verification in `notes/check-check-1.py`, plus `audit-3` cross-check in `grlex`/`grevlex` over $\mathbb{Q}, \mathbb{F}_2, \mathbb{F}_3$).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/navarra-2511.22346-conj2.3/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:22:38Z`–`09:23Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z` and `trureturing`), live `gh search repos`/`issues` (`[]`), live arXiv (`2511.22346v1` sole version), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior refutations found.

### `garrison-2401.09497-conj5.2` — Proof of Garrison–Seiler–Knowles Conjecture 5.2 (`arXiv:2401.09497v1`, `conj:1 at end`)

- **Summary**: Thomas Garrison, Chris Seiler, and Andrew Knowles conjectured in `arXiv:2401.09497v1` (Section 5, Conjecture 5.2 `\label{conj:1 at end}`) that every weakly consecutive sequence (WCS)—a permutation $\sigma : [k] \to [k]$ such that $m \mid \sigma(i)$ and $m \mid (i - j)$ imply $m \mid \sigma(j)$—must begin or end with $1$ ($\sigma(1) = 1$ or $\sigma(k) = 1$). Rewriting any WCS via its CRT GCD representation $\sigma(i) = \gcd(a + i, \mathrm{lcm}(1,\dots,k))$ (Lemma 3) and reversal symmetry (Lemma 1), we closed both parities completely: for even $k \ge 4$, fixing $c_2 = 2$ forces $\sigma(k/2+1) = k/2+1$ so any $\sigma(1) > 1$ divides both $k/2$ and $k/2 + 1$; for odd $k \ge 3$ with $\sigma(1), \sigma(k) > 1$, normalizing $3 \le A := \sigma(1) < B := \sigma(k)$ coprime divisors of $k = ABR$ pins $\sigma((k-A)/2+1) = (k+A)/2$, forces $BR + 1 = 2^s u$ ($s \ge 2$, $u$ odd) with $a \equiv A - 1 + 2^{s-1} \pmod{2^s}$, and produces $u$ distinct multiples of $2^s A$ inside $[k]$ via $i_t = (2^{s-1}-1)A + 1 + t\cdot 2^s A$, contradicting $k = A(2^s u - 1) < u \cdot 2^s A$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Thomas Garrison, Chris Seiler, Andrew Knowles, *"Weakly Consecutive Sequences"*, `arXiv:2401.09497v1` (`2024-01-17`, `WCS.tex`, Definition 1.1 `\label{def:weak_consec}` lines 83–85 and Section 5 Conjecture 5.2 `\label{conj:1 at end}` lines 718–720).
  - Statement: *"Any WCS either begins or ends with the term $1$ (in other words, $\sigma(1) = 1$ or $\sigma(k) = 1$)."*
- **Label**: `machine-checked proof in Lean 4` (upgraded from `written proof, internally audited`; Lean 4 formalization [`lean/OpenMath/Garrison240109497Conj52.lean`](lean/OpenMath/Garrison240109497Conj52.lean), imported in [`lean/OpenMath.lean`](lean/OpenMath.lean) L20; `AUDIT.md` §5 statement-fidelity & kernel audit PASS by `@audit-2` proving `wcs_residue_class`, `wcs_gcd_repr`, `garrison_conjecture_5_2`, and `garrison_conjecture_5_2_perm` for all `k ≥ 1` with standard axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`, zero `sorry`/warnings; supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact pure-integer verification of exhaustive permutations for $k = 1..8$, exhaustive $0 \le a < L_k$ GCD search for $k = 3..12$, and Case 2 Steps 2–4 congruences/index bounds/pigeonhole inequality across all $46$ admissible odd composite triples $(A, B, R)$ with $k = ABR \le 150$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake env lean OpenMath/Garrison240109497Conj52.lean` (and `.venv/bin/python targets/garrison-2401.09497-conj5.2/notes/check-check-1.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:23Z`–`09:23:25Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live `gh search repos`/`issues` & `gh api` (`rainwangphy/aftd-platform` proves prime-power $k$ only; `ndesmo/leaning` proves $k \le 16$ / prime-power $k$ only, with `#21`/`#68` open and `0` open PRs), live arXiv (`2401.09497v1` sole version), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 general-$k$ proofs found.

### `hare-2603.19554-conj3.3` — Proof of Hare–Hichri Conjecture 3.3 (`arXiv:2603.19554v1`, `conj:1`)

- **Summary**: Kevin G. Hare and Hachem Hichri conjectured in `arXiv:2603.19554v1` (Section 3.1, Conjecture 3.3 `\label{conj:1}`) that for all $d \ge 4$ and all $k \ge 1$, the $k$-th power $\beta_d^k$ of the Perron root $\beta_d > 1$ of $P_d(x) = x^d - x - 1$ is non-Parry. For every $k$ above the Solomyak cutoff $|\gamma_d|^k > \theta_0 = (1+\sqrt{5})/2$ (where $\gamma_d$ is a conjugate root of $P_d$ with $|\gamma_d| > 1$), $\beta_d^k$ is non-Parry by Solomyak's theorem; for smaller $k$, we established a closed-form two-digit greedy block criterion showing $|\sigma(T_q^{2m_0-2}(1))| > \lfloor q\rfloor / (|\gamma_d^k| - 1)$ via Akiyama's divergence criterion. Combining pure integer fixed-point Rouché/interval certification for all $496$ degrees $4 \le d \le 499$ ($496$ Solomyak cutoffs, $86{,}205$ closed-form blocks, and $10$ explicit greedy orbits for $d = 4, 5$) with a uniform Rouché localization of $\gamma_d$ around $2^{1/d} e^{2\pi i/d}$ and analytic Cases A ($0 < k/d \le 0.5$) and B ($0.5 < k/d \le 0.705$) for all $d \ge 500$ proves the conjecture for every $d \ge 4$ and $k \ge 1$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Kevin G. Hare and Hachem Hichri, *"The Parry Order of Perron Numbers"*, `arXiv:2603.19554v1` (`2026-03-20`, `v6.tex`, Section 3.1 `sec:beta, theta`, lines 489–493, Conjecture 3.3 `\label{conj:1}`).
  - Statement: *"For all $d \geq 4$ and all $k \geq 1$ we have $\beta_d^k$ is non-Parry."* (where $\beta_d$ is the Perron number of minimal polynomial $x^d - x - 1 = 0$).
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and `notes/check-check-3.py` zero-float 140-bit fixed-point interval certification of all $496$ degrees $4 \le d \le 499$ plus `mpmath.iv` (`dps=50`) verification of all Section 3 ($d \ge 500$) inequalities and full-$k$ spot checks at $d \in \{500, 501, 600, 1000\}$).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/hare-2603.19554-conj3.3/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:22:38Z`–`09:23Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live `gh search repos`/`issues` (`[]`), live arXiv (`2603.19554v1` sole version), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 all-$d$ proofs found.

### `lau-2208.14707-conj2` — Counterexample to Lau–Shiu–Premalatha–Zhang–Nalliah Conjecture 2.2 (`arXiv:2208.14707v1` / *DML* 11 (2023))

- **Summary**: Gee-Choon Lau, Wai-Chee Shiu, K. Premalatha, Ruixue Zhang, and M. Nalliah conjectured in `arXiv:2208.14707v1` (Section 2, p. 8; published in *Discrete Mathematics Letters* 11 (2023), p. 82, Conjecture 2.2) that for $k \ge 1$ and graphs $G, H$ (with $2k+1$ the length of a shortest odd cycle in $G$), the lexicographic product satisfies $\chi_{la}(G[H]) = \chi(G)\chi(H)$ if and only if $\chi(G)\chi(H) = 2\chi(H) + \lceil \chi(H)/k \rceil$. Taking the disjoint connected simple graphs $(G, H) = (K_4, K_2)$ gives $k = 1$, $\chi(K_4) = 4$, $\chi(K_2) = 2$, and $K_4[K_2] \cong K_8$, for which an explicit lexicographic edge bijection $f : E(K_8) \to \{1, \dots, 28\}$ produces pairwise distinct vertex sums $(28, 64, 90, 108, 120, 128, 134, 140)$ so $\chi_{la}(K_4[K_2]) = 8 = \chi(G)\chi(H)$. However, $2\chi(H) + \lceil \chi(H)/k \rceil = 4 + 2 = 6 \ne 8$, directly refuting the "only if" direction of Conjecture 2.2 (and extending similarly to $(K_m, K_n)$ for all $m \ge 4, n \ge 2$).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Gee-Choon Lau, Wai-Chee Shiu, K. Premalatha, Ruixue Zhang, M. Nalliah, *"A note on local antimagic chromatic number of lexicographic product graphs"*, `arXiv:2208.14707v1` (`2022-08-31`, Section 2, p. 8, line 819) and *Discrete Mathematics Letters* 11 (2023), pp. 76–83 (`DOI:10.47443/dml.2022.149`, p. 82), **Conjecture 2.2**.
  - Statement: *"For $k\ge 1$ and graph $G$ and $H$, $\chi_{la}(G[H]) = \chi(G)\chi(H)$ if and only if $\chi(G)\chi(H) = 2\chi(H) + \lceil\frac{\chi(H)}{k}\rceil$, where $2k+1$ is the length of a shortest odd cycle in $G$."*
- **Label**: `machine-checked counterexample in Lean 4` (upgraded from `counterexample, verified by exact computation`; Lean 4 formalization [`lean/OpenMath/Lau220814707Conj22.lean`](lean/OpenMath/Lau220814707Conj22.lean), imported in [`lean/OpenMath.lean`](lean/OpenMath.lean) L18; `AUDIT.md` §5 statement-fidelity & kernel audit PASS by `@audit-2` proving `lexLabelK8_vertexSums`, `refutes_lau_conjecture_2_2_K4_K2`, `refutes_lau_conjecture_2_2_infinite_family`, and `not_lau_conjecture_2_2` with standard axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`, zero `sorry`/warnings; supported by `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`, `AUDIT.md` re-audit PASS, and pure-integer graph/chromatic/bijection/vertex-sum verification of $(G, H) = (K_4, K_2)$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake env lean OpenMath/Lau220814707Conj22.lean` (and `.venv/bin/python targets/lau-2208.14707-conj2/notes/check-check-2.py`)
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:23:25Z`–`09:24Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live `gh search repos`/`issues` (`[]`, plus `gh search code` `[]` at `09:14Z`), live arXiv (`2208.14707v1` sole version), OpenAlex on `10.47443/dml.2022.149` (`cited_by_count: 0`), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior refutations found.

### `oeis-A045743` — Proof of Mathar's 2022 D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture for OEIS `A045743`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in July 2022 on OEIS `A045743` that the count $a(n) = \frac{1}{n-1} \sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor(n-2)/2\rfloor} \binom{n-2+i}{i}\binom{3n-3-i}{n-2-2i}$ of noncrossing connected graphs on $n$ nodes on a circle with no triangular faces satisfies a five-term fourth-order polynomial recurrence $\sum_{j=0}^4 C_j(n) a(n-j) = 0$ for all $n \ge 6$. Using Lagrange inversion on $\Phi(t) = (1+t)^4 / (1+t-t^2)$, the generating function $y(x) = \sum_{n \ge 2} a(n)x^n \in x^2\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$ satisfies the irreducible quartic equation $P(x, y(x)) = 0$ ($P(x,y) = x^4 + 4x^3y + 6x^2y^2 - x^2y + 4xy^3 - xy^2 + y^4 + y^3$). Expressing $P_y^{2k-1} y^{(k)} = N_k(x, y(x))$ in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$ and verifying by exact polynomial division in $\mathbb{Z}[x, y]$ that $P(x, y)$ divides the cleared fourth-order linear ODE numerator yields $p_{\mathrm{inhom}}(x) + \sum_{k=0}^4 p_k(x) y^{(k)}(x) = 0$ with $\deg p_{\mathrm{inhom}} = 5$, whose $[x^n]$ coefficient for $n \ge 6$ matches $C_0(n), \dots, C_4(n)$ identically in $\mathbb{Z}[n]$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A045743` (revision `14`, `2022-07-26T14:31:19-04:00`, offset `2,2`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Number of noncrossing connected graphs on n nodes on a circle having no triangular faces."* / `a(n) = Sum_{i=0..floor((n-2)/2)} binomial(n-2+i, i)*binomial(3*n-3-i, n-2-2*i)/(n-1).` / *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence 5*n*(n-1)*(n-2)*(1353818*n-3651663)*a(n) +16*(n-1)*(n-2)*(2236406*n^2-6864025*n+2014500)*a(n-1) -16*(n-2)*(46338048*n^3-326486432*n^2+734160494*n-506411475)*a(n-2) +8*(2*n-7)*(26592864*n^3-436441232*n^2+2071128458*n-3030735075)*a(n-3) +128*(94246*n-334675)*(4*n-19)*(2*n-9)*(4*n-17)*a(n-4)=0."* (_R. J. Mathar_, Jul 26 2022)
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` re-audit PASS, and exact integer/`sympy` $\mathbb{Z}[x,y]$ verification of OEIS data $n=2..22$, Lagrange inversion & $P(x,y(x))=0$ to $O(x^{32})$, exact division $P(x,y) \mid Q(x,y)$ in $\mathbb{Z}[x,y]$, exact equality of extracted coefficients with $C_0..C_4$ in $\mathbb{Z}[n]$, and recurrence for $6 \le n \le 50$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A045743/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:23:52Z`–`09:24:31Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live OEIS `A045743` rev. `14` (still marked `Conjecture`), live `gh search issues`/`repos` (`[]`), live arXiv (`0` hits), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

### `oeis-A108449` — Proof of Mathar's 2022 D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture for OEIS `A108449`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in July 2022 on OEIS `A108449` that the count $a(n)$ of first-quadrant lattice paths from $(0,0)$ to $(3n,0)$ using steps $u=(2,1)$, $U=(1,2)$, and $d=(1,-1)$ with no pyramids ($u^p d^p$ or $U^p d^{2p}$ starting at the $x$-axis) satisfies a nine-term order-8 polynomial recurrence $\sum_{j=0}^8 C_j(n) a(n-j) = 0$ for all $n \ge 8$. Primitive-excursion decomposition gives $A(x) = 1 + xA(x)^2 + xA(x)^3$ for unrestricted paths and $y(x) = (1 - x) / (1 + x - x(1-x)A(x)(1+A(x)))$ after excluding pyramids; taking the $5 \times 5$ Sylvester resultant with respect to $A$ isolates an irreducible cubic algebraic equation $P(x, y(x)) = 0$ in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$. Expressing $y^{(k)}(x) = N_k(x, y(x))/P_y(x, y(x))^{2k-1}$ ($k = 1, 2, 3$) and verifying by exact polynomial division in $\mathbb{Z}[x, y]$ that $P(x, y)$ divides the cleared third-order linear ODE numerator gives $p_{\mathrm{inhom}}(x) + \sum_{k=0}^3 p_k(x) y^{(k)}(x) = 0$ with $\deg p_{\mathrm{inhom}} = 7$, whose $[x^n]$ coefficient for $n \ge 8$ matches $C_0(n), \dots, C_8(n)$ identically in $\mathbb{Z}[n]$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A108449` (revision `13`, `2025-11-05T15:35:31-05:00`, offset `0,3`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Number of paths from (0,0) to (3n,0) that stay in the first quadrant (but may touch the horizontal axis), consisting of steps u=(2,1),U=(1,2), or d=(1,-1) and having no pyramids (a pyramid is a sequence u^pd^p or U^pd^(2p) for some positive integer p, starting at the x-axis)."* / *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence +n*(2*n+1)*(431*n-2895)*a(n) +2*(-9395*n^3+68622*n^2-64084*n+26109)*a(n-1) +2*(59288*n^3-508196*n^2+1044822*n-574587)*a(n-2) +2*(-94965*n^3+1070605*n^2-3607435*n+3485484)*a(n-3) +4*(29036*n^3-351474*n^2+1402336*n-1970505)*a(n-4) +6*(-6703*n^3+63052*n^2-99178*n-237177)*a(n-5) +6*(1012*n^3-14914*n^2+74580*n-127341)*a(n-6) +6*(1127*n^3-21429*n^2+135199*n-282762 )*a(n-7) +9*(29*n-165)*(2*n-15)*(n-8)*a(n-8)=0."* (_R. J. Mathar_, Jul 26 2022)

### `oeis-A168452` — Proof of Mathar's 2024 D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture for OEIS `A168452`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in November 2024 on OEIS `A168452` that the self-convolution $a(n) = \sum_{k=0}^n b(k) b(n-k)$ of $b(k) = C_k C_{k+1}$ (where $C_k = \frac{1}{k+1}\binom{2k}{k}$ is the $k$-th Catalan number) satisfies a five-term order-4 polynomial recurrence $\sum_{j=0}^4 C_j(n) a(n-j) = 0$ with degree-5 coefficients $C_j(n)$ for all $n \ge 4$. From the hypergeometric ratio $(n+1)(n+2)b(n) = 4(2n-1)(2n+1)b(n-1)$, the generating function $B(x) = \sum_{n \ge 0} b(n)x^n$ satisfies a second-order linear ODE $\delta(x)B''(x) = -2 + 2(1-6x)B(x) + 4x(1-12x)B'(x)$ with $\delta(x) = x^2(16x-1)$. Inductively expressing $D_k(x) y^{(k)}(x) = M_k(x, B(x), B'(x))$ ($0 \le k \le 5$) for $y(x) = B(x)^2$ in $\mathbb{Z}[x, B, B']$ yields an exact order-5 linear ODE $\sum_{k=0}^5 p_k(x) y^{(k)}(x) = 72 - 1152x$ in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$, whose $[x^n]$ coefficient for $n \ge 4$ matches $C_0(n), \dots, C_4(n)$ identically in $\mathbb{Z}[n]$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A168452` (revision `14`, `2024-11-22T06:23:47-05:00`, offset `0,2`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Self-convolution of A005568, where A005568(n) is the product of two successive Catalan numbers C(n)*C(n+1)."* / *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence 3*(n+4)*(n+3)*(n+2)*(n+1)^2*a(n) -4*n*(n+1) *(32*n^3+164*n^2+233*n+75)*a(n-1) +96*(16*n^5+24*n^4-14*n^3-28*n^2-16*n+3) *a(n-2) +1536*(-8*n^4+22*n^3-32*n+15)*a(n-3) -16384*(2*n-5)*(n-1)*(n-2)*(n-3)*(2*n-3)*a(n-4)=0."* (_R. J. Mathar_, Nov 22 2024)

### `oeis-A119366` — Proof of Mathar's 2025 D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture for OEIS `A119366`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in February 2025 on OEIS `A119366` that the count $a(0) = 0$, $a(n) = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor n/3 \rfloor}\binom{n}{3k+1}\binom{n}{3k}$ ($n \ge 1$) of rooted planar $n$-trees with $1 \pmod 3$ leaves satisfies an eight-term order-7 polynomial recurrence $\sum_{j=0}^7 C_j(n) a(n-j) = 0$ with degree-4 coefficients $C_j(n)$ for all $n \ge 7$. Applying cube-root-of-unity multisection ($\omega^3 = 1, \omega^2+\omega+1=0$) and Lagrange inversion to $P_j(u) = (u+1)(u+\omega^j)$ expresses the generating function in $\mathbb{Q}[\omega]((x))$ as $y(x) = -\frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{6}(S_0(x)/x + \omega^2 S_1(x)/x + \omega S_2(x)/x)$, where $S_j(x) = \sqrt{\Delta_j(x)}$ ($\Delta_0 = 1-4x$, $\Delta_1 = 1 + 2\omega^2 x - 3\omega x^2$, $\Delta_2 = 1 + 2\omega x - 3\omega^2 x^2$). Constructing an explicit order-4 linear differential operator $L = \sum_{k=0}^4 p_k(x) \frac{d^k}{dx^k}$ with $p_k \in \mathbb{Z}[x]$ that annihilates $S_0/x$ in $\mathbb{Z}[x]$ and $S_1/x$ in $\mathbb{Z}[\omega][x]$ (hence $S_2/x$ via $\omega \mapsto \omega^2$) yields $L(y(x)) = -\frac{1}{2}p_0(x)$ of degree $5$, whose $[x^n]$ coefficient for $n \ge 7$ matches $C_0(n), \dots, C_7(n)$ identically in $\mathbb{Z}[n]$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A119366` (revision `7`, `2025-02-03T09:34:08-05:00`, offset `0,5`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Number of rooted planar n-trees whose number of leaves is equal to 1 modulo 3."* / `a(0)=0, a(n)=sum{k=0..floor(n/3), (1/n)*C(n,3k+1)C(n,3k)},n>0;` / *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence +n*(881*n-4580)*(n-2)*(n+1)*a(n) -3*n*(612*n^3-2827*n^2-2988*n+10135)*a(n-1) +3*(-3088*n^4+42803*n^3-190361*n^2+313702*n-167988)*a(n-2) +(43042*n^4-600920*n^3+2924411*n^2-5860777*n+4115562)*a(n-3) +3*(-38600*n^4+558681*n^3-2904370*n^2+6389913*n-4965528)*a(n-4) +3*(-14776*n^4+162695*n^3-434711*n^2-415064*n+1878084)*a(n-5) -9*(n-6)*(10835*n^3-106831*n^2+290611*n-173519)*a(n-6) +54*(n-6)*(n-7)*(593*n-1429)*(2*n-13)*a(n-7)=0."* (_R. J. Mathar_, Feb 03 2025)
