### `benfield-2407.20048-conj4.33` — Proof of Benfield–Lippard Conjecture 4.33 (`arXiv:2407.20048v2`, `even-K-exceptions`)

- **Summary**: Brennan Benfield and Oliver Lippard conjectured in `arXiv:2407.20048v2` (Section 4.2, Conjecture 4.33 `\label{even-K-exceptions}`) that for every even integer $K$ and modulus $m > 3$, the $K$-Fibonacci sequence ($F_{K,0}=0, F_{K,1}=1, F_{K,n}=KF_{K,n-1}+F_{K,n-2}$) satisfies three properties connecting Pisano period $\pi_K(m)$, rank $\alpha_K(m)$, and order $\omega_K(m)$: (1) $\omega_K(m) = 4 \iff \alpha_K(m) \equiv \pm 1 \pmod 4$; (2) $\omega_K(m) = 2 \implies 4 \mid \pi_K(m)$ and $2 \mid \alpha_K(m)$; and (3) $4 \nmid \pi_K(m) \implies \omega_K(m) = 1$. Writing $m = 2^x n$ ($x \ge 0$, $n \ge 1$ odd) and proving cleanly that $\omega_K(n)$ satisfies all three assertions for odd $n \ge 3$ (via $U^{\alpha} \equiv \beta I \pmod n$ with $\beta^2 \equiv (-1)^\alpha$) while $\omega_K(2^x) = 1$ and $\pi_K(2^x) = \alpha_K(2^x) = 2^a$ ($a \ge 1$) for even $K$ (via $F_{K,2r} = F_{K,r} L_{K,r}$ and $\nu_2(L_{K,2s}) = 1$), the $\mathrm{lcm}$ identities yield $\omega_K(m) = 2^{\max(a, v + \nu_2(\omega_K(n))) - \max(a, v)}$ ($v = \nu_2(\alpha_K(n))$), establishing all three claims exhaustively across all seven cases for all even $K \in \mathbb{Z}$ and $m > 3$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Brennan Benfield and Oliver Lippard, *"Connecting Zeros in Pisano Periods to Prime Factors of $K$-Fibonacci Numbers"*, `arXiv:2407.20048v2` (`2025-01-30`, `fiborder.tex`, Section 4 lines 273 & 306–310, Section 4.2 Conjecture 4.33 `\label{even-K-exceptions}` lines 675–682).
  - Statement: *"The following portions of Theorem \ref{omega-odd-K} also apply to even $K$ for all moduli $m > 3$: (1) $\omega_K(m)=4$ if and only if $\alpha_K(m)\equiv\pm1\pmod{4}$; (2) If $\omega_K(m)=2$, then $4\mid\pi_K(m)$ and $2\mid\alpha_K(m)$; (3) If $4\not\mid\pi_K(m)$, then $\omega_K(m)=1$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` re-audit PASS, and exact integer modular verification of Lemmas 1–3 and all $7$ proof subcases across $6{,}422$ even-$(K, m)$ pairs with $K \in \{-10, -8, \dots, 40\}$ and $4 \le m \le 250$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/benfield-2407.20048-conj4.33/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:28:57Z`–`09:29Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z` and `the-omega-institute/trureturing*`), live `gh search repos`/`issues` & `gh api` (`trureturing#23317` unchanged open unproved preregistration on Q4.34 explicitly excluding Conjecture 4.33; `trureturing#9448` is Conjecture 5.3(v)), live arXiv (`2407.20048v2` latest), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

### `ballantine-2408.13346-e2p4-rec` — Proof of Ballantine–Beck–Merca Conjectured Order-22 Linear Recurrence for $e_2p_4(n)$ (`arXiv:2408.13346v1`)

- **Summary**: Cristina Ballantine, George Beck, and Mircea Merca conjectured in `arXiv:2408.13346v1` (Section 1, lines 89–91) that $e_2p_4(n) = \sum_{\lambda \in \mathcal{P}(4, n)} e_2(\lambda)$—the sum of the second elementary symmetric polynomial over all length-4 partitions of $n$—satisfies an explicit 22-term homogeneous linear recurrence for sufficiently large $n$. Parameterizing $\mathcal{P}(4, n)$ bijectively by $(y_1, y_2, y_3, y_4) = (\lambda_1-\lambda_2, \lambda_2-\lambda_3, \lambda_3-\lambda_4, \lambda_4) \in \mathbb{Z}_{\ge 0}^3 \times \mathbb{Z}_{\ge 1}$ expands $e_2(\lambda)$ into 9 monomials and separates variables cleanly in $\mathbb{Z}[[q]]$, proving the exact rational generating function $G(q) = \sum_{n \ge 0} e_2p_4(n)q^n = N(q)/D(q)$ with palindromic degree-22 denominator $D(q) = (1-q)^6(1+q)^4(1+q^2)^3(1+q+q^2)^3$ and numerator $N(q) = 6q^4 + \dots + 11q^{16}$ of degree $16 < 22$. Extracting $[q^{n+22}](D(q)G(q)) = 0$ establishes the conjectured 22-term recurrence for **all** $n \ge 0$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Cristina Ballantine, George Beck, Mircea Merca, *"Partitions and elementary symmetric polynomials -- an experimental approach"*, `arXiv:2408.13346v1` (`2024-08-23`, `main.tex`, Section 1, lines 66, 84–85, and lines 89–91).
  - Statement: *"There is considerable numerical evidence to conjecture that the function $e_2p_4(n)$ satisfies the linear recurrence $y(22+n) = -y(n)-y(1+n)+3y(3+n)+6y(4+n)+3y(5+n)-3y(6+n)-12y(7+n)-12y(8+n)-2y(9+n)+10y(10+n)+18y(11+n)+10y(12+n)-2y(13+n)-12y(14+n)-12y(15+n)-3y(16+n)+3y(17+n)+6y(18+n)+3y(19+n)-y(21+n)$ and therefore $e_2p_4(n)$ would have periods modulo $m$ for positive $m$ and sufficiently large $n$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact integer/`sympy` verification of the 9-term expansion, exact $D(q)$ and $N(q)$ with $\deg N = 16 < 22$, direct brute-force partition enumeration of $\mathcal{P}(4, n)$ for $0 \le n \le 40$, and the 22-term recurrence for $0 \le n \le 18$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/ballantine-2408.13346-e2p4-rec/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:29:24Z`–`09:30Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live `gh search repos`/`issues` (`trureturing#8751` is unrelated `2409.11268`), live arXiv (`2408.13346v1` sole version, plus all 9 S2 citing papers of DOI `10.1007/s11139-024-01001-6` checked via arXiv abstracts), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior proofs of the $e_2p_4(n)$ recurrence found.

### `ballantine-2408.13346-e2p4-periods` — Counterexample to Ballantine–Beck–Merca Conjectural Minimal-Period Table for $e_2p_4(n) \pmod m$ (`arXiv:2408.13346v1`)

- **Summary**: Cristina Ballantine, George Beck, and Mircea Merca gave in `arXiv:2408.13346v1` (Section 1, lines 93–97) a 26-entry conjectural table of minimal eventual periods for $e_2p_4(n) \bmod m$, listing minimal period $1150$ for $m = 10$ and $1246$ for $m = 18$. Both entries are false: direct exact computation gives $e_2p_4(1150) = 4204006824112 \equiv 2 \not\equiv 0 = e_2p_4(0) \pmod{10}$ (and $e_2p_4(1154) \equiv 0 \not\equiv 6 = e_2p_4(4) \pmod{10}$) and $e_2p_4(1246) = 6275602376628 \equiv 6 \not\equiv 0 = e_2p_4(0) \pmod{18}$ (and $e_2p_4(1250) \equiv 0 \not\equiv 6 = e_2p_4(4) \pmod{18}$), and both even contradict the paper's own Theorem 2 (`\label{Th0}`) since $48 \nmid 1150$, $48 \nmid 1246$, and $54 \nmid 1246$. Because $(e_2p_4(n))_{n \ge 0}$ satisfies the exact order-22 recurrence from `N(q)/D(q)` with $\mathrm{SL}_{22}(\mathbb{Z})$ companion matrix, $e_2p_4(n) \bmod m$ is purely periodic from $n = 0$ for every $m \ge 2$ (so counterexamples recur for infinitely many $n$), with true minimal periods $\mathrm{per}(10) = \mathrm{lcm}(48, 300) = 1200 \neq 1150$ and $\mathrm{per}(18) = \mathrm{lcm}(48, 324) = 1296 \neq 1246$ (while the other 24 table entries hold).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Cristina Ballantine, George Beck, Mircea Merca, *"Partitions and elementary symmetric polynomials -- an experimental approach"*, `arXiv:2408.13346v1` (`2024-08-23`, `main.tex`, Section 1, lines 66, 84–85, 93–97, and Theorem 2 `\label{Th0}` lines 103–108).
  - Statement: *"Below we give a conjectural table of minimal periods for certain values of $m$."* Table claims period `1150` at $m = 10$ and period `1246` at $m = 18$ (among $26$ moduli $m \in \{2,\dots,37\}$).
- **Label**: `counterexample, verified by exact computation` (supported by `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, exact `int`/`sympy` verification of direct partition DP vs brute force for $0 \le n \le 40$, exact counterexample values at $n=0,2,4,5,6,51,1150,1154,1246,1250$, symbolic rational GF $N(q)/D(q)$ and pure periodicity via $\mathrm{SL}_{22}(\mathbb{Z})$, and exact minimal periods $\mathrm{per}(10) = 1200 \ne 1150$ and $\mathrm{per}(18) = 1296 \ne 1246$ in `notes/check-check-3.py`, plus `audit-3` verification of all $26$ table entries).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/ballantine-2408.13346-e2p4-periods/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:29Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live `gh search repos`/`issues` (`trureturing#8751` is unrelated `2409.11268`), live arXiv (`2408.13346v1` sole version, plus all 9 S2 citing works of DOI `10.1007/s11139-024-01001-6` checked via arXiv abstracts), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior corrections/refutations found.

### `godard-2605.21866-prob3` — Proof of Godard–Reis Problem 5.3 ($s(n) = n/p_0(n) + 1$) (`arXiv:2605.21866v1`)

- **Summary**: Jean Godard and Lucas Reis asked in `arXiv:2605.21866v1` (Section 5, Problem 5.3) for the exact threshold $s(n)$ ($n \ge 4$) such that the quadratic-form graph $\Gamma(Q_b, V)$ on $\mathbb{F}_{q^n}$ ($Q_b(X,Y) = X^2 + bXY + Y^2 \in V$) is connected for all large odd $q$, all $b \in \mathbb{F}_{q^n}^*$, and all $\mathbb{F}_q$-subspaces $V \subsetneq \mathbb{F}_{q^n}$ with $\#V \ge q^{s(n)}$. We proved in full that $s(n) = \frac{n}{p_0(n)} + 1$ for every $n \ge 4$, where $p_0(n)$ is the smallest prime factor of $n$ (settling $s(6)=4$, $s(8)=5$, $s(10)=6$, $s(p)=2$ for odd primes $p$, etc.). For the sharp lower bound at $\#V = q^{n/p_0(n)}$ (all odd $q \ge 3$), $V = \alpha\mathbb{F}_{q^{n/2}}$ isolates $0$ when $n$ is even, and $V = \mathbb{F}_{q^{n/p_0(n)}}$ with $b \in V^*$ disconnects $V$ from $\mathbb{F}_{q^n} \setminus V$ when $n$ is odd; for the upper bound at $\#V = q^{n/p_0(n)+1}$, character sums prove connectedness for all even $n \ge 4$ and odd $q \ge 5$, while odd $n \ge 5$ combines a norm-Weil/Kummer spanning argument for $b = \pm 2$ with Kummer fiber-product normality, Jacobian rank growth, purity/Galois descent ($d_K = 1$ at $K = (n-s+1)^2$), and Lang–Weil + Schwartz–Zippel for $b \notin \{0, \pm 2\}$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Jean Godard and Lucas Reis, *"Graphs from quadratic forms and vector spaces over finite fields"*, `arXiv:2605.21866v1` (`2026-05-21`, `godard-reis.tex`, Section 1 Definition 1.1 lines 100 & 114, Section 5 Problem 5.3 lines 363–368).
  - Statement: *"For each integer $n \ge 4$, find the smallest integer $s(n)$ with the following property: if $q$ is large and $\#V \ge q^{s(n)}$, then $\Gamma(Q_b, V)$ is connected for every $b \in \mathbb{F}_{q^n}^*$."*
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` re-audit PASS, and zero-float exact-arithmetic verification of Section 1 disconnections, Section 2 even-$n$ bounds and BFS on $\mathbb{F}_{5^4}, \mathbb{F}_{3^6}$, Section 3 Kummer–Weil bounds and BFS on $\mathbb{F}_{5^5}, \mathbb{F}_{7^5}$, and Section 4 Lemma 4.1, $\tau_i$, Lemma 4.3 Jacobian rank growth over $\mathbb{F}_{1000003}$ for $(5,2),(7,2),(9,4)$, and BFS on $\mathbb{F}_{5^5}, \mathbb{F}_{3^7}$ in `notes/check-check-1.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/godard-2605.21866-prob3/notes/check-check-1.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:33Z`–`09:33:46Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live `gh search repos`/`issues`/`prs` (`[]`), live arXiv (`2605.21866v1` sole version), OpenAlex (`cited_by=0`), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior solutions found.

### `oeis-A045742` — Proof of Mathar's 2022 D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture for OEIS `A045742`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in July 2022 on OEIS `A045742` that the count $a(2) = 0$, $a(n) = \frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{n-2} k \binom{n+k-2}{k}\binom{3n-3}{n-2-k}$ ($n \ge 3$) of interior faces in all noncrossing connected graphs on $n$ nodes on a circle satisfies a four-term third-order polynomial recurrence $\sum_{j=0}^3 C_j(n) a(n-j) = 0$ with cubic coefficients $C_j(n)$ for all $n \ge 5$. Applying Lagrange–Bürmann inversion to $\Phi(t) = (1+t)^3/(1-t)$ expresses the generating function $y(x) = \sum_{n \ge 2} a(n) x^n = x t(x)^2(1+t(x))/(1 - 4t(x) + t(x)^2)$, and eliminating $t$ via the $6 \times 6$ Sylvester resultant $\mathrm{Res}_t(E_1, E_2) = 2x P(x, y)$ yields the irreducible cubic algebraic equation $P(x, y(x)) = 0$ in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$. Expressing $P_y^{2k-1} y^{(k)} = N_k(x, y(x))$ ($k = 1, 2, 3$) and verifying by exact polynomial division in $\mathbb{Z}[x, y]$ that $P(x, y)$ divides the cleared third-order linear ODE numerator establishes $2544x^3 + \sum_{k=0}^3 p_k(x) y^{(k)}(x) = 0$ in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$, whose $[x^n]$ coefficient for $n \ge 5$ matches $C_0(n), \dots, C_3(n)$ identically in $\mathbb{Z}[n]$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A045742` (revision `20`, `2022-07-26T12:51:11-04:00`, offset `2,3`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Number of interior faces in all noncrossing connected graphs on n nodes on a circle."* / `a(n) = Sum_{k=1..n-2} k*binomial(n+k-2, k)*binomial(3*n-3, n-2-k)/(n-1).` / *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence n*(n-1)*(523*n-1993)*a(n) -6*(n-1)*(759*n^2-6019*n+12790)*a(n-1) -12*(n-2)*(4707*n^2-17937*n+6260)*a(n-2) +72*(3*n-10)*(759*n-2224)*(3*n-11)*a(n-3)=0."* (_R. J. Mathar_, Jul 26 2022)

### `otmani-2608.14453-rem3.2` — Proof of Otmani Section 3, Remark 3.1 (`arXiv:2608.14453v2`, CLF Supercongruence)

- **Summary**: Yassine Otmani posed as an open problem in `arXiv:2608.14453v2` (Section 3, **Remark 3.1**; directory slug `otmani-2608.14453-rem3.2`) whether the Catalan–Larcombe–French numbers $(\mathcal{P}_k)_{k \ge 0}$ satisfy $\sum_{k=0}^{p-1} \mathcal{P}_k^2 / 128^k \equiv \left(\frac{-1}{p}\right) \sum_{k=0}^{p-1} \binom{2k}{k}^4 / 256^k \pmod{p^3}$ for every prime $p > 3$. Expressing the difference via $c_m = \binom{2m}{m}^2 / 16^m$, $B_m = \sum_{i=2m}^{p-1} (\mathcal{P}_i/16^i)\binom{i}{2m}$, and $E_p(m) = c_m - \varepsilon B_m$ ($\varepsilon = (\frac{-1}{p})$, $n = (p-1)/2$), we derived the polynomial ODE $\mathcal{L}_G(G)(x) = p^2(1+x)^p(-2v_p + v_{p-1}(1+x))$ and operator identity $(1+x)\mathcal{L}_A = -x\mathcal{L}_G$ to prove $p^2 \mid E_p(m)$ and $4m^2 \tilde{E}_m - (2m-1)^2 \tilde{E}_{m-1} \equiv 1 \pmod p$ for all $0 \le m \le n$ (seeded at $m = n$ via Morley's congruence and Wolstenholme's harmonic sum). Reflection $S_m = \tilde{E}_m + \tilde{E}_{n-m}$ reduces the mod-$p^3$ congruence to $S_0 \equiv 2\tilde{E}_n - \sum_{k=0}^{n-1} \binom{n-1}{k}^{-2} \equiv 0 \pmod p$, which follows cleanly from self-contained telescoping proofs of Staver's and Apéry's binomial identities.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Yassine Otmani, *"New Congruences Involving $p$-adic dual sequences"*, `arXiv:2608.14453v2` (`2026-08-19`, `Manuscript.tex`, Section 1 `\eqref{eq:CLF}` lines 196–205 and Section 3 **Remark 3.1** lines 471–477; retaining directory slug `otmani-2608.14453-rem3.2`).
  - Statement: *"$\sum_{k=0}^{p-1} \frac{\mathcal{P}_k^2}{128^k} \equiv \left(\frac{-1}{p}\right) \sum_{k=0}^{p-1} \binom{2k}{k}^4 \frac{1}{256^k} \pmod{p^3}$"* for every prime $p > 3$, where $\mathcal{P}_n = \sum_{k=0}^n \binom{2k}{k}^2 \binom{2n-2k}{n-k}^2 / \binom{n}{k}$.
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` re-audit PASS, and exact `int`/`Fraction`/`sympy` verification of CLF identities ($k \le 110$), Lemmas 1–6 mod $p, p^2, p^3, p^4$ ($5 \le p \le 73$), exact operator/ODE/Staver/Apéry identities, and Remark 3.1 directly for all $26$ primes $5 \le p \le 107$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/otmani-2608.14453-rem3.2/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:37Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live arXiv (`2608.14453v2` latest), OpenAlex (`cited_by=0`), live `gh search repos`/`issues`/`prs` (0 proofs), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

### `oeis-A121580` — Proof of Mathar's 2022 D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture for OEIS `A121580`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in July 2022 on OEIS `A121580` that the number of cells in column 1 of all deco polyominoes of height $n$—defined by $a(1) = 1$ and $a(n) = a(n-1) + (n-1)!(1 + n(n-1)/2)$ for $n \ge 2$—satisfies the five-term polynomial recurrence $a(n) + (-n-4)a(n-1) + 3(n+1)a(n-2) + 2(-2n+3)a(n-3) + 2(n-3)a(n-4) = 0$ for all $n \ge 5$, and is therefore P-recursive (D-finite EGF). Rewriting the recurrence identically as a linear combination of first differences $b(k) = a(k) - a(k-1) = \frac{(k-1)!}{2}(k^2 - k + 2)$ for $k \in \{n, n-1, n-2, n-3\}$ factors out $\frac{(n-4)!}{2}$ multiplied by a degree-5 polynomial $P(n)$ that vanishes identically in $\mathbb{Z}[n]$, and summing against $x^{n-4}/(n-4)!$ with $a(0) = 0$ yields the homogeneous order-4 linear ODE $(1-x)\hat{y}^{(4)}(x) + (3x-8)\hat{y}^{(3)}(x) + (15-4x)\hat{y}''(x) + (2x-10)\hat{y}'(x) + 2\hat{y}(x) = 0$ for $\hat{y}(x) = \sum_{n \ge 1} a(n)\frac{x^n}{n!}$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A121580` (revision `12`, `2024-09-15T07:13:47-04:00`, offset `1,2`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Number of cells in column 1 of all deco polyominoes of height n."* / `a(1) = 1, a(n) = a(n-1)+(n-1)!*(1+n*(n-1)/2) for n>=2.` / *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence a(n) +(-n-4)*a(n-1) +3*(n+1)*a(n-2) +2*(-2*n+3)*a(n-3) +2*(n-3)*a(n-4)=0."* (_R. J. Mathar_, Jul 26 2022)
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact `int`/`Fraction`/`sympy` verification of all $19$ live OEIS terms `a(1)..a(19)`, Deutsch's closed form ($1 \le n \le 24$), Steps 1–2 polynomial identity $P(n) \equiv 0$, Step 3 homogeneous order-4 EGF ODE through $x^{44}$, and Mathar's recurrence for $5 \le n \le 60$ in `notes/check-check-2.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A121580/notes/check-check-2.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:37Z`–`09:37:46Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live OEIS `A121580` rev. `12` (still marked `Conjecture`), live `gh search repos`/`issues` (`[]`), live arXiv (`0` hits), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior proofs found.

### `oeis-A121584` — Proof of Mathar's 2022 D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture for OEIS `A121584`

- **Summary**: R. J. Mathar conjectured in July 2022 on OEIS `A121584` that the number of cells in columns 1 and 2 of all deco polyominoes of height $n$—defined by $a(1) = 1$, $a(2) = 4$, and $a(n) = \frac{(2n-3)a(n-1) - (n-1)a(n-2)}{n-2} + \frac{1}{2}(2n^3 - 9n^2 + 17n - 16)\frac{(n-1)!}{n-2}$ ($n \ge 3$)—satisfies a six-term order-5 polynomial recurrence $M(n) = \sum_{j=0}^5 C_j(n) a(n-j) = 0$ for all $n \ge 6$ (D-finite EGF). Clearing $k - 2$ writes the defining relation as a second-order difference operator $B(k) = (k-2)a(k) - (2k-3)a(k-1) + (k-1)a(k-2) = \frac{(k-1)!}{2}(2k^3 - 9k^2 + 17k - 16)$ ($k \ge 3$), and an explicit $\mathbb{Z}[n]$ operator factorization expresses $(n-2)(n-3)M(n) = \sum_{j=0}^3 d_j(n) B(n-j) = \frac{(n-4)!}{2} P(n)$ with $P(n) \equiv 0 \in \mathbb{Z}[n]$, proving $M(n) = 0$ for every $n \ge 6$ and yielding a polynomial-coefficient linear ODE for $\hat{y}(x) = \sum_{n \ge 1} a(n) \frac{x^n}{n!}$.
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: OEIS `A121584` (revision `4`, `2022-07-26T11:38:40-04:00`, offset `1,2`).
  - Name & Formula (Conjecture): *"Number of cells in columns 1 and 2 of all deco polyominoes of height n."* / `a(1)=1, a(2)=4, a(n)=[(2n-3)a(n-1)-(n-1)a(n-2)]/(n-2) + (1/2)(2n^3-9n^2+17n-16)(n-1)!/(n-2) for n>=3.` / *"Conjecture D-finite with recurrence 14*(-n+1)*a(n) +(14*n^2+1731*n-6995)*a(n-1) +3*(-577*n^2+480*n+7243)*a(n-2) +2*(2781*n^2-11952*n+6004)*a(n-3) +(-5987*n^2+36181*n-54220)*a(n-4) +2*(1071*n-3433)*(n-4)*a(n-5)=0."* (_R. J. Mathar_, Jul 26 2022)
- **Label**: `written proof, internally audited` (supported by `PROOF.md`, `AUDIT.md` PASS, and exact `int`/`Fraction`/`sympy` verification of all $19$ live OEIS terms `a(1)..a(19)`, Steps 2–3 exact $\mathbb{Z}[n]$ operator factorization and $P(n) \equiv 0$, and Mathar's recurrence for $6 \le n \le 120$ in `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python targets/oeis-A121584/notes/check-check-3.py`

### `bartnicki-2303.08771-conj3` — Counterexample to Bartnicki–Czerwiński–Grytczuk–Miechowicz Conjecture 3 (`arXiv:2303.08771v1` / *Graphs Combin.* 41 (2025))

- **Summary**: Tomasz Bartnicki, Sebastian Czerwiński, Jarosław Grytczuk, and Zofia Miechowicz conjectured in `arXiv:2303.08771v1` (Section 3, Conjecture 3; published in *Graphs and Combinatorics* **41** (2025), Art. 80) that every finite simple graph $G$ satisfies $\zeta(G) \le \mathrm{col}(G)$, where $\zeta(G)$ is the strong arboricity (minimum number of edge-color classes with no monochromatic broken cycle $C - e$) and $\mathrm{col}(G)$ is the coloring number (degeneracy plus $1$). We refuted this conjecture with an explicit $9$-vertex, $24$-edge simple graph $G$ partitioned into $A \sqcup B \sqcup C$ ($|A|=4, |B|=3, |C|=2$) with edges $K_4$ on $A$, $K_{4,3}$ between $A$ and $B$, and $K_{3,2}$ between $B$ and $C$. Because $G[A \cup \{4\}] \cong K_5$ and ordering $(0, \dots, 8)$ has maximum back-degree $4$, $\mathrm{col}(G) = 5$; however, every strongly woody class satisfies $3e_A(F) + e_{AB}(F) \le 6$, forcing $1$ Type-I and $4$ Type-II classes on $A \cup B$ whose two allowed matchings on $A \times B$ trap any strongly woody $5$-coloring into a monochromatic broken $4$-cycle or a monochromatic $C_4$ on $E_{BC}$, giving $\zeta(G) = 6 > 5 = \mathrm{col}(G)$ (witnessed by an explicit strongly woody $6$-coloring).
- **Exact statement & source**:
  - Source: Tomasz Bartnicki, Sebastian Czerwiński, Jarosław Grytczuk, Zofia Miechowicz, *"Strong arboricity of graphs"*, `arXiv:2303.08771v1` (`2023-03-15`, `Zlamasy_III_11.tex`, Section 1 lines 86–101 and Section 3 lines 248–252) and *Graphs and Combinatorics* **41** (2025), Art. 80 (`DOI: 10.1007/s00373-025-02934-5`, pp. 7–8), Conjecture 3.
  - Statement: *"Every graph $G$ satisfies $\zeta(G)\leqslant \mathrm{col}(G)$."*
- **Label**: `counterexample, verified by exact computation` (supported by `COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`, `AUDIT.md` §5 PASS, and pure-integer exhaustive verification of $\mathrm{col}(G) = 5$ across all $511$ nonempty induced subgraphs, all $24$ single-edge contractions $G/e$ on the explicit $6$-coloring, enumeration of all $3{,}292$ strongly woody classes, and exhaustive exact-cover proof that $\zeta(G) > 5$ and $\zeta(G) = 6$ in `notes/check-check-3.py`).
- **Verification command**: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/bartnicki-2303.08771-conj3/notes/check-check-3.py`
- **Latest novelty receipt**: Gate 3 PASS (`2026-11-27T09:39:57Z`–`09:40:26Z`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`): checked all `608` rebuilt snapshots (`watch/snapshots/**`, incl. `1337isnot1337/solved-conjectures` rebuilt `09:19:40Z`), live `gh search repos`/`issues` (`[]`), live arXiv (`2303.08771v1` sole version), OpenAlex (`W4411944715`, `cited_by_count: 0`), Semantic Scholar (`0` citations on `10.1007/s00373-025-02934-5`), Crossref (`0`), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and web search; 0 prior refutations found.

### Mbirika–Spilker `arXiv:2204.12626v2` Question 6.1 (Conjectures for $\mathcal{F}^3(k)$ and $\mathcal{L}^3(k)$)
- **Statement**: In Section 6 ("Open questions", Question 6.1) of *GCD of sums of $k$ consecutive squares of generalized Fibonacci numbers* (`arXiv:2204.12626v2`, *Fibonacci Quarterly* Vol. 60 (2022), No. 5, 255–269), Mbirika and Spilker conjecture for all even $k$ (with $F_0=0, F_1=1$, $L_0=2, L_1=1$, and $\mathcal{G}_{G_0,G_1}^3(k) = \gcd\{(\sum_{i=1}^k G_{n+i}^3)_{n \ge 0}\}$) that:
  $$\mathcal{F}^3(k) = \begin{cases} \gcd(F_{k+1}^3 - 1, F_{k+2}^3 - 1), & \text{if } 6 \mid k, \\ \frac{1}{2}\gcd(F_{k+1}^3 - 1, F_{k+2}^3 - 1), & \text{if } 6 \nmid k, \end{cases} \qquad \mathcal{L}^3(k) = \begin{cases} \gcd(L_{k+1}^3 - 1, L_{k+2}^3 - 9), & \text{if } 6 \mid k, \\ \frac{1}{2}\gcd(L_{k+1}^3 - 1, L_{k+2}^3 - 9), & \text{if } 6 \nmid k. \end{cases}$$
- **Status**: Refuted (`counterexample, verified by exact computation`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - **$\mathcal{F}^3(k)$ fails in both branches**:
    - At $k=2$ ($6 \nmid 2$), $F_1^3+F_2^3=2$ and $F_2^3+F_3^3=9$ give $\mathcal{F}^3(2)=1$, whereas the formula gives $\frac{1}{2}\gcd(2^3-1, 3^3-1) = \frac{1}{2}\gcd(7,26) = \frac{1}{2} \notin \mathbb{Z}$.
    - At $k=6$ ($6 \mid 6$), $(F_n \bmod 2)$ has period $3 \mid 6$ with two odd terms per period, so every block of $6$ consecutive cubes has $4$ odd summands and is even, while the $n=0,1$ sums are $674$ and $2870$ with $\gcd(674, 2870)=2$, giving $\mathcal{F}^3(6)=2$; however $\gcd(F_7^3-1, F_8^3-1) = \gcd(2196, 9260) = 4 \neq 2$. (The verbatim $\mathcal{F}^3(k)$ formula fails for all 15 even $k \in \{2,4,\dots,30\}$.)
  - **$\mathcal{L}^3(k)$ fails in both branches**:
    - At $k=2$ ($6 \nmid 2$), the $n=0,1,2$ sums are $28, 91, 407$ with $\gcd(28,91,407)=1$, so $\mathcal{L}^3(2)=1$, whereas $\frac{1}{2}\gcd(L_3^3-1, L_4^3-9) = \frac{1}{2}\gcd(63, 334) = \frac{1}{2} \notin \mathbb{Z}$.
    - At $k=12$ ($6 \mid 12$), $(L_n \bmod 4)$ has period $6$ with cubes mod $4$ summing to $10 \equiv 2 \pmod 4$ per period (so every $12$-term sum is $\equiv 0 \pmod 4$), and $(L_n \bmod 5)$ has period $4$ with cubes mod $5$ summing to $10 \equiv 0 \pmod 5$ (so every $12$-term sum is $\equiv 0 \pmod 5$); the $n=0,1,2$ sums $43702500, 185123260, 784200340$ have $\gcd=20$, so $\mathcal{L}^3(12)=20$, whereas $\gcd(L_{13}^3-1, L_{14}^3-9) = \gcd(141420760, 599077098) = 2 \neq 20$.
  - **Scientific transparency note (`AUDIT.md` §3)**: Swapping the $6 \mid k$ and $6 \nmid k$ branch labels makes the $\mathcal{F}^3(k)$ formula match all $60$ even integers $2 \le k \le 120$ tested (`0` mismatches), consistent with a branch-label transposition typo in the paper. By contrast, $\mathcal{L}^3(k)$ still fails substantively even after simultaneously repairing **both** apparent typos (swapping the $6 \mid k$ / $6 \nmid k$ branches and replacing $9 = L_2^2$ with $27 = L_2^3$) at $k = 6$ ($\mathcal{L}^3(6) = 2$ vs. $\frac{1}{2}\gcd(L_7^3-1, L_8^3-27) = 14$), $k = 22$ ($199$ vs. $1393$), $k = 24$ ($1080$ vs. $14040$), $k = 34$, $k = 38$, etc.
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/mbirika-2204.12626-ques6.1/TARGET.md`](targets/mbirika-2204.12626-ques6.1/TARGET.md), [`targets/mbirika-2204.12626-ques6.1/COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`](targets/mbirika-2204.12626-ques6.1/COUNTEREXAMPLE.md), [`targets/mbirika-2204.12626-ques6.1/AUDIT.md`](targets/mbirika-2204.12626-ques6.1/AUDIT.md), [`targets/mbirika-2204.12626-ques6.1/notes/check-check-1.py`](targets/mbirika-2204.12626-ques6.1/notes/check-check-1.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/mbirika-2204.12626-ques6.1/notes/check-check-1.py` exited `0` (`PASS`), verifying Counterexamples 1a, 1b, 2a, 2b, modular periods, $k \in \{2,4,\dots,30\}$ mismatches, and the $L_2^3=27$ remark at $k=6$ in exact `int` / `Fraction` arithmetic; adversarial audit (`@audit-2`, `2026-11-27T09:45Z`) passed and verified statement fidelity against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2204.12626/main.tex` (`arXiv:2204.12626v2`, *Fibonacci Quarterly* Vol. 60 (2022), No. 5, 255–269).
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `09:47Z`–`09:48Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `arXiv:2204.12626v2`, Zenodo API (`0` hits), `watch/snapshots/` (`0/608` files), and live GitHub `gh search repos/issues` (`the-omega-institute/trureturing#22988` targets Question 6.2 scaling only and explicitly excludes Question 6.1).
- **What is not established**:
  - Live `gh search code` was skipped due to GitHub secondary rate-limit cooldown; unindexed private repositories and paywalled papers not citing `2204.12626` are not covered. Does not rule out that a different non-conjectured closed form exists for $\mathcal{L}^3(k)$ or that the branch-swapped $\mathcal{F}^3(k)$ formula holds for all even $k$.

### OEIS `A121751` — Mathar (2022) D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture
- **Statement**: For the sequence `A121751` (rev. 5) defined by $a(1)=0$, $a(2)=1$, and $a(n) = 2\lfloor\frac{n-1}{2}\rfloor a(n-1) - (\lfloor\frac{n-1}{2}\rfloor\lfloor\frac{n-2}{2}\rfloor - 1)a(n-2)$ for $n \ge 3$, R. J. Mathar's Jul 26 2022 `Conjecture` asserts that for all $n \ge 7$, $\sum_{j=0}^6 C_j(n) a(n-j) = 0$ (where $C_0=256$, $C_1=-384$, $C_2=16(-8n^2+40n-67)$, $C_3=16(8n^2-54n+87)$, $C_4=4(4n^4-56n^3+242n^2-304n-21)$, $C_5=4(-2n^4+38n^3-249n^2+647n-554)$, $C_6=(n-4)(n-8)(n^2-12n+31)$) and hence $a(n)$ is D-finite (P-recursive).
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - Splitting the defining recurrence by parity ($m = 2r \ge 4$ vs. $m = 2r+1 \ge 3$) eliminates the floor functions into two polynomial recurrences.
  - For every $n \ge 7$, all five indices $n, n-1, n-2, n-3, n-4$ lie in $\ge 3$; iterating the parity recurrences five times expresses $a(n-4), \dots, a(n)$ as $\mathbb{Z}[k]$-linear combinations of $a(n-5)$ and $a(n-6)$ separately for $n=2k$ ($k \ge 4$) and $n=2k+1$ ($k \ge 3$). Exact polynomial arithmetic in $\mathbb{Z}[k]$ shows that both coefficient polynomials vanish identically in each parity case, proving $M(n) = 0$ for all $n \ge 7$.
  - Extending $a(0)=0$ and summing $M(m+6)\frac{x^m}{m!}$ over $m \ge 1$ converts the recurrence into a 6th-order linear ODE for the exponential generating function $\hat{y}(x) = \sum_{n \ge 1} a(n)\frac{x^n}{n!}$ with leading term $16(x^2-4)^2 \hat{y}^{(6)}(x)$ and boundary constant $-20$; differentiating once yields a non-trivial homogeneous 7th-order linear ODE with polynomial coefficients and nonzero leading coefficient $16(x^2-4)^2$, proving $\hat{y}(x)$ is D-finite.
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/oeis-A121751/TARGET.md`](targets/oeis-A121751/TARGET.md), [`targets/oeis-A121751/PROOF.md`](targets/oeis-A121751/PROOF.md), [`targets/oeis-A121751/AUDIT.md`](targets/oeis-A121751/AUDIT.md), [`targets/oeis-A121751/notes/check-check-2.py`](targets/oeis-A121751/notes/check-check-2.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/oeis-A121751/notes/check-check-2.py` exited `0` (`PASS`), verifying the 23 OEIS terms, Step 1 parity rules for $m=3..200$, Step 2 exact SymPy $\mathbb{Z}[k]$ polynomial cancellation, $M(n)=0$ for $7 \le n \le 100$, and the order-6/7 EGF ODE with boundary $-20$ and leading coefficient $16(x^2-4)^2$ through $x^{23}$; adversarial audit (`@audit-1`) passed and verified statement fidelity against live OEIS `A121751` (rev. 5).
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `09:49Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `watch/snapshots/` (`0/608`), live OEIS rev. 5 (`Conjecture` unchanged), Zenodo API (`0` hits), arXiv (`0` hits), and live GitHub `gh search repos/issues` (`0` hits).

### OEIS `A121754` — Mathar (2022) D-Finite Recurrence Conjecture
- **Statement**: For the sequence `A121754` (rev. 4, offset `1,3`) defined by $a(1)=0$, $a(2)=1$, and $a(n) = (2n-3)a(n-1) - (n-1)(n-3)a(n-2) + (n-2)!\bigl(n - 2 + \frac{1 + (-1)^{n-1}}{2}(n-1)\bigr)$ for $n \ge 3$, R. J. Mathar's Jul 26 2022 `Conjecture` asserts that for all $n \ge 6$, $16(n+1)a(n) + (-16n^2-178n+531)a(n-1) + (-16n^3+178n^2-393n-510)a(n-2) + (16n^4+98n^3-1439n^2+4222n-3623)a(n-3) + (-146n^4+1479n^3-4483n^2+3054n+2841)a(n-4) + (130n-311)(n-6)(n-4)^2 a(n-5) = 0$ and hence $a(n)$ is D-finite.
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - Setting $D(k) = a(k) - k\,a(k-1)$ yields $D(k) - (k-3)D(k-1) = (k-2)!\,f(k)$ and $D(k) = (k-3)!\,S(k)$ for $k \ge 3$, with closed forms $S(2r) = (r-1)^2(4r-1)$ ($r \ge 2$) and $S(2r+1) = r^2(4r-1)$ ($r \ge 1$) proved by induction.
  - Using the exact polynomial identity $M(n) = \sum_{i=0}^4 e_i(n) D(n-i)$ in $\mathbb{Z}[n]$, $M(6) = 0$ follows directly ($e_4(6)=0$), while for $n \ge 7$, $M(2r) = (2r-7)!\, P_{\mathrm{even}}(r) = 0$ ($r \ge 4$) and $M(2r+1) = (2r-6)!\, P_{\mathrm{odd}}(r) = 0$ ($r \ge 3$) vanish identically in $\mathbb{Z}[r]$.
  - Extending $a(0)=0$ gives $M(5) = 339$, so $\sum_{m \ge 0} M(m+5)\frac{x^m}{m!} = 339$ yields an order-6 linear ODE $L[\hat{y}](x) = 339$ with leading coefficient $16x(x-1)^2(x+1)$, differentiating once to a homogeneous order-7 polynomial-coefficient linear ODE for $\hat{y}(x)$.

### Ulfarsson `arXiv:2603.16004v1` Conjecture 2.9 (`conj:general-reverse`, Reverse-Reply Strategy for PAP)
- **Statement**: In Section 2 (Conjecture 2.9, `\label{conj:general-reverse}`) of *A Permutation Avoidance Game with Reverse Replies and Monotone Traps* (`arXiv:2603.16004v1`), Henning Ulfarsson conjectures that for every pattern length $k \ge 3$, there exists $n_0(k)$ such that in the normal-play impartial game **PAP** (*Permutations Avoiding Patterns*) starting from $S_n$, the move-by-move reverse strategy ($p \mapsto p^r$) is a winning strategy for Player II for all $n \ge n_0(k)$, and in particular $\mathrm{sg}(S_n, k) = 0$ for all such $n$.
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - Proved unconditionally for all $k \ge 3$ with explicit threshold $n_0(k) := k + ((2k+1)(k-1))^2 + 1 = 4k^4 - 4k^3 - 3k^2 + 3k + 2$.
  - **Monotone Trap & Conditional Reverse-Reply Criterion (Proposition 1)**: Whenever every non-monotone legal move $p$ from a reverse-closed monotone-free forbidden set $F \subseteq S_k$ admits a legal reverse reply $p^r$ from $F \cup \{p\}$, Player II maintains the reverse-closed monotone-free invariant until Player I plays $\mathrm{id}_k$ or $\mathrm{id}_k^r$, whereupon Player II replies with the opposite monotone pattern ($\mathrm{id}_n^r$ or $\mathrm{id}_n$ remains in $\mathrm{Av}_n(F \cup \{p\})$) and wins immediately since $n \ge n_0(k) > (k-1)^2 + 1$ makes $\mathrm{Av}_n(F \cup \{\mathrm{id}_k, \mathrm{id}_k^r\}) = \emptyset$ by Erdős–Szekeres; induction on finite $|X|$ gives $\mathrm{sg}(S_n, k) = 0$.
  - **1-Point Inflation Extraction & Reduction (Lemmas 0–2)**: For any legal $p$ at length $n \ge n_0(k)$, Erdős–Szekeres plus pigeonhole over at most $2k+1$ grid cells extracts $\sigma \in \mathrm{Av}_n(F)$ decomposing into a $p$-copy and a monotone block $B_0$ of length $n-k$ and sign $\varepsilon \in \{+, -\}$ in a single grid cell. Either $\sigma^r$ witnesses $p^r$ from $F \cup \{p\}$, or $p \in \mathrm{Sh}_k(\sigma^r)$ feeds a 1-point inflation $\omega_1 = I_{n-k+1}(p^r, j_1, \varepsilon) \in \mathrm{Av}_n(F)$ and (if $p \in \mathrm{Sh}_k(\omega_1)$) a second inflation $\omega_2 = I_{n-k+1}(p^r, j_2, -\varepsilon) \in \mathrm{Av}_n(F)$.
  - **Self-Inflation Dichotomy (Theorem 3)**: Collapsing the inflated block (Claims 3A–3B) shows that $p \in \mathrm{Sh}_k(I_k(p^r, j_1, +))$ and $p \in \mathrm{Sh}_k(I_k(p^r, j_2, -))$ force a cyclic chain $\mathrm{des}(p) \le \mathrm{asc}(p) \le \mathrm{des}(p)$ whose rigidity contradicts both $p(1) < p(k)$ and $p(1) > p(k)$. Hence at least one of $\omega_1, \omega_2$ avoids $p$ and witnesses legality of $p^r$ from $F \cup \{p\}$.
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/ulfarsson-2603.16004-conj1/TARGET.md`](targets/ulfarsson-2603.16004-conj1/TARGET.md), [`targets/ulfarsson-2603.16004-conj1/PROOF.md`](targets/ulfarsson-2603.16004-conj1/PROOF.md), [`targets/ulfarsson-2603.16004-conj1/AUDIT.md`](targets/ulfarsson-2603.16004-conj1/AUDIT.md), [`targets/ulfarsson-2603.16004-conj1/notes/check-check-2.py`](targets/ulfarsson-2603.16004-conj1/notes/check-check-2.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/ulfarsson-2603.16004-conj1/notes/check-check-2.py` exited `0` (`PASS`), verifying threshold arithmetic ($3 \le k \le 500$), Lemma 0, Lemma 2 witnesses exhaustively ($k=3,4$), Claims 3A–3B & Theorem 3 exhaustively ($k=3..6$), and exact PAP game trees ($k=3, n=3..6$); adversarial audit (`@audit-1`) passed against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2603.16004v1/ItsAllFunAndGamesUntilMonotonicity.tex`.
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `10:06Z`–`10:07:08Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `watch/snapshots/` (`0/618` resolution hits; OpenAlex `cited_by=0; S2=0`), live arXiv (`2603.16004v1` sole version), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and live GitHub `gh search repos/issues` (`0` hits).
- **What is not established**:
  - Establishes the quartic bound $n_0(k) = 4k^4 - 4k^3 - 3k^2 + 3k + 2$ rather than the smallest possible $n_0(k)$ for each $k \ge 3$. Live `gh search code` was skipped due to GitHub secondary rate-limit cooldown; unindexed private repositories and paywalled papers not mentioning `2603.16004` are not covered. ### Derycke–Dukes–Le Borgne `arXiv:2402.15372v3` Conjecture 6.1 (`conj:ITCCTIsymmetry`, directory `derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1`)
- **Statement**: In Section 6 (*Conclusion*; `% SECTION 7` in `qtss1.tex`) of *The sandpile model on the complete split graph: $q,t$-Schröder polynomials, sawtooth polyominoes, and a cycle lemma* (`arXiv:2402.15372v3`, updated `2025-12-19`), Henri Derycke, Mark Dukes, and Yvan Le Borgne conjecture (**Conjecture 6.1**, `\label{conj:ITCCTIsymmetry}`) that for all $n \ge 1$ and $d \ge 0$ on the complete split graph $S_{n,d}$, $\mathfrak{F}_{n,d}^{\mathrm{CTI}}(q,t) = \mathfrak{F}_{n,d}^{\mathrm{ITC}}(q,t)$.
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - Proved the strictly stronger refined first-round equality $\mathfrak{F}_{n,d,r}^{\mathrm{CTI}}(q,t) = \mathfrak{F}_{n,d,r}^{\mathrm{ITC}}(q,t)$ for all $n \ge 1$, $d \ge 0$, and $1 \le r \le n+d$ (summing over $r$ yields Conjecture 6.1, and combined with Corollary 3.6 of `arXiv:2402.15372v3` also implies Conjecture 6.2 `thm:schroeder`; Conjecture 6.3 `prop:itc-cti` requesting an explicit sandpile bijection is noted as outside scope).
  - **Exact Cell Decompositions (Propositions 1.1 & 2.1)**: Derived from Dhar's burning criterion (`Proposition 2.1` of `arXiv:2402.15372v3`) exact $q$-binomial/$q$-trinomial cell formulas for $\mathfrak{F}_{n,d,r}^{\mathrm{CTI}}(q,t)$ and $\mathfrak{F}_{n,d,r}^{\mathrm{ITC}}(q,t)$ uniformly for all $n \ge 1, d \ge 0$.
  - **Generating Functions & $q$-Hypergeometric Proof (Section 3, Lemmas 4.1–4.3, Theorem 4.4)**: Translated both cell decompositions into bivariate formal power series $P_r(X,Y)$ and $\widetilde{P}_r(X,Y)$ in $\mathbb{Z}[q,t][[X,Y]]$. Using Cauchy's $q$-binomial theorem (Lemma 4.1) and Heine's third transformation at coefficient level (Lemma 4.2), established the finite polynomial symmetry identity (Lemma 4.3), and proved $\widetilde{P}_r = P_r$ by strong induction on total degree $N$ (Theorem 4.4).
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1/TARGET.md`](targets/derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1/TARGET.md), [`targets/derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1/PROOF.md`](targets/derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1/PROOF.md), [`targets/derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1/AUDIT.md`](targets/derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1/AUDIT.md), [`targets/derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1/notes/check-check-2.py`](targets/derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1/notes/check-check-2.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/derycke-2402.15372-conj7_1/notes/check-check-2.py` exited `0` (`PASS`, 0 float constants), verifying direct sandpile CTI=ITC enumeration (`2314` recurrent configs for $n+d \le 6$ + $S_{2,2}$ Example 2.3), Prop 1.1 = Prop 2.1 ($n+d \le 9$), generating-function equality ($N \le 7$), Lemma 4.2 ($N \le 5$), Lemma 4.3 ($r,r' \le 7$), and Theorem 4.4 telescoping ($1 \le a, m \le r' \le 7$); adversarial audit (`@audit-2`, `2026-11-27T10:08Z`) passed against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2402.15372/qtss1.tex`.

### Ciobanu–Turaev `arXiv:2604.22375v1` Section 3.1 Conjecture 1 (`conjecture1`, $\mathbf{VPL}^\forall(G) = \mathbf{REG}^\forall(G)$)
- **Statement**: In Section 3.1 (*$\mathbf{VPL}^\forall$ sets in groups*, Conjecture 1 `\label{conjecture1}`) of *Visibly Pushdown Languages in Groups* (`arXiv:2604.22375v1`, full version of DLT 2026 paper), Ciobanu and Turaev conjecture that in every finitely generated group $G = \langle X \rangle$ (with $\Sigma = X \cup X^{-1}$ and canonical epimorphism $\pi \colon \Sigma^\star \to G$), every $\mathbf{VPL}^\forall$ subset is recognisable, i.e., $\mathbf{VPL}^\forall(G) = \mathbf{REG}^\forall(G)$.
- **Status**: Proved (`machine-checked proof in Lean 4`, upgraded from `written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - $\mathbf{REG}^\forall(G) \subseteq \mathbf{VPL}^\forall(G)$ is immediate by setting $\Sigma_i = \Sigma$, $\Sigma_c = \Sigma_r = \emptyset$.
  - Conversely, let $S \in \mathbf{VPL}^\forall(G)$ with $L = \pi^{-1}(S)$ accepted by a VPA $M = (Q, \Gamma, \delta, Q_0, F)$ over $\Sigma = \Sigma_c \sqcup \Sigma_i \sqcup \Sigma_r$, and let $H := \mathrm{Stab}_L(S) = \{h \in G : hS = S\} \le G$.
  - If $\Sigma_r = \emptyset$, forgetting stack pushes turns $M$ into an NFA $M'$ for $L$, and the reachable-state congruence $R(u_1) = R(u_2)$ gives $H\pi(u_1) = H\pi(u_2)$, bounding $[G : H] \le 2^{|Q|} < \infty$.
  - If $\Sigma_r \neq \emptyset$, the matched-call sublanguage $\mathit{MC}(\Sigma) := \{w \in \Sigma^\star : \forall \text{ suffix } s \text{ of } w,\, \#_c(s) \le \#_r(s)\}$ maps under $\pi$ to a submonoid $M_{\mathrm{mc}} \subseteq G$ that generates $G$ as a group (since $\pi(x) = \pi(xy_0)\pi(y_0)^{-1}$ for $y_0 \in \Sigma_r$, Lemma 1). Every run of $M$ on $u \in \mathit{MC}(\Sigma)$ starting from empty stack $\bot$ terminates with $\bot$, so right-congruence ${\sim_0}$ on $\mathit{MC}(\Sigma)$ has index $\le 2^{|Q|}$ and forces $H\pi(u_1) = H\pi(u_2)$ (Lemma 2). Since right multiplication $\rho_m$ ($m \in M_{\mathrm{mc}}$) stabilizes the finite set $\Omega := \{Hm : m \in M_{\mathrm{mc}}\} \subseteq H \backslash G$ and is injective, $\rho_m(\Omega) = \Omega$ for all $m \in M_{\mathrm{mc}}$, hence for all $g \in G = \langle M_{\mathrm{mc}} \rangle_{\mathrm{grp}}$, yielding $\Omega = H \backslash G$ and $[G : H] \le 2^{|Q|} < \infty$ (Lemma 3).
  - Taking the normal core $N = \bigcap_{g \in G} gHg^{-1} \trianglelefteq G$ ($[G:N] \le [G:H]! < \infty$), $S = \bigcup_{s \in S} Ns$ is a finite union of $N$-cosets, so $S \in \mathbf{REG}^\forall(G)$.
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/ciobanu-2604.22375-conj1/TARGET.md`](targets/ciobanu-2604.22375-conj1/TARGET.md), [`targets/ciobanu-2604.22375-conj1/PROOF.md`](targets/ciobanu-2604.22375-conj1/PROOF.md), [`targets/ciobanu-2604.22375-conj1/AUDIT.md`](targets/ciobanu-2604.22375-conj1/AUDIT.md), [`targets/ciobanu-2604.22375-conj1/notes/check-check-3.py`](targets/ciobanu-2604.22375-conj1/notes/check-check-3.py), [`lean/OpenMath/Ciobanu260422375Conj1.lean`](lean/OpenMath/Ciobanu260422375Conj1.lean) (imported in [`lean/OpenMath.lean`](lean/OpenMath.lean) L21).
  - Lean 4 verification: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake env lean OpenMath/Ciobanu260422375Conj1.lean` (`EXIT=0`, zero `sorry`/warnings; `AUDIT.md` §7 statement-fidelity & kernel audit PASS by `@audit-1`, proving `vpa_empty_stack_on_mc`, `vplForall_finiteIndex_stabL`, `ciobanu_turaev_conjecture_1`, `vplForall_iff_recognisable`, and `ciobanu_turaev_conjecture_1_symGen` with axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/ciobanu-2604.22375-conj1/notes/check-check-3.py` exited `0` (`PASS`, 0 floats), verifying Lemma 1 & Lemma 2(1) on random VPAs, Case 1 & Case 2 over $(\mathbb{Z}/6\mathbb{Z}) \times S_3$, and negative sanity on $G = \mathbb{Z}$; re-audit (`@audit-3`, `2026-11-27T10:11Z`) passed against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2604.22375v1/Arxiv_version.tex` and the compiled PDF.
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `10:12Z`–`10:13Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `watch/snapshots/` (`618` files), live arXiv (`2604.22375v1` sole version; DLT 2026 Springer proceedings `10.1007/978-3-032-28404-4_8` paywalled), Zenodo API (`0` hits), `gh search repos/issues/prs` (`0` hits), and web search (`0` hits).

### Reyes–Dalfó–Fiol `arXiv:2310.16929v1` Conjecture 3.2 (`conjec-creix-alfa`)
- **Statement**: In Section 3 of *On the spectra and spectral radii of token graphs* (`arXiv:2310.16929v1`; *Bol. Soc. Mat. Mex.* 30 (2024), Art. 11, `DOI: 10.1007/s40590-023-00583-3`), Reyes, Dalfó, and Fiol conjecture (**Conjecture 3.2**, `\label{conjec-creix-alfa}`) that for any graph $G$ on $n$ vertices, the $k$-algebraic connectivities $\alpha_k(G) := \min(\mathrm{sp} F_k(G) \setminus \mathrm{sp} F_{k-1}(G))$ (for the Laplacian $L = D - A$ of the $k$-token graph $F_k(G)$) satisfy $\alpha_1(G) \le \alpha_2(G) \le \cdots \le \alpha_{\lfloor n/2 \rfloor}(G)$.
- **Status**: Refuted (`machine-checked counterexample in Lean 4`, upgraded from `counterexample, verified by exact computation`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - The simple connected $6$-cycle $G = C_6$ ($n = 6$, $\lfloor n/2 \rfloor = 3$) has exact Laplacian spectral factorizations $Q_1(x) = P_1/P_0 = (x-1)^2(x-3)^2(x-4)$, $Q_2(x) = P_2/P_1 = (x-2)(x-5)^2(x^2-10x+20)(x^2-7x+8)^2$, and $Q_3(x) = P_3/P_2 = (x-4)^2(x-6)(x^2-10x+12)$, yielding $\alpha_1(C_6) = 1$, $\alpha_2(C_6) = \frac{7-\sqrt{17}}{2}$, and $\alpha_3(C_6) = 5 - \sqrt{13}$.
  - Since neither $\frac{7-\sqrt{17}}{2}$ nor $5 - \sqrt{13}$ appears in a lower token-graph spectrum, $\alpha_2$ and $\alpha_3$ agree under both multiset difference and set difference.
  - Pure integer algebra proves $\alpha_3(C_6) < \alpha_2(C_6)$: $5 - \sqrt{13} < \frac{7-\sqrt{17}}{2} \iff 3 + \sqrt{17} < 2\sqrt{13} \iff 3\sqrt{17} < 13 \iff 9\cdot 17 = 153 < 169 = 13^2$.
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/reyes-2310.16929-conj3.2/TARGET.md`](targets/reyes-2310.16929-conj3.2/TARGET.md), [`targets/reyes-2310.16929-conj3.2/COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`](targets/reyes-2310.16929-conj3.2/COUNTEREXAMPLE.md), [`targets/reyes-2310.16929-conj3.2/AUDIT.md`](targets/reyes-2310.16929-conj3.2/AUDIT.md), [`targets/reyes-2310.16929-conj3.2/notes/check-check-1.py`](targets/reyes-2310.16929-conj3.2/notes/check-check-1.py), [`lean/OpenMath/Reyes231016929Conj32.lean`](lean/OpenMath/Reyes231016929Conj32.lean) (imported in [`lean/OpenMath.lean`](lean/OpenMath.lean) L23).
  - Lean 4 verification: `export PATH="$HOME/.elan/bin:$PATH" && cd lean && lake env lean OpenMath/Reyes231016929Conj32.lean` (`EXIT=0`, zero `sorry`/warnings; `AUDIT.md` §4 statement-fidelity & kernel audit PASS by `@audit-3`, proving `charpoly_C6_token0`..`3`, `algConnK_C6_one`..`three`, `alpha3_lt_alpha2_C6`, `refutes_reyes_conjecture_3_2_C6`, and `not_reyes_conjecture_3_2` with axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/reyes-2310.16929-conj3.2/notes/check-check-1.py` exited `0` (`PASS`, 0 float constants), verifying $F_0..F_3(C_6)$, exact `ZZ[x]` characteristic polynomials $P_0 \mid P_1 \mid P_2 \mid P_3$, and exact `sympy.real_roots` isolation $\alpha_3(C_6) < \alpha_2(C_6)$; adversarial audit (`@audit-2`, `2026-11-27T10:13Z`) passed against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2310.16929/Spectral_radius_tokens-revision-arXiv.tex` and verified over the NetworkX graph atlas ($4 \le n \le 7$, $1{,}245$ graphs) that $C_6$ is the unique counterexample on $4 \le n \le 7$.
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `10:13Z`–`10:14:15Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `watch/snapshots/` (`618` files; `the-omega-institute/trureturing` refutes Conjecture 3.3 on $\rho_k$ maxima only), live arXiv (`2310.16929v1`), Zenodo (`0` hits), `gh search repos/issues/prs` (`0` hits), and web search (`0` hits; `watch-5` inspected all 7 S2 citing papers including `2610.00500v1`).
- **What is not established**:
  - Does not classify counterexamples for $n \ge 8$. Live `gh search code` was skipped due to GitHub secondary rate-limit cooldown, and paywalled full texts (`10.1016/j.laa.2024.02.004`, `10.1016/j.disc.2026.115559`) and unindexed private repositories are not covered. ### Hartwig–Shrivastava `arXiv:2611.23687v1` Conjecture 1.4 (`conj_denominator_N-truncated`)
- **Statement**: In Section 1 of *Extremal Projectors and the Virasoro Algebra* (`arXiv:2611.23687v1`), Jonas T. Hartwig and Sagar Shrivastava conjecture (**Conjecture 1.4**, `\label{conj_denominator_N-truncated}`) that for every $N \ge 1$, $D_N(L_0, c) := \prod_{1 \le n \le N} \tau_{-n}(K_n(L_0, c))$ (where $K_n \in \mathbb{Z}[L_0, c]$ is primitive with positive $L_0$-leading coefficient whose roots are Kac's $\varphi_{r,s}$ with $rs=n$, and $\tau_{-n}(L_0) = L_0 - n$) is the least-degree polynomial in $L_0, c$ regularizing the $N$-truncated Virasoro extremal projector $P_N$ ($Q P_N \in U(\mathrm{Vir})$). (Also settles Conjecture 1.5 `\label{conj_denominator_full}` for the full denominator $D$.)
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - By PBW and the shift automorphism $\tau_{-|\lambda|}$ (Lemma 3.1), $\{L_\lambda^\dagger L_\mu\}$ is a free left $\mathbb{C}[L_0,c]$-basis of $U(\mathrm{Vir})$, so $Q P_N \in U(\mathrm{Vir})$ iff $Q b^{(n)}_{\lambda,\mu} \in \mathbb{C}[L_0,c]$ for all $1 \le n \le N$ and $\lambda,\mu \vdash n$.
  - Explicit discriminant and coefficient comparison proves every $K_m$ ($m \ge 1$) is squarefree with constant leading $L_0$-coefficient and all shifted irreducible factors $\tau_{-n}(\varphi_{r,r})$, $\tau_{-n}(P_{r,s})$ are pairwise coprime across $(n, r, s)$.
  - Excluding a finite resultant set $Z_n \subset \mathbb{C}$ for $c$, the level-$m$ Shapovalov kernel at $h_0 = h_{r,s}(c)$ ($rs = m \le n$) is 1-dimensional (spanned by a singular vector $v_0$), and free $U(\mathrm{Vir}^-)$-action gives $\dim \ker S^{(n)}(h_0, c) \ge p(n-m) = \mathrm{ord}_\varepsilon \det S^{(n)}$, forcing Smith normal form exponents $e_i \in \{0,1\}$ and $\ker S^{(n)}(h_0,c) = \mathrm{span}\{L_\nu^\dagger v_0 : \nu \vdash n-m\}$.
  - Inducting on $n$ via $B^{(n)}_{\mathrm{shifted}} S^{(n)} = R^{(n)}$: for $1 \le m < n$, $P_{n-1}(L_\nu^\dagger v_0) = P_{n-m}(L_\nu^\dagger v_0) = 0$ gives $R^{(n)}(h_0,c)\ker S^{(n)}(h_0,c) = 0$, cancelling all $K_m$ poles ($m < n$); for $m = n$, $R^{(n)}(h_0,c) u_0 = -u_0 \neq 0$ forces every irreducible factor of $K_n$ to appear in a denominator of $B^{(n)}_{\mathrm{shifted}}$. Hence $D_N P_N \in U(\mathrm{Vir})$ and $D_N \mid Q$ for every regularizing polynomial $Q \in \mathbb{C}[L_0, c]$.
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/hartwig-2611.23687-conj1.4/TARGET.md`](targets/hartwig-2611.23687-conj1.4/TARGET.md), [`targets/hartwig-2611.23687-conj1.4/PROOF.md`](targets/hartwig-2611.23687-conj1.4/PROOF.md), [`targets/hartwig-2611.23687-conj1.4/AUDIT.md`](targets/hartwig-2611.23687-conj1.4/AUDIT.md), [`targets/hartwig-2611.23687-conj1.4/notes/check-check-3.py`](targets/hartwig-2611.23687-conj1.4/notes/check-check-3.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/hartwig-2611.23687-conj1.4/notes/check-check-3.py` exited `0` (`PASS`), verifying Step 2 primitivity/coprimality & $D_1..D_6$, exact projector $P_1..P_4$ over $\mathbb{Q}(L_0, c)$, Step 3–4 local SNF & $R^{(n)} \ker S^{(n)}$ identities ($n=1..4$ at $c=899/35$), and Step 1 PBW shift ($n=1..4$); adversarial audit (`@audit-2`, `2026-11-27T10:13Z`) passed against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2611.23687/main.tex`.
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `10:13:44Z`–`10:14:15Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `watch/snapshots/` (`618` files; OpenAlex `cited_by=0; S2=0`), live arXiv (`2611.23687v1`), companion repo `sagarsmaths-afk/Virasoro-Extremal-Projector` ($N \le 6$ only), Zenodo (`0` hits), `gh search repos/issues/prs` (`0` hits), and web search (`0` hits).

  - Live `gh search code` was skipped in Gate 3 due to GitHub secondary rate-limit cooldown; unindexed private repositories and paywalled venues not indexing `2611.23687` are not covered. Internal adversarial audit and finite symbolic projector checks ($N \le 4$–$6$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### Yasutomi `arXiv:2609.32450v2` Section 5 Conjecture 5.1 (directory `yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1`)
- **Statement**: In Section 5 (*Conjecture*, Conjecture 5.1; historical folder slug `yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1`) of *On the Ordering and Injectivity of Lagrange Constants Associated with Certain Substitutions* (`arXiv:2609.32450v2`), Shin-ichi Yasutomi conjectures for all integers $1 \le a < b$ and $x, y \in \mathbb{Q} \cap [0, 1]$ with $x < y$ (classified into Types (1), (2), (3) by Section 1 Lemma 1.4 `\label{l1}`) the exact strict ordering between $\mathcal{L}([\phi_{a,b}(G(x))])$ and $\mathcal{L}([\phi_{a,b}(G(y))])$ in both regimes (I) $b < a^2 + 2$ (Types (1),(3) requiring $a \ge 2$) and (II) $b \ge a^2 + 2$.
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - Starting from Theorem 3.5 (`\label{t3}` of `lagrangev2.tex`), expressed $\mathcal{L}([\phi_{a,b}(G(x))]) = L(x, 1)$ as $a_0 + [0; a_1, a_2, \dots] + [0; a_{-1}, a_{-2}, \dots]$, verified the boundary cases $(0,1)$, $x=0<y<1$, and $0<x<y=1$ via exact continued-fraction tail bounds (including $a=1, b=2$ via $\frac{400}{49} > 8$).
  - For $0 < x < y < 1$, decomposed $L(y,1) - L(x,1)$ across Types (1), (2), (3) using convergent matrices $T_{AA}, T_{AB}, T_{SA}, T_{SB}$ built from $A, B, S_b$ and the shared middle matrix $M = \prod_{k=2}^{n-1} \begin{pmatrix}1 & c_k \\ c_k & c_k^2+1\end{pmatrix}$ ($\det M = 1$).
  - Using exact matrix structural bounds (including $a=1$ continued-fraction induction $\alpha, \beta \le \frac{13}{21} < \frac{5}{8}$ and exact denominator $\frac{a}{ab+1} = \frac{1}{a^2+1+1/a}$ at $b=a^2+1$), reduced every case (Type (2) for all $1 \le a < b$; Type (3) and Type (1) in regimes (I) and (II)) to bivariate/univariate polynomial inequalities in $(u, v) \ge 0$ (and $(m_1, m_2, m_3, m_4)$ quadratic forms) whose coefficients are all strictly non-negative with positive constant/leading terms.
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1/TARGET.md`](targets/yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1/TARGET.md), [`targets/yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1/PROOF.md`](targets/yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1/PROOF.md), [`targets/yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1/AUDIT.md`](targets/yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1/AUDIT.md), [`targets/yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1/notes/check-check-3.py`](targets/yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1/notes/check-check-3.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/yasutomi-2609.32450-conj6.1/notes/check-check-3.py` exited `0` (`PASS`, 0 float constants), verifying matrix/convergent identities, all Type (1)–(3) exact polynomial positivity certificates, and 60-digit interval checks of $L(x,1)$ for $\mathrm{den} \le 6$; re-audit (`@audit-2`, `2026-11-27T10:15Z`) passed against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2609.32450/lagrangev2.tex`.
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `10:14:01Z`–`10:15:01Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `watch/snapshots/` (`618` files; OpenAlex `cited_by=0; S2=0`), live arXiv (`2609.32450v2`), Zenodo (`0` hits), `gh search repos/issues/prs` (`0` hits), and web search (`0` hits).

### Ivanov `arXiv:2607.08944v1` Section 1.4 Conjecture 6 (`conj:fleanverts`, directory `ivanov-2607.08944-conj3`)
- **Statement**: In Section 1.4 (*Two Kinds of Primitive Polytopes*, Conjecture 6 `\label{conj:fleanverts}`; historical folder slug `ivanov-2607.08944-conj3`) of *Illuminating Primitive Polytopes* (`arXiv:2607.08944v1`), Illya Ivanov conjectures that a primitive polytope in $\mathbb{E}^d$, $d \ge 4$, has at most $2^d$ vertices, and even fewer if it isn't an affine $d$-cube.
- **Status**: Refuted (`machine-checked counterexample in Lean 4`, upgraded from `counterexample, verified by exact computation`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - Exhibited an explicit $13$-polytope $P \subset \mathbb{R}^{13}$ defined by $24$ halfspaces: the $4$ base simplex inequalities $x_1, x_2, x_3 \le 100$ and $-x_1 - x_2 - x_3 \le 100$, together with $20$ lifted inequalities $y_j \le 1$ and $-y_j + x_1 + x_2 - j x_3 \le 119 + j^2$ ($0 \le j \le 9$).
  - Proved $\mathbf{0} \in \mathrm{int} P$, linear span $\mathbb{R}^{13}$ and positive dependence $\mu = (1, 1, 56, 11, 1, \dots, 1) > \mathbf{0}$ (hence $P$ is a bounded $13$-polytope), and $24$ explicit ray-unboundedness certificates $\mathbf{r}_i$ when any single halfspace is omitted (hence $P$ is primitive with $24$ facets $< 26$, so not an affine $13$-cube).
  - Projecting onto $(x_1, x_2, x_3)$ yields a $3$-polytope $P_x$ with $24$ vertices ($2$ with $r(x)=0$ lifting to $2\cdot 2^{10}=2048$, $4$ with $r(x)=1$ lifting to $4\cdot 2^9=2048$, and $18$ with $r(x)=2$ lifting to $18\cdot 2^8=4608$), giving **$8704 > 8192 = 2^{13}$** distinct full-rank vertices of $P$, refuting both the $\le 2^d$ and strict $< 2^d$ claims.
- **Verification**:

### OEIS `A187430` — Irwin (2017) Catalan Generating Function Transform Conjecture
- **Statement**: For the sequence `A187430` (rev. 55, offset `0,3`), where $a(n)$ counts $n$-step nonnegative walks starting and ending at $0$ using steps in $\{-2, -1, +1, +2\}$, Benedict W. J. Irwin's Jan 13 2017 `Conjecture` asserts that $1 + \sum_{n \ge 0} a(n)\frac{(-1)^n x^{n+1}}{(1-x)^{2n+2}} = C(x)$, the generating function of the Catalan numbers (`A000108`), in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$.
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - From the primary nonnegative-walk recurrence for $w(n, h)$, the bivariate generating function $G(t, u) = \sum_{n \ge 0} \sum_{h=0}^{2n} w(n,h) u^h t^n \in \mathbb{Z}[u][[t]]$ satisfies the kernel equation $K(t, u) G(t, u) = u^2 - t(u+1)A(t) - t u G_1(t)$ with $K(t,u) = u^2 - t(u^4 + u^3 + u + 1)$ and $A(t) = G_0(t)$ (Step 1).
  - Letting $y(t) \in t\mathbb{Z}[[t]]$ be the unique formal series reversion of $f(y) = \frac{y(1-y)^2}{(1-y+y^2)^2}$ and setting $s(t) = \frac{y(t)}{1-y(t)}$, $K(t,u)$ factors exactly as $M(t,u)Q(t,u)$ in $\mathbb{Z}[[t]][u]$ where $M(t,u) = u^2 - s(t)u - y(t)$ is monic of degree $2$; reducing the kernel equation modulo $M(t,u)$ in the free rank-2 $\mathbb{Z}[[t]]$-module $\mathbb{Z}[[t]][u]/(M(t,u))$ and reading the coefficient of $1$ gives $t A(t) = y(t)$ (Step 2).
  - Setting $Y(x) = 1 - C(x) \in x\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$, the Catalan equation $x C(x)^2 = C(x) - 1$ implies $f(Y(x)) = \frac{-x}{(1-x)^2}$, so $1 - C(x) = y\bigl(\frac{-x}{(1-x)^2}\bigr) = -\sum_{n \ge 0} a(n)\frac{(-1)^n x^{n+1}}{(1-x)^{2n+2}}$ in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$ (Step 3).
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/oeis-A187430/TARGET.md`](targets/oeis-A187430/TARGET.md), [`targets/oeis-A187430/PROOF.md`](targets/oeis-A187430/PROOF.md), [`targets/oeis-A187430/AUDIT.md`](targets/oeis-A187430/AUDIT.md), [`targets/oeis-A187430/notes/check-check-2.py`](targets/oeis-A187430/notes/check-check-2.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/oeis-A187430/notes/check-check-2.py` exited `0` (`PASS`, 0 float constants), verifying direct walk DP ($0 \le n \le 32$, reproducing all 29 OEIS rev. 55 terms), Step 1 kernel ($n \le 32$), Step 2 $K=MQ$ & reversion $f(tA(t))=t \bmod t^{33}$, Step 3 modulo $xC^2-C+1$, and Irwin's g.f. equality through $x^{30}$; adversarial audit (`@audit-2`, `2026-11-27T10:18Z`) passed against live OEIS `A187430` (rev. 55).
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `10:16:42Z`–`10:17:21Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `watch/snapshots/` (`618` files incl. `watch/snapshots/zenodo-23112421-oeis-ids.md`), live OEIS rev. 55 (`Conjecture` unchanged), Zenodo API (`0` hits), arXiv (`0` hits), and live GitHub `gh search repos/issues/prs` (`0` hits).
- **What is not established**:
  - Live `gh search code` was skipped in Gate 3 due to GitHub secondary rate-limit cooldown; unindexed private repositories and paywalled papers not mentioning `A187430` in OEIS/arXiv/Zenodo/snapshot indexes are not covered. Internal adversarial audit and finite series/polynomial checks are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### Guo–Lin `arXiv:2412.02932v1` Section 5.2 Conjecture 5.7 (`locond-3`)
- **Statement**: In Section 5.2 (*A positivity conjecture*, Conjecture 5.7 `\label{locond-3}`) of *Schubert polynomials and patterns in permutations* (`arXiv:2412.02932v1`), Peter L. Guo and Zhuowei Lin conjecture that in the pattern-occurrence expansion $\theta_w = 1 + \sum_u d_u p_u(w)$ of the number of monomials $\theta_w = |\mathrm{supp}(\mathfrak{S}_w(x))|$ in the Schubert polynomial $\mathfrak{S}_w(x)$, every permutation $u$ satisfies $d_u \in \mathbb{Z}_{\ge 0}$.
- **Status**: Refuted (`counterexample, verified by exact computation`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - Exhibited the explicit $S_9$ permutation $u = 134562897 = s_2 s_3 s_4 s_5 s_7 s_8$ (Lehmer code $(0,1,1,1,1,0,1,1,0)$), whose Schubert polynomial factors as $\mathfrak{S}_{134562897}(x) = e_4(x_1,\dots,x_5) \cdot e_2(x_1,\dots,x_8)$, giving $\nu_u = \binom{5}{4}\binom{8}{2} = 140$ and exact monomial support count $\theta_u = 15 + 63 + 35 = 113$.
  - Across its $54$ proper distinct standardized subpatterns, exactly $8$ have $d_\sigma \neq 0$ (`132`: $1\cdot 16$, `12453`: $1\cdot 11$, `13254`: $2\cdot 8$, `21453`: $1\cdot 4$, `132564`: $2\cdot 4$, `134265`: $2\cdot 12$, `1342675`: $3\cdot 6$, `13452786`: $4\cdot 4$, summing to $113$), yielding $1 + \sum_{\sigma \neq u} d_\sigma p_\sigma(u) = 114$ and hence $d_{134562897} = 113 - 114 = -1 < 0$ (while $c_u = 0$). Combined with Guo–Lin's verification for $m \le 8$ (`main.tex` L1370), $n = 9$ is the minimal permutation size of a counterexample.
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/guo-2412.02932-conj5.7/TARGET.md`](targets/guo-2412.02932-conj5.7/TARGET.md), [`targets/guo-2412.02932-conj5.7/COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`](targets/guo-2412.02932-conj5.7/COUNTEREXAMPLE.md), [`targets/guo-2412.02932-conj5.7/AUDIT.md`](targets/guo-2412.02932-conj5.7/AUDIT.md), [`targets/guo-2412.02932-conj5.7/notes/check-check-3.py`](targets/guo-2412.02932-conj5.7/notes/check-check-3.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/guo-2412.02932-conj5.7/notes/check-check-3.py` exited `0` (`PASS`, 0 float constants), verifying all of $S_1 \dots S_6$ (matching all 25 nonzero $(c_u, d_u)$ entries of Table 3 and $S_6$ remarks), the full downward pattern closure of $u$, all 54 proper subpatterns, $\theta_u = 113$, $1 + \sum_{\sigma \neq u} d_\sigma p_\sigma(u) = 114$, and $d_u = -1 < 0$; adversarial audit (`@audit-1`) passed against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2412.02932/main.tex` (and independently verified all 65 $S_9$ superpatterns of `13452786`).
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `10:18:57Z`–`10:19:30Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `watch/snapshots/` (`618` files; all 5 S2 citing preprints inspected in Gate 2 and live arXiv `2412.02932v1`), Zenodo (`0` hits), `gh search repos/issues/prs` (`0` hits), and web search (`0` hits).

### OEIS `A186997` — Peter Bala (2016) 2-Adic Valuation Conjectures
- **Statement**: For the sequence `A186997` (rev. 85, offset `0,3`) defined by $A(x) = \sum_{k \ge 0} a(k) x^k = 1 + x A(x)^3 + x^2 A(x)^4 \in \mathbb{Z}[[x]]$, Peter Bala's Aug 02 2016 `Conjectures` assert that (with $C_m = \frac{1}{m+1}\binom{2m}{m}$, `A000108`): (1) $v_2(a(2m+1)) = v_2(C_{2m+1}) = v_2(C_m)$ for all $m \ge 0$, and (2) $v_2(a(2m)) = 3 + v_2(C_{m-2})$ for all $m \ge 2$.
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - Setting $W(x) := x A(x^2)$ gives $\frac{W(x)(1 - W(x)^2)}{1 + W(x)^4} = x$ in $\mathbb{Z}[[x]]$; Lagrange inversion yields $(2k+1) a(k) = \sum_{j=0}^k t_j$ with $t_j = (-1)^{k-j} 2^j \binom{2k+1}{j} \binom{2k+1-2j}{k-j}$ (Step 1), where Legendre/Kummer identities give $v_2(t_j) = j + v_2\binom{2k+1}{j} + s_2(k+1-j) - 1$ and $v_2(C_m) = s_2(m+1) - 1$ (Step 2).
  - For odd $k = 2m+1$ ($m \ge 0$), $v_2(t_0) = v_2(C_{2m+1}) = v_2(C_m)$, $v_2(t_1) = 1 + v_2(m+1) + v_2(C_k) > v_2(t_0)$, and for $j \ge 2$, $v_2(t_j) \ge v_2(C_m) + v_2(j!) > v_2(t_0)$, so $v_2(a(2m+1)) = v_2(t_0) = v_2(C_m)$ by ultrametric domination (Step 3).
  - For even $k = 2m$ ($m \ge 2$), $t_0 + t_1 = -k\binom{2k+1}{k}$ satisfies $v_2(t_0 + t_1) = 3 + v_2(C_{m-2}) = s_2(m) + r - 1$ ($r := v_2(m) + 2 \ge 2$). Kummer/Pascal valuations show $v_2(t_j) \ge s_2(m) + r > v_2(t_0+t_1)$ for $2 \le j \le 2^r - 1$ (using $j - s_2(j) - \max(v_2(j), v_2(j-1)) \ge 0$), and $v_2(t_j) \ge s_2(m) + r + 1 > v_2(t_0+t_1)$ for $j \ge 2^r$, yielding $v_2(a(2m)) = 3 + v_2(C_{m-2})$ for all $m \ge 2$ (Step 4).
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/oeis-A186997/TARGET.md`](targets/oeis-A186997/TARGET.md), [`targets/oeis-A186997/PROOF.md`](targets/oeis-A186997/PROOF.md), [`targets/oeis-A186997/AUDIT.md`](targets/oeis-A186997/AUDIT.md), [`targets/oeis-A186997/notes/check-check-3.py`](targets/oeis-A186997/notes/check-check-3.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/oeis-A186997/notes/check-check-3.py` exited `0` (`PASS`, 0 floats), verifying $a(0)..a(250)$, $(2k+1)a(k)=\sum_j t_j$, ultrametric domination through $k \le 250$ ($m \le 124$) and $j - s_2(j) - v \ge 0$ ($2 \le j \le 1000$), and $W(x)(1-W(x)^2) = x(1+W(x)^4) \bmod x^{60}$; adversarial audit (`@audit-3`, `2026-11-27T10:19Z`) passed against live OEIS `A186997` (rev. 85).
  - Novelty check: Gate 3 receipts (`notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`, `10:19:57Z`–`10:20:00Z`, `<1h` before reporting) cleared `watch/snapshots/` (`618` files incl. `watch/snapshots/zenodo-23112421-oeis-ids.md`), live OEIS rev. 85 (`Conjectures` unchanged), arXiv (`0` hits), Zenodo API (`0` hits), and live GitHub `gh search repos/issues` (`0` hits).
- **What is not established**:
  - Live `gh search code` was skipped in Gate 3 due to GitHub secondary rate-limit cooldown; unindexed private repositories and paywalled papers not mentioning `A186997` in OEIS/arXiv/Zenodo/snapshot indexes are not covered. ### OEIS `A158960` — M. F. Hasler (2014) Denominators of `Hermite(n, 1/5)` Conjecture
- **Statement**: For the sequence `A158960` (rev. 28, offset `0,2`) giving the numerator of the physicists' Hermite polynomial $H_n(1/5)$ (where $H_0(x)=1, H_1(x)=2x, H_n(x) = 2x H_{n-1}(x) - 2(n-1)H_{n-2}(x)$ for $n \ge 2$), M. F. Hasler's Feb 16 2014 `COMMENTS` conjecture that the reduced denominator of $H_n(1/5)$ is $5^n$ (`A000351(n)`) (and hence the numerator is $a(n) = 5^n H_n(1/5)$) for all $n \ge 0$.
- **Status**: Proved (`machine-checked proof in Lean 4`, upgraded from `written proof, internally audited`).
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - Setting $b(n) := 5^n H_n(1/5)$ yields $b(0) = 1$, $b(1) = 2$, and $b(n) = 2 b(n-1) - 50(n-1) b(n-2) \in \mathbb{Z}$ for all $n \ge 2$, so $H_n(1/5) = b(n)/5^n$.
  - Reducing modulo $5$ ($50 \equiv 0 \pmod 5$) gives $b(n) \equiv 2 b(n-1) \pmod 5$ for all $n \ge 2$, hence $b(n) \equiv 2^n \not\equiv 0 \pmod 5$ for all $n \ge 0$. Thus $\gcd(b(n), 5^n) = 1$ and $b(n)/5^n$ is in lowest terms for every $n \ge 0$.
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/oeis-A158960/TARGET.md`](targets/oeis-A158960/TARGET.md), [`targets/oeis-A158960/PROOF.md`](targets/oeis-A158960/PROOF.md), [`targets/oeis-A158960/AUDIT.md`](targets/oeis-A158960/AUDIT.md), [`targets/oeis-A158960/notes/check-check-2.py`](targets/oeis-A158960/notes/check-check-2.py), [`lean/OpenMath/OeisA158960.lean`](lean/OpenMath/OeisA158960.lean) (imported in [`lean/OpenMath.lean`](lean/OpenMath.lean) L19).

  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/oeis-A158960/notes/check-check-2.py` exited `0` (`PASS`), verifying exact `Fraction` evaluations of $H_n(1/5)$, $b(n) \equiv 2^n \pmod 5$, reduced denominator $5^n$ for $0 \le n \le 100$, Greubel's explicit formula ($n \le 40$), and all 18 live OEIS rev. 28 terms; adversarial audit (`@audit-1`) passed against live OEIS `A158960` (rev. 28).

### Mbirika–Schrader–Spilker `arXiv:2301.05758v3` Section 6.1 Question 30
- **Statement**: In Section 6.1 (*GCD of sums of $k$ consecutive squares*, Question 30) of *Pell and associated Pell braid sequences as GCDs of sums of $k$ consecutive Pell, balancing, and related numbers* (`arXiv:2301.05758v3`, *J. Integer Sequences* 26(6) 2023 Art. 23.6.4), aBa Mbirika, Janee Schrader, and Jürgen Spilker ask for closed forms for $\mathcal{G}_B^2(k), \mathcal{G}_C^2(k), \mathcal{G}_b^2(k), \mathcal{G}_c^2(k)$ (for balancing $B_n$, Lucas-balancing $C_n$, cobalancing $b_n$, Lucas-cobalancing $c_n$, from Definitions 3, 6, and 7) and record three Mathematica-supported observations for all $k \ge 1$: (1) $\mathcal{G}_B^2(k) = \frac{1}{2}\mathcal{G}_C^2(k)$ ($k$ even) or $\mathcal{G}_C^2(k)$ ($k$ odd); (2) $\mathcal{G}_C^2(k) = \mathcal{G}_c^2(k)$ ($k \not\equiv 3 \pmod 6$) or $\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{G}_c^2(k)$ ($k \equiv 3 \pmod 6$); (3) $\mathcal{G}_b^2(k) \equiv 0 \pmod 4$.
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`) — exact closed forms for all four GCD sequences and all three observations proved for every $k \ge 1$.
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - **Consecutive-square sum identities (Lemma 1 & Corollary 2)**: Derived from Binet formulas exact closed expressions for $S_X(k, n) = \sum_{i=1}^k X_{n+i}^2$ ($X \in \{C, B, c, b\}$) in terms of $B_k, P_k, Q_k$ and shifted terms $C_{2n+k+1}, C_{2n+k}, Q_{2n+k}, P_{2n+k}$.
  - **Periodicity & $p$-adic structure (Lemmas 3–4)**: Using determinant-$1$ companion matrices, proved pure periodicity modulo any $M \ge 2$ of the even/odd subsequences, together with modular congruences and exact $2$-, $3$-, and $7$-adic valuations (including $B_k \equiv 3k \pmod{2^{a+4}}$, $B_k \equiv 51k \pmod{2^{a+6}}$, and $P_k \equiv 3k \pmod{2^{a+3}}$ for $4 \mid k$).
  - **Exact closed forms & observations (Theorems A–D)**: Proved unconditionally for all $k \ge 1$:
    - $\mathcal{G}_C^2(k) = \gcd(k, B_k)$;
    - $\mathcal{G}_B^2(k) = \frac{1}{2}\gcd(k, B_k)$ ($k$ even) and $\gcd(k, B_k)$ ($k$ odd) (Observation 1);
    - $\mathcal{G}_c^2(k) = \gcd(k, 3B_k) = \gcd(k, B_k)$ ($k \not\equiv 3 \pmod 6$) and $3\gcd(k, B_k)$ ($k \equiv 3 \pmod 6$) (Observation 2);
    - $\mathcal{G}_b^2(k) = 4\gcd\!\left(B_k, \frac{B_k - P_k}{4}, \frac{B_k + 5k - 8P_k}{64}\right)$ ($k$ even) and $4\gcd\!\left(B_k, \frac{B_k - Q_k}{2}, \frac{3B_k + 5k - 8Q_k}{64}\right)$ ($k$ odd), establishing $\mathcal{G}_b^2(k) \equiv 0 \pmod 4$ (with $\nu_2(\mathcal{G}_b^2(k)) = \nu_2(k)+1$ for $k$ even and $2$ for $k$ odd; Observation 3).
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/mbirika-2301.05758-ques30/TARGET.md`](targets/mbirika-2301.05758-ques30/TARGET.md), [`targets/mbirika-2301.05758-ques30/PROOF.md`](targets/mbirika-2301.05758-ques30/PROOF.md), [`targets/mbirika-2301.05758-ques30/AUDIT.md`](targets/mbirika-2301.05758-ques30/AUDIT.md), [`targets/mbirika-2301.05758-ques30/notes/check-check-2.py`](targets/mbirika-2301.05758-ques30/notes/check-check-2.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/mbirika-2301.05758-ques30/notes/check-check-2.py` exited `0` (`PASS`), verifying all 6 sequences, Lemma 1, Corollary 2 ($1 \le k \le 50, 0 \le n \le 20$), Lemma 4 valuations, Theorems A–D, all 4 closed forms, and all 3 Question 30 observations across $1 \le k \le 120$; re-audit (`@audit-1`) passed against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2301.05758/aBa_Janee_Jurgen_JIS_final_published_version_for_arXiv.tex`.

### Caicedo–Juhnke–Poullot `arXiv:2508.18890v4` Section 3 Conjecture 3.4 (directory `caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3`)
- **Statement**: In Section 3 (*Ehrhart non-positivity*, Conjecture 3.4; historical folder slug `caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3`) of *Ehrhart non-positivity and unimodular triangulations for classes of $\mathbf{s}$-lecture hall simplices* (`arXiv:2508.18890v4`, `Ehrhart_non_positivity.tex` lines 109–111), Jhon B. Caicedo, Martina Juhnke, and Germain Poullot conjecture that for $n \ge 5$, if the $\mathbf{s}$-lecture hall simplex $\mathcal{P}_n^{(a,\dots,a,a+1)}$ is Ehrhart non-positive for some $a \in \mathbb{N}$, then for all $b \ge a$, $\mathcal{P}_n^{(b,\dots,b,b+1)}$ is also Ehrhart non-positive.
- **Status**: Proved (`written proof, internally audited`) — proved unconditionally for all $n \ge 5$ and $b \ge a \ge 1$.
- **Proof / Counterexample summary**:
  - **Exact Ehrhart & generating function (Lemma 1)**: Proved $\mathcal{L}_n(t,a) = \binom{at+n}{n} + \sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\binom{a\ell+n-1}{n-1}$ (disclosing the line 88 misprint in the preprint) and $\sum_{n \ge k} ([t^k]\mathcal{L}_n(t,a)) x^n = \frac{(a u(x))^k}{k!} F_a(x)$ ($u(x) = -\ln(1-x)$, $F_a(x) = \frac{1}{1-x} + \frac{x(1-x)^{a-1}}{1-(1-x)^a}$).
  - **Small $a$ & finite $n$ regimes (Lemmas 2–3)**: Proved positivity of all Ehrhart coefficients for $1 \le a \le 6$ and all $n \ge 1$ via $f_{a,n} \ge 1 - \frac{1}{a}\sum_{m=1}^{a-1} \frac{1}{2\sin(\pi m/a)} > 0$ (Lemma 2). For $5 \le n \le 32$, exact rational Sturm/interval isolation on the polynomials $[t^k]\mathcal{L}_n(t,a) \in \mathbb{Q}[a]$ establishes exact integer thresholds $m_n \in [8, 16]$ above which non-positivity holds for all $a \ge m_n$ (Lemma 3).
  - **Large-$a$ quartic & residue-contour regimes (Lemmas 4–5)**: For $n \ge 5$ and $a \ge 2n^{4/3}$, proved $[t^{n-4}]\mathcal{L}_n(t,a) < 0$ from its exact degree-4 polynomial in $a$ (Lemma 4). For $n \ge 33$ and $8 \le a < 2n^{4/3}$, isolated the conjugate poles $x_1, \overline{x_1}$ ($|x_1|=2\sin(\pi/a)$) across $k \in \{1,2,3,4\}$ so that $\cos(-n\theta_a - k\pi/2) \ge 1/\sqrt{2}$, bounding the remainder via direct series decay for $a \in \{8,9,10\}$ (Case 5a) and a Cauchy contour on $|x| = R_a = 3\sin(\pi/a)$ with uniform $|1 - (1-x)^a| > 0.85$ and explicit monotonicity in $a \ge 11, n \ge 33$ (Case 5b), forcing $[t^k]\mathcal{L}_n(t,a) < 0$ (Lemma 5).
- **Verification**:
  - Artifacts: [`targets/caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3/TARGET.md`](targets/caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3/TARGET.md), [`targets/caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3/PROOF.md`](targets/caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3/PROOF.md), [`targets/caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3/AUDIT.md`](targets/caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3/AUDIT.md), [`targets/caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3/notes/check-check-1.py`](targets/caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3/notes/check-check-1.py).
  - Independent check: `.venv/bin/python -u targets/caicedo-2508.18890-conj3.3/notes/check-check-1.py` exited `0` (`PASS`, 0 floats), verifying Lemmas 1–5 with pure `int`/`Fraction`, SymPy `Poly.intervals` Sturm isolation ($5 \le n \le 32$), and 50-digit `mpmath.iv` interval arithmetic; re-audit (`@audit-1`) passed against `tools/.arxiv_cache/eprint/2508.18890/Ehrhart_non_positivity.tex`.

### `targets/kaul-2112.05316-ques3/` — Refutation of Kaul–Maxfield–Mudrock–Thomason Clique-Gluing DP Color Function Questions 2 & 3

- **Claim**: `arXiv:2112.05316v2` (*Enumerative Combinatorics and Applications* 4:2 (2024), Art. S2R11, Section 1.3–1.4, Question 2 `ques: classify` & Question 3 `ques: easier`) asks whether every $G$-preserving $(m, K_p)$-gluing of $K_{p+1}$ with a graph $G$ containing a vertex $v$ of degree $d_G(v) = p$ satisfies $P_{DP}(G', m) = P_{DP}(G, m)$ when $m \ge p+1$. **Refuted** for all $d_G(v) = p \ge 4$ (and hence Question 2 for all $p \ge 4$) at $m = p+1$: taking $G_0^{(p)} = K_{p-2} \vee K_{2,p}$ and tessellating along its $2p$ $p$-cliques $K_1, \dots, K_{2p}$ yields $G^* = G_{2p}$ with $d_{G^*}(v) = p$ for $v \in V(K_{2,p})$ of size $2$, while $P_{DP}(G^*, p+1) < P_{DP}(G_0^{(p)}, p+1)$.

### `targets/oeis-A119262/` — Proof of OEIS A119262 Hessenberg Determinant Conjecture

- **Claim**: OEIS [`A119262`](https://oeis.org/A119262) (rev. 55), Benedict W. J. Irwin (Apr 19 2017) conjecture: for $n \ge 1$, the $n \times n$ matrix $M_n$ satisfies $\det(M_n) = a(n+1)$. Proved for all $n \ge 1$ by index reversal $r = n-i, c = n-j$ to a fixed infinite upper-Hessenberg matrix $B$ ($B_n = P_n M_n P_n^{-1}$), last-column Laplace expansion giving $d_n = \sum_{r=0}^{n-1} \binom{n-1-r}{r} d_r$ via the block upper-triangular minor $\det B_n^{(r,n-1)} = (-1)^{n-1-r} d_r$, and strong induction showing $d_n = a(n+1)$.
