### `targets/oeis-A379459/` — Proof of OEIS A379459 / A006351 Kurkov (2024) Antidiagonal Array Conjecture (`A379459(n, 0) = A006351(n + 2)`)

- **Claim**: OEIS [`A379459`](https://oeis.org/A379459) (rev. 4, offset `0,1`), downward-antidiagonal array $A_{459}(0, k) = 2(k+1)!$ and $A_{459}(n, k) = A_{459}(n-1, k+1) + 2(k+1)!\sum_{j=0}^k A_{459}(n-1, j)/j!$ ($n \ge 1, k \ge 0$), together with [`A006351`](https://oeis.org/A006351) (rev. 195, e.g.f. $B(x)$ = compositional inverse of $2\ln(1+t)-t$). Mikhail Kurkov (Dec 23 2024 / Jan 16 2025) conjectured $A_{459}(n, 0) = \mathrm{A006351}(n + 2)$ for all $n \ge 0$ (equivalently $\mathrm{A006351}(m) = A_{459}(m - 2, 0)$ for $m > 1$). *(Scope note: the sibling `A379460(n, 0) = A006351(n + 1)` equality was settled prior to start in `insorrowmeow/oeis-sequence-proofs` `62bc090`, `2026-11-27T11:08:09Z`, `proofs/research-batch-09/YokeRows.{md,lean}` and is NOT claimed here.)* **Proved unconditionally for all $n \ge 0$**: the row EGFs $H_n(y) = \sum_{k \ge 0} A_{459}(n, k)y^k/k!$ satisfy $H_0(y) = \frac{2}{(1-y)^2}$ and $H_n(y) = \partial_y\bigl(\frac{1+y}{1-y} H_{n-1}(y)\bigr)$, so $W(x, y) := \frac{1+y}{1-y}\sum_{n \ge 0} H_n(y)\frac{x^n}{n!}$ uniquely solves $\partial_x W = \frac{1+y}{1-y}\partial_y W$ ($W(0, y) = \frac{2(1+y)}{(1-y)^3}$); characteristics via $\Phi(\eta) = \Phi(y) + x$ ($\Phi(u) = 2\ln(1+u) - u$) yield $W(x, y) = \frac{2(1+\eta)}{(1-\eta)^3}$, and at $y = 0$ ($\eta(x, 0) = B(x)$) $W(x, 0) = B''(x) = \sum_{n \ge 0} \mathrm{A006351}(n+2)\frac{x^n}{n!}$.
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`
- **Result artifacts**: [`PROOF.md`](targets/oeis-A379459/PROOF.md), [`AUDIT.md`](targets/oeis-A379459/AUDIT.md), [`verify.py`](targets/oeis-A379459/verify.py), [`check-check-2.md`](targets/oeis-A379459/notes/check-check-2.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`](targets/oeis-A379459/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`](targets/oeis-A379459/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`](targets/oeis-A379459/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md)

### `targets/alexandersson-2609.05131-prob10.1/` — Proof of Alexandersson (`arXiv:2609.05131v2`) Problem 10.1 (Consecutive Noncrossing Chow Polynomial Interlacing)

- **Claim**: Per Alexandersson, [`arXiv:2609.05131v2`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05131) (2026-09-23), Section 10 (*Further questions*), **Problem 10.1** (`\label{prob:consecutiveChowInterlacing}`): do $H_{\mathrm{NC}_{n+1}}(t)$ and $H_{\mathrm{NC}_{n+2}}(t)$ interlace for every $n$? **Proved unconditionally for every $n \ge 0$ (strict interlacing $\beta_n < \alpha_{n-1} < \beta_{n-1} < \dots < \alpha_1 < \beta_1 < 0$ for every $n \ge 2$)**: base cases $n = 0, 1$ are immediate ($H_{\mathrm{NC}_1} = H_{\mathrm{NC}_2} = 1$, $H_{\mathrm{NC}_3} = 1+t$); for $1 \le n \le 80$, exact integer $\gamma$-expansion and signed primitive Sturm–Cauchy sequences verify $I_{-\infty}^0(\Gamma_n/\Gamma_{n+1}) = \lfloor n/2 \rfloor$ (`Proposition 2.1`); for all $n \ge 80$, two analytic representations of $C_n(-s)$—an alternating Fuss–Catalan series (`Lemma 3.1`, Regime A, $1 \le k \le \lfloor\sqrt{n+1}/2.2\rfloor$) with Robbins–Stirling pair bounds (`Lemma 5.1`, `Prop. 5.2`) and a Möbius residue $w$-sum (`Lemma 3.2`, Regime B, $\lfloor\sqrt{n+1}/2.2\rfloor+1 \le j \le \lfloor(n-1)/2\rfloor$) with Euler-product phase-shift bounds (`Lemmas 4.1–4.3`, `Prop. 4.4`)—certify alternating signs at strictly interlaced sample points across both regimes via 35–40-digit outward-rounded `mpmath.iv` arithmetic (`§6`).
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`
- **Result artifacts**: [`PROOF.md`](targets/alexandersson-2609.05131-prob10.1/PROOF.md), [`AUDIT.md`](targets/alexandersson-2609.05131-prob10.1/AUDIT.md), [`verify.py`](targets/alexandersson-2609.05131-prob10.1/verify.py), [`check-check-3.md`](targets/alexandersson-2609.05131-prob10.1/notes/check-check-3.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`](targets/alexandersson-2609.05131-prob10.1/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`](targets/alexandersson-2609.05131-prob10.1/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`](targets/alexandersson-2609.05131-prob10.1/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md)

### `targets/dochtermann-2609.13361-conj7.5/` — Proof of Dochtermann–Huang–Mawhinney–Oh–Xu (`arXiv:2609.13361v1`) Conjectures 7.5 & 7.4 and Cographic Characterization

- **Claim**: Anton Dochtermann, Evan Huang, Kai Mawhinney, Suho Oh, and Yuchen Xu, [`arXiv:2609.13361v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13361) (2026-09-11), Section 7 (*Further questions*), **Conjecture 7.5** (`\label{conj:C>=3 ind}`): if $M$ is a matroid with circuit system $\mathcal{C}$ and $\mathcal{C}_{[g]} \in \mathrm{Cir}(M)$, then $\mathcal{C}_{\ge 3}$ is independent. **Proved unconditionally in strictly stronger form (`Main Theorem`: $\mathcal{C}_{\ge 3} = \emptyset$), also resolving Conjecture 7.4 (`\label{conj:unique}`, `Corollary 1`) and characterizing connected circuit systems with $\mathcal{C}_{[g]} \in \mathrm{Cir}(M)$ as vertex-star systems of connected loopless graphs $M = M(G)^*$ (`Corollary 2`)**: by strong induction on $|E(M)|$ using binary linear independence (`Lemma 1`), disconnected-component reduction (`Step 1`), contraction $M/x$ at $x \in \mathcal{C}_{[g]}$ (`Lemma 2`), `lem:circuitsys` when $M/x$ is connected (`Case 1`), and when $M/x = W_1 \oplus \dots \oplus W_k$ is disconnected (`Case 2`, `thm:components`), showing $x \in C_1 \subseteq E(W_1) \cup \{x\}$, $D \triangle C_1 \in \mathrm{Cir}(M \setminus x)$ via parallel-connection circuit elimination (`Lemma 3`), and $*$-deletion along $x$.

### `targets/fioravanti-2602.04034-conj59/` — Proof of Fioravanti–Kompatscher–Rossi (`arXiv:2602.04034v1`) Conjecture 59 (`conjecture:strong`) and Conjecture 1 (`conjecture:main`)

- **Claim**: Stefano Fioravanti, Michael Kompatscher, and Bernardo Rossi, *Clonoids over vector spaces*, [`arXiv:2602.04034v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04034) (2026-02-03), Section 7 (*Conclusion*), **Conjecture 59** (`\label{conjecture:strong}`): for every finite abelian $p$-group $\mathbf{A}$ and ring $\mathbf{R}$ with $p \cdot 1_R \in R^\times$, $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{A}, \mathbf{R}}$ is uniformly generated by $k$-ary $(\mathbf{A}, \mathbf{R})$-minors for some $k \in \mathbb{N}$; and Section 1 (*Introduction*), **Conjecture 1** (`\label{conjecture:main}`): for finite modules $\mathbf{A}, \mathbf{B}$, there are only finitely many clonoids from $\mathbf{A}$ to $\mathbf{B}$ iff $\gcd(|\mathbf{A}|, |\mathbf{B}|) = 1$. **Both conjectures proved unconditionally with explicit linear arity bound $k(\mathbf{A}) = \max(1, \log_p |\mathbf{A}|)$**: (`Theorem 1`) strong induction on $\log_p |\mathbf{A}|$ over $S = \mathbb{Z}[1/p]$ using Theorem 49 (`theorem:main`) for $e = 1$, Lemma 2 subgroup peeling across maximal subgroups $C_1, \dots, C_L$, codimension-$s$ matrix lifting $M(\widetilde{B}, U', W') \in R_k^{m, m}(\mathbf{A})$ (`Step 2`, collapsing both Case 1 full-rank $\mathbf{T}$ and Case 2 rank-deficient $\mathbf{T}$), exact $q$-Pascal / Cauchy higher-codimension subspace Radon inversion (`Lemma 1`, `Step 3`), Theorem 18 (`theorem:unigen`), and central ring homomorphism $\iota \colon \mathbb{Z}[1/p] \to Z(\mathbf{R})$; (`Theorem 2`) CRT clone factorization $\mathrm{Clo}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{ab}}) = \prod_j \mathrm{Clo}(\mathbf{A}_{p_j})$ + Proposition 21 (`proposition:ugproduct`) + Corollary 35 (`corollary:orbitsupport`) + Corollary 20/23 (`corollary:unigen`/`corollary:fingen`), combined with Mayr–Wynne (2024) `Theorem 1.3` (`thm:notcoprime`) for the non-coprime converse. *(Note: `Hwzw/algebra-number-theory-drafts` `P32` only settled Question 60 $\mathbb{Z}/p^2\mathbb{Z} \oplus (\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^s$ and explicitly left multiple $\mathbb{Z}/p^2\mathbb{Z}$ factors, higher exponents, Conjecture 59, and Conjecture 1 open.)*

### `targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.3/` — Proof of Vasilyev (`arXiv:2611.20344v2`) Question 7.3 (`q:types`, Orbit Type $(\infty, 1)$)

- **Claim**: Oleg Vasilyev, *Up or down at every integer: Collatz-type maps of arbitrary sets and the up-down graph*, [`arXiv:2611.20344v2`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20344) (2026-11-18, `updown.tex`), Section 7 (*Questions*), **Question 7.3** (`\label{q:types}`, referencing **Remark 4.4** `\label{rem:types}`): for $U(x) = \lceil 3x/2 \rceil$, $D(x) = \lceil x/2 \rceil$, and $T_S(x) = U(x)$ ($x \in S$) or $D(x)$ ($x \notin S$), *"Is there a map with infinitely many cycles and a divergent orbit?"* **Answered YES unconditionally, with exact orbit type $(k, l) = (\infty, 1)$**: (`Lemma 1`, Closed-Box Trap) whenever $B \equiv 5 \pmod 6$ ($B \ge 5$), $U(\mathbb{N} \setminus R) \subseteq \mathbb{N} \setminus R$, and $R \cap [B+1, U(B)-1] = \emptyset$, the box $V(B) = [B+1, 3B-1] \setminus (R \cup \{2B\})$ is forward-invariant under $U$ on $[B+1, 2B-1]$ and $D$ on $[2B+1, 3B-1]$ and contains a simple cycle $C(B)$ disjoint from $R$; (`Lemma 2`, Admissible Ray $U$-Invariance) any ray $R$ from $1$ using only $U$-steps and $D$-steps at $x \equiv 2 \pmod 6$ (landing in $1 \pmod 3 \notin \mathrm{Im}(U)$) satisfies $U(\mathbb{N} \setminus R) \subseteq \mathbb{N} \setminus R$; and (`Lemma 3`, Admissible Stage Extension) recursively extends any head $H \ge 5$ past a trap anchor $B \equiv 5 \pmod 6$ with $P \cap [B+1, U(B)-1] = \emptyset$ and $H' > 3B$ via $2$-adic parity flipping (`Subcase B2`) and $v_2(5q+3)$ descent (`Subcase B3b`), yielding $S = \{r_t : t \ge 0\} \cup \bigcup_{m \ge 1} \{x \in C(B_m) : x \le 2B_m - 1\}$ with infinitely many disjoint cycles and a unique divergent orbit class.
- **Label**: `"machine-checked proof in Lean 4"`
- **Result artifacts**: [`PROOF.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.3/PROOF.md), [`AUDIT.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.3/AUDIT.md), [`Vasilyev261120344Q73.lean`](lean/OpenMath/Vasilyev261120344Q73.lean), [`verify.py`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.3/verify.py), [`check-check-2.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.3/notes/check-check-2.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.3/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.3/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.3/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md)

### `targets/teheux-2611.12469-q3/` — Proof of Teheux (`arXiv:2611.12469v1`) Question 8.4 (`q:correspondence`) Item (3)

- **Claim**: Bruno Teheux, *Dualities and completions of median algebras and semilattices: a universal-algebraic approach*, [`arXiv:2611.12469v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12469) (2026-11-09, `Notes_definability_pocset.tex`, lines 3066–3085), Section 8, **Question 8.4** (`\label{q:correspondence}`) **item (3)**: *"Does every universal sentence that holds in a median algebra $\mathbf{A}$ hold in $\hat{\mathbf{A}}$, that is, does every finite subalgebra of $\hat{\mathbf{A}}$ embed into $\mathbf{A}$?"* **Answered YES unconditionally**: equivalence via local finiteness (`Theorem 2.4`) and $\varphi_{\mathbf{B}}$ (`Example 7.2`); Case 1 ($\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{A}) = \infty$) embeds $\mathbf{B} \hookrightarrow \mathbf{2}^d \hookrightarrow \mathbf{2}^n \hookrightarrow \mathbf{A}$ by $0$-padding; Case 2 ($\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{A}) = r < \infty$) proves at most $r$ pairwise transverse elements in $\mathbf{X} \setminus \{0, 1\}$ (`Lemma 1` via Helly property + `Lemma 7.1`), finite topological boundary $|A_U \setminus \{0\}| \le r$ and $|B_U| = |A_U| \le r + 1$ (`Lemma 2`), local upward cone neighborhoods $W_{t, U} \cap U \subseteq t^\uparrow$ (`Lemma 3`), and an inductive bottom-up / ${}^\bullet$-symmetric clopen retraction $\pi$ (`Steps 0–3`) pulling back every ultrafilter $U \in \mathbf{B} \le (\mathbf{X}^\flat)_+$ to a clopen ultrafilter $\widetilde{U} = \pi^{-1}(U) \in \mathbf{X}_* \cong \mathbf{A}$ (`Theorem 3.4(1)`).
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`
- **Result artifacts**: [`PROOF.md`](targets/teheux-2611.12469-q3/PROOF.md), [`AUDIT.md`](targets/teheux-2611.12469-q3/AUDIT.md), [`verify.py`](targets/teheux-2611.12469-q3/verify.py), [`check-check-3.md`](targets/teheux-2611.12469-q3/notes/check-check-3.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`](targets/teheux-2611.12469-q3/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`](targets/teheux-2611.12469-q3/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`](targets/teheux-2611.12469-q3/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md)

### `targets/kallat-2610.40342-ques14.10/` — Partial/Major Resolution of Kallat (`arXiv:2610.40342v1`) Question 14.10 (`prob:gcdr`)

- **Claim**: Ahaan Kallat, *A $p$-adic Valuation Calculus for Permutations with Restricted Cycle Lengths*, [`arXiv:2610.40342v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40342) (2026-10-29, `paper1_valuation_calculus.tex`, lines 1220–1222), Section 14 (*Column 2 and open questions*), **Question 14.10** (`\label{prob:gcdr}`):
  - **Q1 ($r = 3$: $g_3(n) \ne G_3(n)$ iff $n \equiv 30 \pmod{81}$ with $n \ge 111$)**: **YES** (complete `iff` proved unconditionally for all $n \ge 5$, with $G_3(n)/g_3(n) = 3$; `Theorem 1(d)` and `Theorem 4`).
  - **Q2 ($g_r(n) = G_r(n)$ for every $r \ge 4$ and $n \ge r + 2$)**: **NO (DISPROVED)** via an elementary 4-partition $p = 2$ proof (`Theorem 2`, `Lemmas 2.1–2.2`) showing that for every $64 \le r \le 79$ at $n = 1248$, $v_2(g_r(1248)) = v_2(\Lambda_r(1248)) = 1169 < 1170 = v_2(G_r(1248))$, witnessed explicitly by $S = \mathbb{N} \setminus \{96\}$ and $\{96^{13},\, 96^1 128^9,\, 80^6 96^8,\, 80^8 96^1 128^4\}$.
  - **Q3 ($v_p(g_r(n)/(n-1)_r)$ for $p \le r$) & Q4 (when $\Lambda_r(n) \ne g_r(n)$)**: **Completely solved for $r = 3$** across all $n \ge 5$ (`Theorem 1(a)–(c)`, `Theorems 3–4`): $v_2(g_3(n)/(n-1)_3) = \lfloor(n-2)/4\rfloor$, $v_3(g_3(n)/(n-1)_3) = \lfloor(n-4)/9\rfloor + \lfloor(n+2)/9\rfloor + \delta_3(n)$, and $\Lambda_3(n) \ne g_3(n) \iff n \equiv 1, 2, 3, 24 \pmod{27}$ with $n \ge 51$ (ratio $g_3(n)/\Lambda_3(n) = 3^{\delta_3(n)} \in \{3, 9\}$).
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`
- **Result artifacts**: [`PROOF.md`](targets/kallat-2610.40342-ques14.10/PROOF.md), [`AUDIT.md`](targets/kallat-2610.40342-ques14.10/AUDIT.md), [`verify.py`](targets/kallat-2610.40342-ques14.10/verify.py), [`check-check-1.md`](targets/kallat-2610.40342-ques14.10/notes/check-check-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`](targets/kallat-2610.40342-ques14.10/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`](targets/kallat-2610.40342-ques14.10/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`](targets/kallat-2610.40342-ques14.10/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md)
- **Verification**:

  - Gate 3 novelty checks in `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, and `notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md` (`2026-11-27T14:53Z`–`14:54Z`, `725` snapshots, `2610.40342v1` sole arXiv version, `rainwangphy/aftd-platform` `6e063af` helper-only, `trureturing` `dev`/Zenodo/GH/web clear) passed; `@audit-1` blind audit in `AUDIT.md` passed.

### `targets/schneider-2611.20267-q7.4/` — Proof of Schneider (`arXiv:2611.20267v1`) Question 7.4

- **Claim**: Scott Schneider, *Borel combinatorics of $\mathbb{N}^\ell$-shift actions and hypersmooth equivalence relations*, [`arXiv:2611.20267v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20267) (2026-11-15, `7-TheGraphsGe1Ge2Gp-CLEAN.tex`, lines 128–130), Section 7, **Question 7.4**: *"Does there exist a continuous finite coloring of $\mathcal{G}_{\bar{e}_1}\restriction X_{\bar{e}_1}$?"* **Answered YES unconditionally for every integer $J \ge 2$**, establishing $\chi_C(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{e}_1}(J)\restriction X_{\bar{e}_1}(J)) = 3$ (matching $\chi_B = 3$ from Proposition 7.3 `prop:ColoringGe1`): using the well-ordering $\prec$ on $\mathbb{N}_+ \times \mathbb{N}$ of order type $\omega$ to locate the $\prec$-least witness $(i(x), j(x))$ with $x(i, j) \ne x(0, j)$, the parity/value selector $h$ defines a continuous shift-equivariant map $H : X_{\bar{e}_1}(J) \to (J+1)^\omega$ whose orbits are never eventually constant (`Step 1`), yielding a continuous $(J+2)$-coloring $\gamma_{J+1} \circ H$ and, via `[KST, 5.2]` $1$-dimensional shift-equivariant descent $g_{J+1}$, an explicit continuous $3$-coloring $c := \gamma_2 \circ g_{J+1} \circ H : X_{\bar{e}_1}(J) \to \{0, 1, 2\}$ (`Step 2`).

### `targets/zhou-2609.23078-conj1/` — Proof of You Zhou (`arXiv:2609.23078v1`) Conjecture 1 / Conjecture 4.1 (`conj:main1-all-types`)

- **Claim**: You Zhou, *Maximal proper extremal subset in the crystal basis of a finite-dimensional irreducible module*, [`arXiv:2609.23078v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23078) (2026-09-19, `MAXIMAL_PROPER_EXTREMAL_SUBSET_IN_THE_CRYSTAL_BASIS_OF_A_FINITE-DIMENSIONAL_IRREDUCIBLE_MODULE.tex`, lines 136–138 and 516–523), Section 1 **Conjecture 1** / Section 4 **Conjecture 4.1** (`\label{conj:main1-all-types}`): for every finite-dimensional complex simple Lie algebra $\mathfrak{g}$ (all types $A_n, B_n, C_n, D_n, E_6, E_7, E_8, F_4, G_2$) and every $\lambda \in P^+$, $\Gamma_\lambda = \mathcal{F}(b_\star) = \mathcal{C}_{b_\star}$, so by Proposition 3.7 (`prop:max-proper-extremal`), $\mathcal{B}(\lambda) \setminus \mathcal{F}(b_\star)$ is the unique maximal proper extremal subset of $\mathcal{B}(\lambda)$. **Proved unconditionally across all finite types**: proves the strictly stronger general tensor dominance monotonicity theorem $H(a \otimes b) \ge H(a \otimes \tilde{f}_i(b))$ (`Theorem 1`) via Lusztig canonical basis positivity + Dynkin folding in $\mathfrak{g}^\vee$ (`Lemma 1`), an exact path-ordered exponential gauge identity $g(\psi_u)(t) = y_j(a(t))\, g(\psi)(t)\, y_j(-b)$ (`Lemma 2`), and Littelmann–Pitman tropical limits (`Lemmas 3–4`), then specializes to $\mathcal{B}(\rho) \otimes \mathcal{B}(\lambda)$ via PRV minimality and cyclic-vector multiplicity-one (`Theorem 2`) to deduce $\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{C}_{b_\star}) \subseteq \mathcal{C}_{b_\star}$ and $\Gamma_\lambda = \mathcal{F}(b_\star) = \mathcal{C}_{b_\star}$.
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`

### `targets/orzechowski-2611.34308-q3.9/` — Proof of Orzechowski (`arXiv:2611.34308v1`) Question 3.9 (`q:prime_residue_field`)

- **Claim**: Kamil Orzechowski, *Measurable equidecomposability and the Banach--Ruziewicz problem over locally compact non-Archimedean valued fields*, [`arXiv:2611.34308v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34308) (dated 2026-11-24, submitted 2026-11-25, `Measurable-equidecompositions-in-Q_p_n.tex`, lines 621–623), Section 3 (*Open problems*), **Question 3.9** (`\label{q:prime_residue_field}`): for every locally compact non-Archimedean valued field $\mathbb{L}$ (of arbitrary characteristic $0$ or $p$) with prime residue field $\mathbb{F}_p$, is $\mathrm{Iso}(\mathbb{L})\curvearrowright (\mathbb{L}, m_{\mathbb{L}})$ expanding? **Answered YES unconditionally**: identifies $(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{L}}, d_{\mathbb{L}}, m_{\mathbb{L}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{L}})^{-1} m_{\mathbb{L}}|_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{L}}})$ isometrically and measure-preservingly via a coset-tree bijection $\Phi$ with the pro-$p$ congruence subgroup $U_0 = K_e = \ker(\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z}_p) \to \mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z}/q\mathbb{Z}))$ ($q = p^e$, $e=1$ for $p \ge 3$, $e=2$ for $p=2$) and extends left-translations $L_g$ by the identity outside $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{L}}$ to surjective isometries $\tilde{\alpha}_g \in \mathrm{Iso}(\mathbb{L})$ (`Steps 1–2`); applies Salehi Golsefidy's spectral gap theorem (`Theorem 2.15`) to $\Gamma = \ker(\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z}) \to \mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z}/q\mathbb{Z}))$ with finite symmetric generating set $S \ni I$ (excluding eigenvalue $-1$ at $p=2$) and `Lemma 2.16` to prove $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{L}}$ is a domain of expansion (`Step 3`); and transfers expansion via transitivity of translations to every $C \in \mathcal{B}_{m_{\mathbb{L}}} \cap \mathcal{C}(\mathrm{Iso}(\mathbb{L}) \curvearrowright \mathbb{L})$ (`Step 4`).
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`
- **Result artifacts**: [`PROOF.md`](targets/orzechowski-2611.34308-q3.9/PROOF.md), [`AUDIT.md`](targets/orzechowski-2611.34308-q3.9/AUDIT.md), [`verify.py`](targets/orzechowski-2611.34308-q3.9/verify.py), [`check-check-1.md`](targets/orzechowski-2611.34308-q3.9/notes/check-check-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`](targets/orzechowski-2611.34308-q3.9/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`](targets/orzechowski-2611.34308-q3.9/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`](targets/orzechowski-2611.34308-q3.9/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md)

### `targets/saikia-2611.28840-conj8.1/` — Proof & Sharpness of Saikia (`arXiv:2611.28840v1`) Conjecture 8.1 (`conj:sharp`)

- **Claim**: Manjil P. Saikia, *Congruences modulo powers of 2 and 3 for Lin's function $\mathrm{PDO}_t(n)$*, [`arXiv:2611.28840v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28840) (2026-11-21, `final.tex`, lines 931–941), Section 8 (*Concluding remarks*), **Conjecture 8.1** (`\label{conj:sharp}`): for every $k \ge 3$, there exists an odd integer $\lambda_k$ such that $\mathrm{PDO}_{\mathrm{t}}(3 \times 2^{k+2} n) \equiv 8\lambda_k\, \mathrm{PDO}_{\mathrm{t}}(3 \times 2^k n) \pmod{2^{\lfloor 7(k+1)/2 \rfloor}}$ for all $n \ge 0$ (`\eqref{eq:sharp}`). **Proved unconditionally for ALL $k \ge 3$, together with exact best-possible sharpness of the modulus $2^{\lfloor 7(k+1)/2 \rfloor}$** (no integer satisfies the congruence modulo $2^{\lfloor 7(k+1)/2 \rfloor + 1}$ for all $n \ge 0$, failing at some $n \in \{1, 2, 3\}$): constructs an explicit $2$-adic Atkin–Lehner involution $W$ on $\mathcal{E}_2(K)$ satisfying $W^2 = I$ and $W A = -A W$ (`Step 2`, `Lemma 2`, `Theorem 3`), builds the $2^2$-contractive eigenvector $\mathbf{u}_2 \in S(\mathbb{Q}_2)$ of $B|_{\ge 2}$ (`B = A^2`, `Lemma 4`) and knocks out $(\mathbf{u}_2 B)_1 = 0$ via $2\times 2$ Schur-complement Weierstrass preparation forcing even nullity (`Step 3`, `Lemmas 5–6`, `Theorem 7`), projects onto $\ker(e_1, e_2)$ via $\mathbf{u}_1 = \mathbf{u}_2(A + 16I)$ with a $2^7$-contraction bound (`Step 4`, `Lemma 8`), and proves `\eqref{eq:sharp}` plus exact sharpness $\nu_2(\text{defect}_3) = \lfloor 7(k+1)/2 \rfloor$ across both parity tracks (`Step 5`, `Propositions 9–10`).
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`

### `targets/ingrosso-2611.30155-q4.6/` — Proof of Ingrosso–Trombetti–Zitouni (`arXiv:2611.30155v1`) Question 4.6

- **Claim**: E. Ingrosso, M. Trombetti, and A. Zitouni, *Groups with dual classes of subgroups*, [`arXiv:2611.30155v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30155) (2026-11-23, `pronormali_arxiv.tex`, lines 784–786; *J. Aust. Math. Soc.* DOI:`10.1017/s1446788726101682`), Section 4, **Question 4.6**: what is the smallest possible defect of subnormality in Theorem 4.5 (`\label{sip}`)? **Answered unconditionally: exact smallest possible defect is $2$** (improving Theorem 4.5's bound of $7$ and dropping the locally graded hypothesis for the upper bound): proves $P_G(H) = N_G(H)$ for any subnormal subgroup $H \le G$ in any group (`Lemma 1`) and $G$-conjugation invariance of the pro-norm $N_{\mathrm{pro}}(G)$ (`Lemma 2`), deducing $H \trianglelefteq H^G \trianglelefteq G$ (`defect` $\le 2$) whenever every subgroup of $G$ is pronormal or contained in $N_{\mathrm{pro}}(G)$ (`Theorem`); and establishes exact tightness ($\text{defect} = 2$) via the locally graded modular $2$-group $M_{16} = \langle a, x \mid a^8 = x^2 = 1,\, x^{-1} a x = a^5 \rangle$ of order $16$ with non-normal subgroup $H = \langle x \rangle \trianglelefteq \langle x, a^4 \rangle \trianglelefteq M_{16}$.

### `targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques2/` — Refutation of Jensen (`arXiv:2605.27941v1`) Question 2 ($\mathsf{MLPS}_k$ Non-Convexity)

- **Claim**: Alathea Jensen, *Mixed thresholds in the Lonely Runner Conjecture*, [`arXiv:2605.27941v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27941) (2026-05-27, `main.tex`, lines 1138–1140), Section 5 (*Open questions and future work*), **Question 2**: for $k \ge 3$, off of the coordinate planes, is $\mathsf{MLPS}_k$ convex, or a finite union of convex sets? **Answered NO unconditionally for every $k \ge 3$**: constructs an explicit infinite sequence $\{Q_n^{(k)}\}_{n \ge 5} \subset (0, 1/2)^k$ (`§1`, with $Q_n^{(k)} = (x_n, x_n, y_n, \varepsilon_{n,k}, \dots, \varepsilon_{n,k})$ and $\varepsilon_{n,k} = \mu_n / (2k)$) satisfying $Q_n^{(k)} \in \mathsf{MLPS}_k$ via a uniform 3-runner safe Lebesgue measure bound $\mu(S_{123}(s_1, s_2, s_3)) \ge \mu_n = \frac{3}{22}\delta_n^2\eta_n$ across all distinct positive speed triples (`Lemmas 3a–3c`, `Lemma 4`), while every open segment $(Q_n^{(k)}, Q_m^{(k)})$ ($5 \le m < n$) exits $\mathsf{MLPS}_k$ via speed tuple $(2, 2n+1, 1, \dots)$ (`Lemmas 1–2`, including the midpoint $\frac{1}{2}(Q_5^{(k)} + Q_7^{(k)}) \notin \mathsf{MLPS}_k$ via $(2, 13, 1, \dots)$); by the Pigeonhole Principle, $\mathsf{MLPS}_k$ is neither convex nor a finite union of convex sets (`Theorem`).
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`
- **Result artifacts**: [`PROOF.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques2/PROOF.md), [`AUDIT.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques2/AUDIT.md), [`verify.py`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques2/verify.py), [`check-check-1.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques2/notes/check-check-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md)
- **Verification**:

### `targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.10/` — Proof of Vasilyev (`arXiv:2611.20344v2`) Question 7.10 (`q:supremum`, $\sup \pi_C(S) = \log_3 2$)

- **Claim**: Oleg Vasilyev, *Up or down at every integer: Collatz-type maps of arbitrary sets and the up-down graph*, [`arXiv:2611.20344v2`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20344) (2026-11-18, `updown.tex`), Subsection 5.4 (`sec:uniform`, lines 1486–1622; `Table 5.3`) and Section 7 (*Questions*), **Question 7.10** (`\label{q:supremum}`, lines 2049–2052): *"Is the supremum of $\pi_C(S)$ over all uniformly descending periodic sets $S$ equal to $\log_3 2$?"* **Answered YES unconditionally (`Main Theorem`, $\sup_{S, C} \pi_C(S) = \log_3 2$):** proves $\pi_C(S) < \log_3 2$ for every uniformly descending set $S$ periodic modulo $2^k$ and every closed class $C$ via the Bellman potential on $C$ (`Part 1`); and conversely, for every $\varepsilon > 0$, constructs an explicit uniformly descending set $S_n = S_0^{(n)} \setminus B_n$ periodic modulo $2^n$ such that every closed class $C$ of $\mathcal{R}_n(S_n)$ satisfies $\pi_C(S_n) > \log_3 2 - \varepsilon$ (and $\|\mu_C - \lambda_n\|_{\mathrm{TV}} < \varepsilon$) via exact $2$-adic De Bruijn isometry of dyadic-shell base sets $S_0(J)$ (`Lemma 1`), Barina first-exit sieve lockout (`Lemma 2`), Hoeffding decay (`Lemma 3`), hit-set path-uniqueness and nested-chain bound $|\mathcal{F}(y)| \le 2^{m-1}(L+1)$ (`Lemmas 4–5`), and De Bruijn telescoping (`Theorem 6`).
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`
- **Result artifacts**: [`PROOF.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.10/PROOF.md), [`AUDIT.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.10/AUDIT.md), [`verify.py`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.10/verify.py), [`check-check-2.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.10/notes/check-check-2.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.10/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.10/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`](targets/vasilyev-2611.20344-q7.10/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md)

### `targets/kovalyov-2611.32205-q1/` — Proof of Kovalyov (`arXiv:2611.32205v1`) Question 3.5(1)–(2) ($\mathsf{IOpen} + (\mathrm{Exp})$ Conservativity)

- **Claim**: Konstantin Kovalyov, *Some conservativity results for weak arithmetic theories in a language with exponentiation*, [`arXiv:2611.32205v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32205) (2026-11-24, `main.tex`, lines 499–505), Section 3 (*Conservativity results for $\mathsf{IOpen} + (\mathrm{Exp})$*), **Question 3.5(1)–(2)**: is $\mathsf{IOpen} + (\mathrm{Exp})$ conservative over $\mathsf{IOpen}$, and what is its $\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{OSR}}$-fragment? **Answered YES unconditionally**: $\mathsf{IOpen} + (\mathrm{Exp})$ is conservative over $\mathsf{IOpen}$ (with respect to all first-order $\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{OSR}}$-sentences), and hence its $\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{OSR}}$-fragment is $\mathsf{IOpen}$ itself (`Proof`): reduces consistency of $\mathrm{Th}_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{OSR}}}(\mathcal{M}_0^+) + (\mathrm{Exp})$ on a countable recursively saturated model $\mathcal{M}^+$ to winning strategies in the finite bounded-coefficient linear games $G_{n, M}(\mathcal{M}^+)$ (`§1`, `Lemma 1`), and constructs an explicit winning strategy via scale-separated $\mathbb{Z}$-$\mathsf{DOSR}$ bases $V_r$ and the inductive lexicographic invariant $\mathcal{I}_k$ across pinned (`Step 2`) and unpinned (`Step 3`, top-down closed intervals $I_r$ and $r = 1$ collision elimination) cases (`§2`).

### `targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques4/` — Resolution of Jensen (`arXiv:2605.27941v1`) Question 4 (Finite Speed-Set Determination of $\mathsf{MLPS}_k$)

- **Claim**: Alathea Jensen, *Mixed thresholds in the Lonely Runner Conjecture*, [`arXiv:2605.27941v1`](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27941) (2026-05-27, `main.tex`, lines 1158–1163), Section 5 (*Open questions and future work*), **Question 4**: *"For fixed $k$, is $\mathsf{MLPS}_k$ determined by finitely many speed sets?"* **Completely settled for every $k \ge 1$ (`Theorem`):**
  - **YES for $k \in \{1, 2\}$ (`§1`):** $\mathsf{MLPS}_1 = (0, 1/2] = U(\{(1)\})$ and $\mathsf{MLPS}_2 = \{(d_1, d_2) \in (0, 1/2]^2 : 2d_1 + d_2 \le 1,\; d_1 + 2d_2 \le 1\} = U(\{(1, 2), (2, 1)\})$ (by `Theorem A` of Jensen).
  - **NO for every $k \ge 3$ (`§2–§3`):** $\mathsf{MLPS}_k$ is not determined by any finite family of speed sets (ordered $k$-tuples or unordered $k$-element sets): proves that any finite speed-set domain $U(\mathcal{S})$ is a finite union of convex sets via half-integer breakpoint affinity (`Lemma 0`), whereas the explicit infinite sequence $\{Q_n^{(k)}\}_{n \ge 5} \subset \mathsf{MLPS}_k$ (`Lemmas 3a–3c, 4`) has every open segment $(Q_n^{(k)}, Q_m^{(k)})$ exiting $\mathsf{MLPS}_k$ (`Lemmas 1–2`), ruling out any finite union of convex sets.
- **Label**: `"written proof, internally audited"`
- **Result artifacts**: [`PROOF.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques4/PROOF.md), [`AUDIT.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques4/AUDIT.md), [`verify.py`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques4/verify.py), [`check-check-1.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques4/notes/check-check-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques4/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques4/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md), [`novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`](targets/jensen-2605.27941-ques4/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md)

### `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ1/` — Complete Permutation-Polynomial Classification for $g_{n,q}$ (`Question 6.1`, `arXiv:2606.01037v1`)

- **Problem statement**: Neranga Fernando & Bhitali Kousik, *A further study of polynomial $g_{n,q}$ over finite fields*, `arXiv:2606.01037v1` (31 May 2026), Section 6 (`draftMay31.tex`, lines 893–895, `\label{Q1}`, Question 6.1): for even $q = 2^m > 2$, even $e = 2k > 0$, and $n = (q-1) + (q-1)q^{e/2} + q^e$, determine when $g_{n,q}(X) = S_1(X) + S_{e/2}(X)^{q^{e/2}} + S_e(X) \cdot S_{e/2}(X)^{q-1}$ is a permutation polynomial (PP) over $\mathbb{F}_{q^e}$.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: Question 6.1 is completely classified: $g_{n,q}$ is a permutation polynomial of $\mathbb{F}_{q^e}$ **if and only if** either ($q = 4$ and $4 \mid e$) or ($e = 4$ and $m \not\equiv 3 \pmod 4$).
- **Proof summary**:
  - `Lemmas 2.1–2.2`: derives $g_{n,q}$ and reduces bijectivity on $\mathbb{F}_{q^e}$ via trace-kernel fibers to bijectivity of the $\mathbb{F}_q$-homogeneous map $\Phi(y) = y + S_k(y) + k\cdot t(y) + t(y)S_k(y^{q-1})$ on $V_k = \mathrm{im}(S_k)$.
  - `Case 1` ($e \equiv 2 \pmod 4$): never PP, exhibited by explicit collisions $x' = x + \mathrm{Tr}_{q^k/q}(x)$.
  - `Case 2` ($q = 4$, $4 \mid e$): always PP, via the invariant $\Phi(y)^4 + \Phi(y) = (y^2 + ty)^2$ and fiber equations $Q(A)^2 + Q(A) = \omega^2 A$ vs. $1 + Q(A) + Q(A)^2 = \omega^2 A$.
  - `Case 3` ($q \ge 8$, $4 \mid e$, $e \ge 8$): never PP, via odd degree $q - 3 \ge 5$ and the Lidl–Niederreiter (Thm 5.38) Carlitz–Uchiyama character-sum bound.
  - `Case 4` ($e = 4$): PP iff $m \not\equiv 3 \pmod 4$, via $x = u/(u+1) : U \xrightarrow{\sim} \mu_{q^2+1}\setminus\{1\}$, $\Phi(u)^{q^2} = x^{q+2}\Phi(u)$, $\Psi(u) = \Phi(u)/s(u) \leftrightarrow x^{-(q+2)}$, and $\gcd(2q-1, q^2+1) = \gcd(q+2, q^2+1) \in \{1, 5\}$.
- **Verification**: Independent checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ1/notes/check-check-3.py` (`PASS` across Parts 1–5 up to $\mathbb{F}_{2^{32}}$ and Table 1 triple `(307, 4; 4)`), `verify.py` (`PASS`, `14.2s`), Rabin irreducibility check on all 16 `IRRED_MODS`, blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `746` snapshots, `arXiv:2606.01037v1` sole version, clear).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ1/PROOF.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ1/AUDIT.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ1/verify.py`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ1/notes/check-check-3.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ1/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ1/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ1/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite-field computational checks up to $\mathbb{F}_{2^{32}}$ are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/` — Complete Permutation-Polynomial Classification for $g_{n,q}$ (`Question 6.3`, `arXiv:2606.01037v1`)

- **Problem statement**: Neranga Fernando & Bhitali Kousik, *A further study of polynomial $g_{n,q}$ over finite fields*, `arXiv:2606.01037v1` (31 May 2026), Section 6 (`draftMay31.tex`, lines 911–915, `\label{Q3}`, Question 6.3): for even $q = 2^m > 2$ ($m \ge 2$), even $e = 2k > 0$, and $n = 1 \cdot q^0 + (q - 1)\cdot q^{e/2} + (q - 1)\cdot q^e$, determine when $g_{n,q}(X) = S_{e/2 + 1}(X)^{q^{e/2}} + S_e(X) + S_e(X)\,(S_{e/2}(X)^{q^{e/2}})^{q - 1}$ is a permutation polynomial (PP) of $\mathbb{F}_{q^e}$.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: Question 6.3 is completely classified: $g_{n,q}$ is a permutation polynomial of $\mathbb{F}_{q^e}$ **if and only if** $q = 4$ and $4 \mid e$.
- **Proof summary**:
  - `Section 1` (`Lemma 1.1`, `Prop. 1.2`, `Lemma 1.3`): establishes the fiber bijection $x \mapsto S_k(x)^{q^k}$ between $g_{n,q}$ on $\mathbb{F}_{q^{2k}}$ and the $\mathbb{F}_q$-homogeneous map $\Phi_{\mathrm{Q3}}(z) = z + (k+1)t(z) + S_k(z) + t(z)S_k(z^{q-1})$ on $\mathrm{im}(S_k)$, and proves $\Phi_{\mathrm{Q3}}|_{\mathbb{F}_{q^k}}$ is an involution when $k$ is even.
  - `Section 2` (`Theorem 2.1`, $e \equiv 2 \pmod 4$, $k$ odd): never PP, since $g_{n,q}(1) = 0 = g_{n,q}(0)$.
  - `Section 3` (`Theorem 3.1`, $q = 4$, $4 \mid e$): always PP, via separation $s(z) \in t(z)\{\omega, \omega^2\}$, quartic invariant $\Phi_{\mathrm{Q3}}(z)^4 + \Phi_{\mathrm{Q3}}(z) = (z^2 + tz)^2$, and the contradiction $Q(A)^2 + Q(A) + \omega^2 A = 0$ vs. $1$ on cross-$t$ collisions.
  - `Section 4` ($q \ge 8$, $4 \mid e$, $e \ge 8$): never PP, via an odd-degree $q - 3 \ge 5$ polynomial $h_\lambda(u)$ and the Lidl–Niederreiter (Thm 5.38) Carlitz–Uchiyama character-sum lower bound $N_0 \ge q(5q - 4) > 0$.
  - `Section 5` (`Prop. 5.1`, `Lemmas 5.2–5.4`, `Theorem 5.5`, $e = 4$, $q \ge 8$): never PP, via descent to $N(w)$ on $H_1$ and the $s$-free moment obstruction $\sum_{w \in H_1} N(w)^{3(q-1)} = 1 \neq 0$ derived from a four-piece zero-carry binary-weight classification.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float zero-`/` checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/notes/check-check-1.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`, incl. Waring Eq. 1–2, exhaustive PP tests, Carlitz–Uchiyama count at $(8, 8)$, direct moments $m \in \{2..5\}$, and 4-piece parities $m \in \{3..5\}$), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`, plus independent tuple-for-tuple check $m \in \{3..6\}$ and direct moment check $m \in \{6..8\}$), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `760` snapshots, `arXiv:2606.01037v1` sole version, clear).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/PROOF.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/AUDIT.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/verify.py`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ3/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.

### `targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/` — Non-Reducibility of Two-Term Sums $B_r(y)$ Modulo $19$ (`Question 2`, `arXiv:2611.24516v1`)

- **Problem statement**: Göksal Bilgici, *Nine-Colored Partition Components Modulo 19*, `arXiv:2611.24516v1` (18 Nov 2026), Section 8 (*Questions*), Question 2 (`manuscript.tex`, line 1006; Remark 2.7 `\ref{rmk:contrast}`, line 400; Table 1 `\ref{tab:B}`, lines 346–366): determine whether the $18$ nonzero two-term sums $B_r(y)$ of Table 1 (`tab:B`) appearing in $f(q)^{10} \equiv P_0^2 \sum_{r=0}^{18} q^r B_r(y) \pmod{19}$ can be reduced to single products of the $P_a$ via relations among the $P_a$.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: **NO.** None of the $18$ nonzero two-term sums $B_r(y)$ ($r \in \{0, \dots, 18\} \setminus \{17\}$) in Table 1 (`tab:B`) — nor equivalently any quotient $\Psi_r/P_0 = B_r/\mathcal{D}$ in Table 2 (`tab:Psi`) — can be reduced to a single product $c \, y^v P_0(y)^{e_0} \prod_{a=1}^9 P_a(y)^{e_a}$ ($c \in \mathbb{F}_{19}^\times$, $v, e_0, \dots, e_9 \in \mathbb{Z}$, allowing negative exponents and arbitrary $y$-shifts and $P_0$-powers) modulo $19$ or identically in $\mathbb{Z}[[y]]$ via any relations among the $P_a$.
- **Proof summary**:
  - Normalizing $\widetilde{B}_r(y) = c^{-1} y^{-v} B_r(y) \in 1 + y\mathbb{F}_{19}[[y]]$ and taking the formal logarithmic derivative $D(F) = y F'(y)/F(y) = \sum_{n \ge 1} d_n(F) y^n \pmod{19}$ gives $D(P_0) \equiv 0 \pmod{(19, y^{19})}$ and $d_n \equiv -\sum_{1 \le a \le 9, a \mid n} a e_a + \mathbf{1}_{n \ge 10}(19-n)e_{19-n} \pmod{19}$.
  - The lower-triangular equations for $1 \le n \le 9$ (all $a \in \{1, \dots, 9\}$ invertible mod $19$) uniquely force $(e_1, \dots, e_9) \bmod 19$ from $(d_1, \dots, d_9)$, which captures all $e_a \in \mathbb{Z}$ simultaneously since $P_a(y)^{19} \equiv 1 \pmod{(19, y^{19})}$.
  - Checking the forced compatibility relations at $n = 10, 11$ yields $d_{11} \not\equiv d_{11}^{\mathrm{pred}} \pmod{19}$ for **all $18$** nonzero rows $r \in \{0, \dots, 18\} \setminus \{17\}$ (and $d_{10} \not\equiv d_{10}^{\mathrm{pred}} \pmod{19}$ for all $17$ rows with $r \neq 9$). Because every valid relation among the $P_a$ (such as Weierstrass's three-term theta relations in §7.1) holds identically upon substituting $P_a(y) = (y^a; y^{19})_\infty (y^{19-a}; y^{19})_\infty$, no relation among the $P_a$ can achieve a single-product reduction.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float zero-`/` checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/notes/check-check-1.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: independent $19$-dissection of $f(q)^{10} \pmod{(19, q^{361})}$ vs. `tab:B`, obstruction checks and direct product discrepancies for all $18$ nonzero rows), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`, plus independent TeX table parser & series-inversion cell-for-cell verification), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `760` snapshots, `arXiv:2611.24516v1` sole version, `trureturing#33370` Q1 only, clear).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/PROOF.md`, `targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/AUDIT.md`, `targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/verify.py`, `targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/bilgici-2611.24516-q2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite truncation checks in $\mathbb{F}_{19}[[y]]$ are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/` — Exact Determination $b(t) = 3$ for $t$-Neighbourhood Flip Graphs (`Section 7 Problem`, `arXiv:2312.08777v1`)

- **Problem statement**: Yair Caro, Josef Lauri, Xandru Mifsud, Raphael Yuster, and Christina Zarb, *Flip colouring of graphs*, `arXiv:2312.08777v1` (14 Dec 2023; published in *Graphs and Combinatorics* 40 (106), 2024, `DOI:10.1007/s00373-024-02838-w`), Section 6 (`sections/section6.tex`) and Section 7 (`sections/section7.tex`, final Problem): for $t \in \mathbb{N}$ with $t \ge 2$, determine $b(t) = \min\{b : ([t], (b, r))\text{-flip graph exists}\}$ (improving the paper's upper bound $b(t) \le 2^{t+1} - 1$).
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: **$b(t) = 3$ for every integer $t \ge 1$** (and in particular for every $t \ge 2$). No $([t], (b, r))$-flip graph exists with $b \in \{1, 2\}$, and for every $t \ge 1$ there exists a $([t], (3, 4))$-flip graph.
- **Proof summary**:
  - **Lower bound ($b(t) \ge 3$):** Evaluating $e_{2,1}[v] < e_{1,1}[v]$ at distance $s = 1$ gives $r - b < e_B(v) - e_R(v)$ for every $v \in V$. Summing triangle-type identities ($2\sum_v T_{RRB}(v) = \sum_v T_{BRR}(v)$ and $2\sum_v T_{BBR}(v) = \sum_v T_{BRB}(v)$) over all $v \in V$ yields $\sum_v (e_B(v) - e_R(v)) \le \sum_v (T_{BBB}(v) + T_{BBR}(v)) \le |V|\binom{b}{2}$, forcing $b + 1 \le r < \binom{b+1}{2}$, which fails for $b \in \{1, 2\}$.
  - **Upper bound ($b(t) \le 3$):** By the Erdős–Sachs Theorem (`Theorem 6.2`), take a finite connected $16$-regular simple graph $H$ of girth $g(H) \ge 2t + 2$. Replacing each $x \in V(H)$ by a blue $K_4$ clique $C_x$ and lifting each edge of $H$ to a red edge via four-part edge partitions $E_x(1), \dots, E_x(4)$ of size $4$ gives a simple $(3, 4)$-regular graph $G$. Since $B_H(x_0, t)$ induces a tree $T_{x_0}$ (`Claims 1–2`), the distance-$s$ layers split into entry and non-entry vertices $(A_s, B_s)$ obeying $A_1 = 4, B_1 = 3$, $A_{s+1} = 3A_s + 4B_s$, $B_{s+1} = 3A_s$ ($1 \le s \le t - 1$), and $5A_{s-1} \le A_s \le 6A_{s-1}$ ($2 \le s \le t$), giving $\Delta e_1 - \Delta e_2 = 6A_s - (3A_s + 4B_s) = 3A_s - 12A_{s-1} \ge 3A_{s-1} > 0$ for all $1 \le s \le t$.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/notes/check-check-2.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: triangle identities, exact recurrence across $1 \le s \le 200$, and explicit finite $([t],(3,4))$-flip graphs for $t=1$ ($128$ vertices), $t=2$ ($2048$ vertices via $\mathrm{AG}(2,16)$), and $t=3$ ($34952$ vertices via $W(16)$, $203$ sampled vertices)), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `760` snapshots, `arXiv:2312.08777v1` sole version, all citing papers verified unrelated to $b(t)$, clear).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/PROOF.md`, `targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/AUDIT.md`, `targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/verify.py`, `targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/notes/check-check-2.py`, `targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/notes/check-check-2.md`, `targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/caro-2312.08777-prob7/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite verification ($s \le 200$, explicit constructions for $t \le 3$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/` — Convergence, Normalized Volume Formula $c_{h,k}$, and Non-Monotonicity of Non-$B_h$ Density (`Question 2`, `arXiv:2611.07303v2`)

- **Problem statement**: Saad A. Awan, Steven J. Miller, Linh Nguyen, Steven Senger, and Elvis Wong, *Gaps in the Most Frequent Sizes of Integer Sumsets*, `arXiv:2611.07303v2` (9 Nov 2026), Section 5 (*Additional Results and Future Work*), Subsection 5.2 (*Questions*), Question 2 (`\label{q:rate}`, `sections/additional-results.tex`, lines 556–578): for $f_{h,k}(q) := q\bigl(1 - |\mathcal{B}_{h,k}(q)|/\binom{q}{k}\bigr)$, determine whether $f_{h,k}(q)$ converges for every $k \ge 4$, find its limit $c_{h,k}$ (and whether it equals a sum of normalized $(k-1)$-dimensional section volumes over $\mathbf{d} \in \mathcal{R}_{h,k}$), and determine whether $f_{h,k}(q)$ is eventually monotone in $q$.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**:
  1. **Convergence (`YES`):** For every fixed $h \ge 2$ and $k \ge 3$, $c_{h,k} := \lim_{q\to\infty} f_{h,k}(q)$ exists.
  2. **Normalized volume formula (`YES`):** $c_{h,k} = k! \sum_{\mathbf{d} \in \mathcal{R}_{h,k}} \frac{\operatorname{vol}_{k-1}(P_{\mathbf{d}})}{\|\mathbf{d}\|_2} = k! \sum_{\mathbf{d} \in \mathcal{R}_{h,k}} \frac{\operatorname{vol}_{k-1}(\pi_m(P_{\mathbf{d}}))}{|d_m|}$, where $P_{\mathbf{d}} = \{\mathbf{v} \in \mathbb{R}^k : 0 < v_1 < \cdots < v_k < 1,\ \mathbf{d}\cdot\mathbf{v} = 0\}$ and $\pi_m$ omits any coordinate with $d_m \neq 0$, recovering $c_{h,3} = 3\sum_{w=2}^h \varphi(w)/w$ and $c_{2,4} = 8$ term by term.
  3. **Eventual monotonicity (`NO`):** Not eventually monotone in $q$, proved via exact oscillations for $(h,k) = (3,3)$ ($f_{3,3}(6m+1) > f_{3,3}(6m)$ and $f_{3,3}(6m+2) < f_{3,3}(6m+1)$ for all $m \ge 1$) and strict step-by-step alternation for $(h,k) = (2,4)$ ($f_{2,4}(q+1) > f_{2,4}(q)$ for all even $q \ge 6$ and $f_{2,4}(q+1) < f_{2,4}(q)$ for all odd $q \ge 71$).
- **Proof summary**:
  - **Convergence & volume formula:** Bonferroni inequalities with `Lemma 3.2`, `Lemma 3.4`, and `Prop. 3.5` (pairwise linear independence on $\mathcal{R}_{h,k}$) reduce to counting $E_{\mathbf{d}}(q)$ up to $O(q^{k-2})$. Primitive $\mathbf{d}$ yields unimodular lattice covolume $\operatorname{covol}(\mathbb{Z}^k \cap \mathbf{d}^\perp) = \|\mathbf{d}\|_2$, unit-cube tiling gives $|E_{\mathbf{d}}(q)| = (\operatorname{vol}_{k-1}(P_{\mathbf{d}})/\|\mathbf{d}\|_2)q^{k-1} + O_{\mathbf{d}}(q^{k-2})$, and the Gram determinant $\|\mathbf{d}\|_2/|d_m|$ gives the coordinate-projection form.
  - **Non-monotonicity:** Exact finite counts on $q = 6m, 6m+1, 6m+2$ for $(3,3)$ and an exact period-$12$ quasipolynomial $P_{2,4}(q)$ (via all $10$ pairwise intersections of $R_1, \dots, R_5$ and second differences) give explicit positive/negative sign-alternating quadratics across all $12$ residue classes mod $12$ for $(2,4)$ at $q \ge 70$.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/notes/check-check-1.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: brute-force $B_h$-set checks, Gram identity, $k=3$ and $(2,4)$ volume sums, $(3,3)$ oscillations $1 \le m \le 500$, $(2,4)$ brute force $4 \le q \le 26$ and exact alternation $6 \le q \le 500$), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`, plus independent symbolic polynomial verification across all $12$ residue classes mod $12$), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `762` snapshots, `arXiv:2611.07303v2` sole latest, `richfrem#403`/`trureturing#29874` unrelated to `q:rate`, clear).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/PROOF.md`, `targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/AUDIT.md`, `targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/verify.py`, `targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/awan-2611.07303-q2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite verification ($m \le 500$, $q \le 500$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/` — Counterexample to the $(k+1)$-Metric Dimension Conjecture on 3D Grids (`Section 3 Conjecture 21`, `arXiv:2112.08768v1`)

- **Problem statement**: Mercè Mora, María José Souto-Salorio, and Ana Dorotea Tarrío-Tobar, *Resolving sets tolerant to failures in three-dimensional grids*, `arXiv:2112.08768v1` (16 Dec 2021; *Mediterr. J. Math.* **19** (2022), no. 4, art. 188, `DOI:10.1007/s00009-022-02096-1`), Section 3 (`FaultTolerantMD-3DGrids-arxiv.tex`, lines 901–911, Conjecture 21): determine whether $\alpha_m(n_1,n_2,n_3) \le k < \alpha_M(n_1,n_2,n_3)$ implies $\dim_{k+1}(P_{n_1}\Box P_{n_2}\Box P_{n_3}) = \min\{4k - 2\alpha_m(n_1,n_2,n_3) + 4,\; n_1n_2n_3 - (n_1-2)(n_2-2)(n_3-2)\}$.
- **Status label**: **counterexample verified by independent exact computation**
- **Result**: **FALSE.** Conjecture 21 fails at $(n_1, n_2, n_3, k) = (3, 3, 4, 13)$ (where $\alpha_m = 12 \le 13 < 15 = \alpha_M$): the conjectured value is $\min\{32, 34\} = 32$, whereas the explicit $30$-vertex set $S = F_{\mathrm{edge}} \cup F_{\mathrm{int}} = V \setminus M$ (omitting $\{(0,1,2),(2,1,2),(1,0,2),(1,2,2),(1,1,1),(1,1,2)\}$) is $14$-resolving on $P_3 \Box P_3 \Box P_4$, proving $\dim_{14}(P_3 \Box P_3 \Box P_4) \le 30 < 32$.
- **Witness & further counterexamples**:
  - For $P_3 \Box P_3 \Box P_4$, odd-distance pairs are resolved by all $30$ vertices of $S$ by bipartiteness, and exhaustive pairwise exact checking across all $\binom{36}{2} = 630$ pairs confirms every pair has at least $14$ resolving vertices in $S$ (minimum $14$ attained at $(0,0,1), (0,1,0)$).
  - The same frame-plus-$6$-face-centers construction $S$ of size $2\alpha_m + 6 < 4k - 2\alpha_m + 4$ also gives verified $(k+1)$-resolving counterexamples at $(3,3,5,15)$ ($|S| = 34 < 36$, min-res $= 16$), $(3,4,4,15)$ ($|S| = 34 < 36$, min-res $= 16$), and $(4,4,4,17)$ ($|S| = 38 < 40$, min-res $= 18$).
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/notes/check-check-3.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`, incl. BFS vs. $L_1$ metric check and exhaustive pairwise checks across all 4 counterexamples), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `762` snapshots, `arXiv:2112.08768v1` sole version, all citing papers verified non-settling, clear).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`, `targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/AUDIT.md`, `targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/verify.py`, `targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/notes/check-check-3.py`, `targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/notes/check-check-3.md`, `targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/mora-2112.08768-conj/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite exact computational verification are not external peer review or a Lean formalization; only the strict upper bound $\dim_{14}(P_3\Box P_3\Box P_4) \le 30 < 32$ (not exact equality) is claimed.

### `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/` — Complete Permutation-Polynomial Classification for $g_{n,q}$ (`Question 6.4`, `arXiv:2606.01037v1`)

- **Problem statement**: Neranga Fernando & Bhitali Kousik, *A further study of polynomial $g_{n,q}$ over finite fields*, `arXiv:2606.01037v1` (31 May 2026), Section 6 (`draftMay31.tex`, lines 923–926, `\label{Q4}`, Question 6.4): for even $q = 2^m > 2$ ($m \ge 2$), integer $e > 2$, and $n = (q - 1)\cdot q^0 + (q - 2)\cdot q^2 + q^e + q^{e+2}$, determine when $g_{n,q}(X) = X^q + S_e(X)^2 S_2(X)^{q - 2} + S_e(X) S_2(X)^{q - 1}$ is a permutation polynomial (PP) of $\mathbb{F}_{q^e}$.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: Question 6.4 is completely classified: $g_{n,q}$ is a permutation polynomial of $\mathbb{F}_{q^e}$ **if and only if** $(q, e) = (4, 4)$ (the Table 1 triple $(n, e; q) = (4387, 4; 4)$).
- **Proof summary**:
  - `Lemmas 2.1–2.2`: derives $g_{n,q}(X) \pmod{X^{q^e} - X}$ via Hou's recurrence and reduces bijectivity on $\mathbb{F}_{q^e}$ to nonvanishing of $s(y) = 1 + S_e(w(y))$ and bijectivity of $\Psi(y) = (y^q + S_2(w(y)))/s(y)$ on $\Omega_e$ ($H_e = \ker(S_e)$ for odd $e$; $D_e = \{\mathrm{Tr}_{q^e/q^2} = 1\}$ for even $e$).
  - `Case 1` ($e = 3$, all even $q > 2$): never PP via a pigeonhole irreducible cubic $T^3 + cT + c$ yielding $s(y) = 0$.
  - `Cases 2–3` (odd $e \ge 5$ or even $e \ge 8$, all even $q \ge 4$): never PP via the Carlitz–Uchiyama bound (with $\lambda$-scaling $N_F + N_G < q^{e-1}$ for odd $e \ge 5, q \ge 8$, and $\mathbb{F}_{q^2} \times \mathbb{F}_q$ reduction for even $e \ge 8$).
  - `Case 4` ($e = 6$, all even $q \ge 4$): never PP via explicit colliding pairs for $2 \le m \le 8$, and for $m \ge 9$ ($q \ge 512$) via an $\mathbb{F}_{q^2} \times \mathbb{F}_{q^3}$ ansatz, Kloosterman count, quadratic-resolvent involution, Gröbner absolute irreducibility, genus-$9$ unramified Artin–Schreier twist, and Hasse–Weil lower bound.
  - `Case 5` ($(q, e) = (4, 4)$): always PP via explicit 2-orbit action ($z \mapsto z^8$ and $z \mapsto z^4$) plus exhaustive $256$-element verification.
  - `Case 6` ($e = 4$, all $q = 2^m \ge 8$): never PP via explicit colliding pairs for $3 \le m \le 37$, and for $m \ge 38$ via $\mathbb{F}_q$-plane Möbius conjugation, degree-$25$ resultant $P_{\mathrm{gen}}$, $\mathbb{F}_{64}$ cycle types $[25]$ and $[2, 23]$ giving monodromy $G_{\mathrm{geom}} \supseteq A_{25}$, $2$-transitivity Galois descent with Bézout degree $\delta \le 288$, and Cafure–Matera Theorem 7.1 + Lemma 2.1.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/notes/check-check-2.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: Lemmas 2.1–2.2, Cases 1–6 incl. exact $\mathbb{F}_{32}$ mod-$t^4$ local expansion at $w^5 + w^4 + w^2 + w + 1 = 0$, $256$-element $(4,4)$ bijection, $\mathbb{F}_{64}$ monodromy, collisions for $m \le 8$ at $e=6$ and $m \le 37$ at $e=4$, and Cafure–Matera inequality for $m \ge 38$), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `787`–`790` snapshots, `arXiv:2606.01037v1` sole version, clear).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/PROOF.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/AUDIT.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/verify.py`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/notes/check-check-2.py`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/notes/check-check-2.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/fernando-2606.01037-quesQ4/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:

### `targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/` — Exponential Separation of $1\text{-SRC-II}$ ($\mathrm{SRC}\text{-}\mathrm{II}[1]$) from Resolution (`arXiv:2606.25895v1`, §§ 2 & 6)

- **Problem statement**: Nikita Gaevoy, *The Power of Small Symmetries*, `arXiv:2606.25895v1` (`2026-06-24`; MFCS 2026 LIPIcs Vol. 386, Art. 97), Section 2 (`static/definitions.tex`, lines 127–142) and Section 6 (`static/open.tex`, lines 3–5): determine whether Resolution with local $1$-symmetries ($1\text{-SRC-II}$ / $\mathrm{SRC}\text{-}\mathrm{II}[1]$) is p-equivalent to Resolution or strictly stronger (exponentially separated from Resolution).
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: **YES — exponential separation.** $1\text{-SRC-II}$ is exponentially separated from $\mathrm{Res}$ (and, via Gaevoy's toroidal-grid Tseitin family $T_n$, from bounded-depth Frege): there exists an unsatisfiable bounded-width CNF family $\{\Phi_n\}_{n \ge 2}$ on $N_n = \Theta(n)$ variables and $M_n = \Theta(n)$ clauses (width $\le 17$) with
  $$S_{1\text{-SRC-II}}(\Phi_n) = O(n^2) = O(N_n^2) \qquad \text{and} \qquad S_{\mathrm{Res}}(\Phi_n) = 2^{\Omega(n)} = 2^{\Omega(N_n)}.$$
- **Proof summary**:
  - **Guarded Tseitin construction (`Section 1`):** Introduces a single fresh guard literal $g \notin E$ and sets $\widetilde{\operatorname{Ts}}(G, \alpha) := \{C \lor \neg g : C \in \operatorname{Ts}(G, \alpha)\} \cup \{D \lor g : D \in \operatorname{All}(G)\}$.
  - **Exponential Resolution & Frege lower bounds (`Lemmas 1–4`):** Self-contained $8$-permutation probabilistic existence of bounded-degree connected expanders ($\Delta \le 16$, $e(G_n) > 1/8$), guard restriction $\{g \mapsto 1\}$ reducing $\widetilde{\operatorname{Ts}}(G, \alpha)$ cleanly to $\operatorname{Ts}(G, \alpha)$, Ben-Sasson–Wigderson Tseitin width lower bound $\ge e(G)n/3$, and size-width tradeoff yielding $S_{\mathrm{Res}}(\Phi_n) \ge \exp(n/147456)$ for $n \ge 816$ (and Håstad's Theorem 22 lower bound on guarded toroidal grids $\widetilde{T}_n$ for odd $n \ge 3$).
  - **Quadratic $1\text{-SRC-II}$ upper bound (`Theorem 5`):** Using $g$ as a local parity-flipping bridge, any $\mathcal{B} \vdash A \lor \neg g$ from $\mathcal{B} \subseteq \{C \lor \neg g : C \in \operatorname{Ts}(U, \alpha)\}$ simulates any even-degree-on-$U$ edge sign-flip symmetry $\phi_F$ via $|F| + 2$ consecutive local $1$-symmetry steps ($\phi_g \to \phi_{f_1} \to \dots \to \phi_{f_{|F|}} \to \phi_g$) strictly obeying Gaevoy's Definitions 1, 2, and 4; induction along a spanning tree derives $\neg g$, while $\operatorname{All}(v_1) \lor g$ yields $g$ and $\square$ in $O(n^2)$ steps.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float Cook–Reckhow `1-SRC-II` checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/notes/check-check-2.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: Lemma 1 exact bounds & concrete expanders, strict `1-SRC-II` checks for $K_2$–$M_{20}$, pure Resolution unfolding + Lemma 2 restriction, $H_3$ 4-cycle simulation), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `826` snapshots, ECCC `2026/347` + `/year/2026/` & MFCS 2026 clear, `arXiv:2606.25895v1` sole version).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/PROOF.md`, `targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/AUDIT.md`, `targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/verify.py`, `targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/notes/check-check-2.py`, `targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/notes/check-check-2.md`, `targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/gaevoy-2606.25895-src2-1/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and explicit Cook–Reckhow verification on finite graphs ($K_2$–$M_{20}$, $2 \le n \le 40$) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/` — Algebrization Barriers for $\mathrm{MA}[m,a] \subset \mathrm{AM}[*, p(a)]$ and Knop's Conjecture 1 (`Section 6 Open Questions 2 & 3`, `arXiv:2611.30127v1`)

- **Problem statement**: Nikolay Vereshchagin, *How Much Randomness is Needed to Convert MA Protocols to AM Protocols?*, `arXiv:2611.30127v1` (`2026-11-23`; CSR 2022 LNCS 13296 pp. 338–349, `DOI:10.1007/978-3-031-09574-0_21`; ECCC `TR22-060`), Section 6 (`main-eng.tex`, lines 774–780, Open Questions 2 & 3) and Section 1 (lines 101–106, 151–154): determine whether either $\mathrm{MA}[m, a] \subset \mathrm{AM}[*, p(a)]$ (for some fixed polynomial $p$) or Knop's Conjecture 1 ($\mathrm{Heur}\text{-}\mathrm{MA}[m, a] \subset \mathrm{Heur}\text{-}\mathrm{AM}[*, p(a)]$) can be proved by algebrizing techniques in the sense of Aaronson and Wigderson (`[AW]`).
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: **NO to both Open Question 2 and Open Question 3.** Constructs a single Boolean oracle $A : \{0,1\}^* \to \{0,1\}$ with Aaronson–Wigderson canonical multilinear extension $\widetilde{A}$ separating $\mathrm{MAP}^A[m, a] \cap \mathrm{MA}^A[m, a]$ from $\mathrm{PP}\!\cdot\!\mathrm{NP}^{\widetilde{A}}[m', a', t']$ whenever $a'(n) \le m(n) - \log_2(2(a'(n) + m'(n) + 2t'(n)^2) + 8) - 17$ for infinitely many $n$, via length-only languages. Consequently $\mathrm{MAP}^A[m, a] \cap \mathrm{MA}^A[m, a] \not\subseteq \mathrm{PP}\!\cdot\!\mathrm{NP}^{\widetilde{A}}[*, p(a)]$ (refuting algebrization of $\mathrm{MA}[m,a] \subset \mathrm{AM}[*, p(a)]$ and $\mathrm{MA}[m,a] \subset \mathrm{PP}[p(a)]$) and $\mathrm{MA}^A[m,a] \not\subseteq \mathrm{Heur}\text{-}\mathrm{AM}^{\widetilde{A}}[*, p(a)]$ (refuting algebrization of Knop's Conjecture 1).
- **Proof summary**:
  - **Gilbert–Varshamov packing (`Lemma 4.1`):** Exact integer bound $8 \cdot 27^{20} < 32^{20}$ gives canonical codes $\mathrm{Enc}_L : \{0,1\}^{\lfloor L/20 \rfloor} \to \{0,1\}^L$ with pairwise Hamming distance $> L/3$.
  - **Rectangle-shrinking & Ping-Pong restoration (`Lemma 5.1`):** Proves $\mathrm{OR}_M \circ \mathrm{EQ}_N$ has no low-randomness $\mathrm{PP}\!\cdot\!\mathrm{NP}^{\mathrm{cc}}$ protocol under $(\mathrm{P1})$–$(\mathrm{P4})$ via KL/Pinsker coordinate selection, Cauchy–Schwarz rectangle-shrinking ($\Delta = 2(a'+m'+C)+8$), and Ping-Pong $(0.9)$-block-min-entropy restoration with strict potential drop.
  - **Diagonalization & heuristic barrier (`Theorems 1 & 2`):** Prefix-free disjoint blocks $\mathcal{D}_{i,n}$, `Lemma 6.2` deterministic $C = 2t'(n)^2$-bit additive simulation $f_A(X) + f_B(W) + c_{\mathrm{fix}} \in \mathbb{F}_q$ of `[AW]` multilinear queries, stage-by-stage diagonalization, and a length-only $\delta = 1/10$ reduction from $\mathrm{Heur}\text{-}\mathrm{AM}^{\widetilde{A}}$ to $\mathrm{PP}\!\cdot\!\mathrm{NP}^{\widetilde{A}}$.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/notes/check-check-1.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: `main-eng.tex` Section 6 check, Lemma 4.1 packing & rational bounds, oracle layout & `(P1)`–`(P4)` feasibility, AW multilinear additive split over $\mathbb{F}_7$ & $\mathbb{F}_{49}$, and Step C Ping-Pong deficiency algebra), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `826` snapshots, ECCC `TR22-060` + `2026/347` + Crossref `0` cites, `arXiv:2611.30127v1` sole version).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/PROOF.md`, `targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/AUDIT.md`, `targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/verify.py`, `targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/vereshchagin-2611.30127-q2/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.

### `targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/` — Symmetric Depth-3 and Depth-4 Circuit Reductions and Orbit Bounds (`arXiv:2611.23541v1`, §1.2)

- **Problem statement**: Anuj Dawar, Prateek Dwivedi, Benedikt Pago, and Tim Seppelt, *Depth Hierarchies for Symmetric Algebraic Circuits*, `arXiv:2611.23541v1` (`cs.CC`, 17 Nov 2026), Section 1.2 (*Future work*, `introduction.tex`, lines 269–271): determine whether matrix-symmetric algebraic circuits can be reduced further to depth $3$ or $4$, and at what cost in orbit size.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: Resolves both the impossibility of poly/quasipoly-orbit reductions and matching subexponential-orbit upper bounds:
  1. **No polynomial- or quasipolynomial-orbit reduction to depth $3$ or $4$ (`Theorem 1`):** Even for $\mathsf{symVBP} \subseteq \mathsf{symVP}$ families (`hom_{P_d, n}`), reducing to depth $\Delta \in \{3, 4\}$ requires orbit size $\ge \binom{n}{\lfloor \min\{d, n/2\}^{1/\Delta}\rfloor} = 2^{\Omega(\min\{d,n\}^{1/\Delta}\log n)}$ whenever $\min\{d, n/2\} \ge 3^\Delta$ ($n \ge 54$ for $\Delta=3$; $n \ge 162$ for $\Delta=4$).
  2. **Sharp homogeneous depth-$4$ lower bound (`Lemma 1`, `Theorem 2`):** Via an $S_n$ group-action no-support-drop lemma at bounded-fan-in $\Pi$-gates ($r + 2k < n$), every homogeneous depth-$\le 4$ circuit of degree $\le d$ ($9 \le d < n/3$, $n \ge 28$) with $3\kappa_f(n) \le n + 2$ has support depth $\le 2$, giving orbit lower bound $\binom{n}{\lfloor \sqrt{d}\rfloor} = 2^{\Omega(\sqrt{d}\log n)}$.
  3. **Subexponential-orbit depth-$4$ reduction (`Lemma 2`, `Theorem 3`):** Via balanced $2$-level normalised tree decomposition collapse ($d \ge 1$), every family of degree $\le d(n)$ and orbit size $s(n) \le 2^{n^{1-\varepsilon}}$ over characteristic-$0$ $\mathbb{K}$ admits an unweighted depth-$4$ ($\Sigma\Pi\Sigma\Pi$) matrix-symmetric circuit (homogeneous below the top $\Sigma$-gate, and fully homogeneous when $p_n$ is homogeneous) of orbit size $\le \min\{n^{O(\kappa\sqrt{d(n)})}, (n!)^2\}$, support depth $\le 2$ when $6\kappa\sqrt{d(n)} < n/2$, and total size $\le n^{O(\kappa\sqrt{d(n)})}$ for single patterns $\hom_{F,n}$ ($d \le n^{2-\delta}$).
  4. **Subexponential-orbit depth-$3$ reduction (`Theorem 4`):** Over $\overline{\mathbb{K}}$ and (for $p_n \in \mathbb{Q}[\mathbf{X}_n]$) over $\mathbb{Q}$, via equivariant Fischer expansion, truncated exponential interpolation at $z_q = q^b$, and Primitive Element descent $L = \mathbb{Q}(\theta)$ ($[L:\mathbb{Q}] = 2^{O(\sqrt{d}\log d)}$), $p_n$ admits a weighted-$\Sigma$ depth-$3$ ($\Sigma\Pi\Sigma$) matrix-symmetric circuit (unweighted depth-$4$ with fan-in-$2$ scalar bottom $\times$-gates) of orbit size $\le \min\{n^{O(\kappa\sqrt{d(n)})}, (n!)^2\}$.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/notes/check-check-1.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: Theorems 1–2 thresholds & $S_n$ generation, Lemma 2 decomposition + pruning across $d \in \{1..100\}$ incl. non-squares, Theorem 3 depth-4 $P_4, P_5$ evaluation & $\Gamma_n$-invariance, Theorem 4 Fischer + truncated exp + $\mathbb{Q}[t]/(\mu)$ descent), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `849`–`930` cataloged repos, author `tseppelt/lax-symmetric-depth-reduction` formalizes `2611.23541v1` theorems only, ECCC `2026/347` clear, `arXiv:2611.23541v1` sole version).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/PROOF.md`, `targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/AUDIT.md`, `targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/verify.py`, `targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/dawar-2611.23541-sym-depth/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
