### `targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/` — $\mathsf{NP}$-Completeness of `WLCS` on $\{1^\star\}$-Bounded Complete Multipartite Switches (`arXiv:2611.32927v1`, §5)

- **Problem statement**: Christoph Kern, Alexander Firbas, and Manuel Sorge, *Algorithms and Complexity of Connectivity Switching in Graphs*, `arXiv:2611.32927v1` (24 Nov 2026; full version of LATIN 2026), Section 5 (`sections/conclusion.tex`, line 5; `WLCS` defined in `sections/stcon.tex`, lines 215–221): determine whether `Weighted Local-Con Switching` (`WLCS`) restricted to $\{1^\star\}$-bounded complete multipartite switches is solvable in polynomial time.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: **NO** (assuming $\mathsf{P} \neq \mathsf{NP}$). `WLCS` is $\mathsf{NP}$-complete even when restricted to $\{1^\star\}$-bounded $K_{2,2}$-shaped (complete bipartite, hence complete multipartite) switches, binary weights $w(s, i) \in \{0, 1\}$, and weight bound $\lambda = 0$.
- **Proof summary**:
  - Linear-time reduction ($O(|\widehat{G}|)$) from unweighted `Local-Con Switching` (`LCS`) restricted to $\{1^\star\}$-bounded $(K_2 + K_2)$-shaped switches, which is $\mathsf{NP}$-complete by Theorem `thm:dichotomy-lcs` (`sections/stcon.tex`, lines 149, 184–186).
  - By monotonicity of $a$--$b$ connectivity, empty positions are deleted and duplicates deduplicated so each switch $s$ has $s[0] = \{u_s v_s\}$, $s[1] = \{x_s y_s\}$; replacing $s$ with $\tilde{s}[0] = \{u_s v_s\}$ ($w = 0$), $\tilde{s}[1] = \{x_s y_s\}$ ($w = 0$), $\tilde{s}[2] = \{u_s y_s\}$ ($w = 1$), $\tilde{s}[3] = \{x_s v_s\}$ ($w = 1$) at $\lambda = 0$ yields a $\{1,1,1,1\}$-bounded $K_{2,2}$-shaped switch whose weight-$\le 0$ configurations are in exact bijection with configurations of $\widehat{G}$.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/notes/check-check-1.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: `REDUCTION_EXACT: checked=120, yes=48, no=72, lam1_false_positives=24`), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `854` snapshots / `935` cataloged repos, ECCC `2026/347` clear, `arXiv:2611.32927v1` sole version).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/PROOF.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/AUDIT.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/verify.py`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-wlcs-multipartite/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Conditional on $\mathsf{P} \neq \mathsf{NP}$; internal adversarial audit and exact finite verification (`120` instances) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/` — Polynomial-Time Algorithm for `WLCS` and `OOLCS` on $\{1,1\}$-Bounded Connected Switches (`arXiv:2611.32927v1`, §5)

- **Problem statement**: Christoph Kern, Alexander Firbas, and Manuel Sorge, *Algorithms and Complexity of Connectivity Switching in Graphs*, `arXiv:2611.32927v1` (24 Nov 2026; full version of LATIN 2026), Section 5 (`sections/conclusion.tex`, line 5; `WLCS`/`OOLCS` defined in `sections/stcon.tex`, lines 215–224): determine whether `On-Off Local-Con Switching` (`OOLCS`) restricted to $\{1,1\}$-bounded connected switches is solvable in polynomial time.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: **YES** — even the full weighted problem `Weighted Local-Con Switching` (`WLCS`, with arbitrary real weights, hence in particular `OOLCS`) restricted to $\{1,1\}$-bounded connected switches is solvable in polynomial time, specifically $O(|V^*|(|E^*| + |V^*|\log|V^*|)) = O(m^2 \log m)$ via Edmonds' minimum-weight perfect matching on an auxiliary graph $G^* = (V^*, E^*)$ with $|V^*| \le 2n + 6m$ and $|E^*| \le 9m + n$.
- **Proof summary**:
  - **Normalization & weight shifting (`Section 1`):** Shifting each switch $s$ by $m_s = \min_i w(s, i)$ yields non-negative edge costs $c(e) \ge 0$ against $\lambda' = \lambda - \sum_s m_s$; every $\{1,1\}$-bounded connected switch has either $1$ edge or $2$ edges sharing a center vertex $z_s$ ($P_3$-switch), so no two edges of the same switch are vertex-disjoint.
  - **Class-valid path & matching reduction (`Claims 1–2`):** Using at most one edge per switch reduces equivalently to finding a simple $a$--$b$ path using at most one edge per incident switch class $C_{v, s}$ at every vertex $v$, which reduces via half-edge gadgets $(e_u, e_v)$, class pairs $(c_{v,s}, c'_{v,s})$, and hubs $H_a, H_b$ (size $1$) / $H_v$ (size $2$) with non-negative costs $W^*(e_v c_{v,s}) = \frac{1}{2} c(e)$ to minimum-weight perfect matching on $G^*$ (eliminating non-simple walk bypasses and dropping $0$-or-positive-weight cycle components).
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/notes/check-check-1.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: `SIZE_WITNESS (n=3, m=2): |V*|=16, |E*|=17`, `WLCS_11CONNECTED_EXACT: checked=40, yes=16, no=24, trap=5`), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `854` snapshots / `935` cataloged repos, ECCC `2026/347` clear, `arXiv:2611.32927v1` sole version).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/PROOF.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/AUDIT.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/verify.py`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-oolcs-11connected/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite exact verification (`40` instances incl. non-simple walk traps) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/` — Counterexamples Refuting the `Global-Con Switching` Matroid-Switch Dichotomy (`arXiv:2611.32927v1`, §4)

- **Problem statement**: Christoph Kern, Alexander Firbas, and Manuel Sorge, *Algorithms and Complexity of Connectivity Switching in Graphs*, `arXiv:2611.32927v1` (24 Nov 2026; full version of LATIN 2026), Section 4 (`sections/gcon.tex`, lines 19–22, 116): conjecture that for any family of switches $\Pi$, `Global-Con Switching` (`GCS`) restricted to $\Pi$ is in $\mathsf{P}$ whenever all switches in $\Pi$ are matroid switches, and $\mathsf{NP}$-complete otherwise.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: **FALSE** (assuming $\mathsf{P} \neq \mathsf{NP}$; unconditionally refutes the characterization that only matroid-switch families lie in $\mathsf{P}$). Exhibits three explicit families of **non-matroid switches** for which `GCS` is solvable in polynomial time:
  1. **$\Pi_1$ (all $\{2,1\}$-bounded $K_3$-shaped switches, `O(n+m)`):** Every $s \in \Pi_1$ has $|s[0]| = 2, |s[1]| = 1$, $s[0] \cap s[1] = \emptyset$, fails matroid augmentation (`Claim 1`), and has a unique dominant size-$2$ position spanning all $3$ vertices, yielding an $O(n+m)$ BFS algorithm (`Theorem 1`).
  2. **$\Pi_2$ (incomparable $\{3,2\}$-bounded paw-graph $L_{3,1}$ switches, `O(n^2 m \log n)`):** Switches isomorphic to $s_2[0] = \{ax, xb, ab\}$, $s_2[1] = \{ax, ac\}$ induce incomparable partitions $\{\{a,x,b\},\{c\}\}$ vs. $\{\{a,x,c\},\{b\}\}$, fail matroid augmentation, and reduce component-preservingly to static $\{a_s x_s\}$ plus $\{1,1\}$-bounded $(\{a_s b_s\}, \{a_s c_s\})$ matroid switches (solvable in $O(n^2 m \log n)$ via Theorem `thm:gcs-matroid-switches`, and also placing `WGCS` in $\mathsf{P}$ via `thm:wgcs-matroid`).
  3. **$\Pi_3$ ($\{3,3\}$-bounded acyclic spanning-tree $K_4$-shaped switches, `O(n+m)`):** Disjoint spanning trees $s_3[0] = \{ab, bc, cd\}$, $s_3[1] = \{ac, bd, da\}$ fail matroid augmentation yet span identical connected components.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/notes/check-check-1.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: `Non-matroid axioms and GCS equivalences (Pi_1, Pi_2, Pi_3): PASS`), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `854` snapshots / `935` cataloged repos, ECCC `2026/347` clear, `arXiv:2611.32927v1` sole version).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/COUNTEREXAMPLE.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/AUDIT.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/verify.py`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/kern-2611.32927-gcs-dichotomy/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Conditional on $\mathsf{P} \neq \mathsf{NP}$ for the dichotomy refutation wording; internal adversarial audit and finite exact verification are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/` — FPT-Sized Regular Resolution Refutations Parameterized by $\mathrm{tw}_{\mathrm{plog}}^*(F)$ (`arXiv:2610.02047v1`, §8)

- **Problem statement**: Shaowei Cai and Ziqun Li, *Short Resolution Refutations for CNFs with Bounded Weighted Incidence Treewidth*, `arXiv:2610.02047v1` (`cs.CC`, 1 Oct 2026), Section 8 (*Conclusion and future directions*, `main.tex`, lines 4025–4031): determine whether every unsatisfiable CNF formula has an FPT-sized regular resolution refutation parameterized by partially log-weighted incidence treewidth $\mathrm{tw}_{\mathrm{plog}}^*(F)$.
- **Status label**: **written proof, internally audited**
- **Result**: **YES.** Every unsatisfiable CNF formula $F$ (without empty or tautological clauses, with $n$ variables, $m$ clauses, maximum clause width $k \ge 1$, and $w := \mathrm{tw}_{\mathrm{plog}}^*(F)$) admits a **regular resolution refutation** of length
  $$|\pi'| \le k^2(n+m)\, 2^{O(w \log(w+2))} \le (n+m)^3\, 2^{O(w \log(w+2))},$$
  strictly generalizing Theorem `thm:regular-main-bounds` from $\mathrm{tw}_{\log}^*(F)$ to $\mathrm{tw}_{\mathrm{plog}}^*(F)$ (and recovering FPT-sized regular resolution for $\mathrm{tw}_{\mathrm{os}}(F)$ via `thm:plog-one-sided`).
- **Proof summary**:
  - **Off-path width bound & post-order variable ordering (`Lemma 1`, `Observation 1`):** From `lem:plog-decomposition-size` (`|V(T)| = O(k(n+m)w)`), off-path clauses $c \in \chi_c(t) \setminus P_t$ satisfy $\prod_{c \in \chi_c(t) \setminus P_t} |c_t^{\mathrm{int}}| \le 2^{O(w \log(w+2))}$. Ordering $\mathrm{var}(F)$ by unique forget-variable post-order index $\mathrm{forg}(x)$ separates subtree variables (`Var_{t_1} \setminus \chi_v(t_0) < Var_{t_2} \setminus \chi_v(t_0) < \chi_v(t_0)`).
  - **Hybrid expansion & bottom-up regular `mRes` (`Sections 2–4`, `Lemma 2`):** Defines the hybrid multiclause target family $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{plog}}(t, \alpha, A)$ using $c_t^{\mathrm{ext}}$ for on-path $c \in B_t(A) \cap U_\alpha(A)$, $\mathcal{C}_1(c_t^{\mathrm{int}})$ for off-path $c \in A \setminus P_t$, and $\mathcal{C}_0(c_t^{\mathrm{int}})$ for off-path $c \in (\chi_c(t) \setminus P_t \setminus A) \cap U_\alpha(\chi_c(t))$, maintaining invariants `(P0)`–`(P3)` across all six nice-tree node types (`leaf`, `intro_c`, `intro_v`, `forget_v`, `forget_c`, `join`) via Safe Sequential Elimination (`Lemma 2`) and mapping via `lem:mres-to-res` to a regular resolution refutation $\pi'$.
- **Verification**: Independent zero-float checker `.venv/bin/python -u targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/notes/check-check-1.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: `JOIN_COUNTEREXAMPLE`, `COMPLEX_TD_MODES`, `AUDIT2_ADVERSARIAL_TDS: 5/5 + 27/27 join modes PASS`), `verify.py` (exit `0`, `PASS`: `35` exact cases), blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`), and Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-{1,5,6}`, `863` snapshots / `944` cataloged repos, ECCC `2026/347` clear, `arXiv:2610.02047v1` sole version).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/PROOF.md`, `targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/AUDIT.md`, `targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/verify.py`, `targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/cai-2610.02047-plog-reg/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite exact-arithmetic TD verification (`35` cases + `5/5 + 27/27` adversarial/join modes) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/myrisiotis-2611.09020-edit-graph/` (Dimitrios Myrisiotis, `arXiv:2611.09020v1`, Open Question `op:edit-graph` — written proof, internally audited)

- **Question (`op:edit-graph`)**: Does there exist a universal (or adjunction-universal, extendable) multitape Turing machine $V$ and a total computable length threshold $b(x)$ such that for every $x \in \{0,1\}^*$, every length $\ell \ge b(x)$, and every deadline $t$, every nonempty edit-graph fiber $\mathcal{E}^t_{\ell, V}(x)$ is connected under single-bit edits?
- **Result (YES)**: Explicit adjunction-universal multitape TM $V$ (extendable with interpretation property via `lem:interpretation-completion`) and total computable threshold $b(x) = N_x + B_x \le 2^{|x|+2} + O(|x|)$ ($B_x = 5 + |P_x| \ge 9$, $P_x = \phi(c(x))$ $\texttt{00}$-free, $N_x \le \max(2^{|x|+2}, 4B_x) + 2$). Using streaming-DFA parsing, Phase-3 tally cancellation ($L_0 + (L_1 + 1)B_x$ common time $T^*(n_{\mathrm{body}}, p', x) > C(n) > H(x)$), syndrome-guarded hubs (`lem:connected-syndrome-coloring`), and regular bridge language $\mathcal{U}$, constructive 1-edit walks connect every nonempty $\mathcal{E}^t_{\ell, V}(x)$ to the canonical hub $h_x = \texttt{1001}\,P_x\,\texttt{1000}$ for all $\ell \ge b(x)$ and all deadlines $t$.
- **Checks**: Blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`, label `written proof, internally audited`), Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-1`, `watch-5`, `watch-6`; ECCC `2026/347` clear, `arXiv:2611.09020v1` sole version), `verify.py` (`OVERALL: PASS`), and zero-float checker `notes/check-check-1.py` (`THRESHOLD_EPS: N_eps=34, B_eps=10, L79_bound=42`, `EDIT_GRAPH_WALKS: 12/12` start classes verified, `PASS`, `AST_FLOATS: []`).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/myrisiotis-2611.09020-edit-graph/PROOF.md`, `targets/myrisiotis-2611.09020-edit-graph/AUDIT.md`, `targets/myrisiotis-2611.09020-edit-graph/verify.py`, `targets/myrisiotis-2611.09020-edit-graph/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/myrisiotis-2611.09020-edit-graph/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/myrisiotis-2611.09020-edit-graph/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/myrisiotis-2611.09020-edit-graph/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/myrisiotis-2611.09020-edit-graph/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite exact-arithmetic walk verification (`12/12` start classes) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/say-2611.32633-bqtisp-sublog/` (A. C. Cem Say, `arXiv:2611.32633v1`, Section 5 — written proof, internally audited)

- **Question (Section 5, `main.tex` lines 1924–1928)**: Do strict polynomial-time quantum space hierarchy separations $\mathsf{BQTISP}\bigl(n^{O(1)}, o(s(n))\bigr) \subsetneq \mathsf{BQTISP}\bigl(n^{O(1)}, O(s(n))\bigr)$ (generalizing Theorem `thm:hier` at $\Theta(\log n / (\log\log n)^i)$) hold for more general space functions throughout $\omega(\log\log n) \cap o(\log n)$?
- **Result (YES)**: Proves Theorem 1 confirming the Section 5 belief: for every monotone (for large $n$), doubling-smooth ($s(2n) \le C s(n)$), log-constructible ($\mathrm{bin}(r) \mapsto \mathrm{bin}(s(2^r))$ in deterministic space $O(s(2^r))$ and time $2^{O(r)}$) space function $s(n) \in \omega(\log\log n) \cap o(\log n)$, there exists a two-track palindrome language $L_s \in \mathsf{TISP}(n^{O(1)}, O(s(n))) \setminus \mathsf{BQTISP}_{\mathbb{C}}(n^{O(1)}, o(s(n)))$, establishing strict hierarchies $\mathsf{BQTISP}\bigl(n^{O(1)}, o(s(n))\bigr) \subsetneq \mathsf{BQTISP}\bigl(n^{O(1)}, O(s(n))\bigr)$ and likewise for $\mathsf{BQTISP}_{\mathbb{C}}$. The proof combines an exact $O(\log\log n)$-space bit-length estimate $\tilde{r}_k$ with $2^{\tilde{r}_k} \le N_k < 2^{\tilde{r}_k+2}$ (Lemma 1), an $O(s(n))$-space / $n^{O(1)}$-time DTM upper bound (Lemma 2), and Remscrim's QTM tradeoff `thm:remscrimQTMtradeoff` via 3-step doubling smoothness giving $D_{L_s}(n) \ge 2^{2^{d_0 s(n)}}$ with $d_0 = \frac{1}{2}C^{-3}$ (Lemma 3).
- **Checks**: Blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`, label `written proof, internally audited`), Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-1`, `watch-5`, `watch-6`; ECCC `2026/347` clear, `arXiv:2611.32633v1` sole version), `verify.py` (`OVERALL: PASS`), and zero-float checker `notes/check-check-1.py` (`LEMMA1_EXACT: k=1..5000 string_len_ok=True, k=128..35000 bounds_ok=True`, 4 sublogarithmic families `PASS`, `AST_FLOATS: []`, `FLOAT_CALLS: []`).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/say-2611.32633-bqtisp-sublog/PROOF.md`, `targets/say-2611.32633-bqtisp-sublog/AUDIT.md`, `targets/say-2611.32633-bqtisp-sublog/verify.py`, `targets/say-2611.32633-bqtisp-sublog/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/say-2611.32633-bqtisp-sublog/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/say-2611.32633-bqtisp-sublog/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/say-2611.32633-bqtisp-sublog/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/say-2611.32633-bqtisp-sublog/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite exact-integer verification (`k <= 35000` and 4 representative families) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.

### `targets/radici-2609.07769-open/` (Gabriele Radici & Massimiliano Sala, `arXiv:2609.07769v2`, Section 9 — written proof, internally audited)

- **Question (Section 9, `main-v32.tex` lines 1498–1527)**: Does the binary zero-count gap inequality $\alpha_1(q, n, d) > \alpha_0(q, n, d)$ (`eq:main` of Section 7 Theorem `thm:main`) extend to all three open finite fields $q \in \{3, 4, 5\}$ across all $n \ge 2$ and all reduced degrees $2 \le d \le n(q-1)$?
- **Result (YES)**: Settles all three Section 9 open problems unconditionally: $\alpha_1(q, n, d) > \alpha_0(q, n, d)$ holds for every $q \in \{3, 4, 5\}$, every $n \ge 2$, and every $2 \le d \le n(q-1)$ (plus confirms the reversal $\alpha_1(q, n, 2) < \alpha_0(q, n, 2)$ for every prime power $q \ge 7$ and all sufficiently large $n$). The proof combines evaluation-matroid simplicial port-splitting (`cor:pointed-reduction-full`, `lem:interval`, `lem:shadow`), Affine Dimension Lemma 1, exact $\mathrm{GRM}_q(d, 2)^\perp$ and $\mathrm{GRM}_5(2, 3)^\perp$ dual enumerators ($A_6 = 8{,}060{,}000$, $A_7 = 81{,}840{,}000$), and normalized $q$-ary MacWilliams Taylor/moment bounds (Lemmas 4–5).
- **Checks**: Blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`, label `written proof, internally audited`), Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-1`, `watch-5`, `watch-6`; ECCC `TR26-347` and IACR `2026/2022` clear, `arXiv:2609.07769v2` latest), `verify.py` (`OVERALL: PASS`), and zero-float checker `notes/check-check-1.py` (`BASE_N2` exact for all 15 `(q,d)` pairs with `q in {3,4,5}`, `LEMMA5_COEFFS`, `CASE2..CASE6`, exact `GRM_5(2,3)^perp` MacWilliams tail, `PASS`, `AST_FLOATS: []`, `FLOAT_CALLS: []`).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/radici-2609.07769-open/PROOF.md`, `targets/radici-2609.07769-open/AUDIT.md`, `targets/radici-2609.07769-open/verify.py`, `targets/radici-2609.07769-open/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/radici-2609.07769-open/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/radici-2609.07769-open/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/radici-2609.07769-open/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/radici-2609.07769-open/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:

### `targets/dobson-2611.13347-ising-clifford/` (Sawyer Dobson & Zhenghan Wang, `arXiv:2611.13347v1`, Section 5 Problem (3) — written proof, internally audited)

- **Question (Section 5, `sections/5.tex` line 11, Problem (3))**: Does ancilla-assisted dense-encoded Ising anyon braiding (with $|0^a\rangle$ ancillas) still fail to capture all $n$-qubit Clifford gates for $n \ge 3$, and how do Ising complexity classes compare to $\mathbf{L}^{\texttt{STAB}}$, $\mathbf{PUSC}$, and $\mathbf{LUSC}$ under braiding-only vs. measurement-assisted circuit models?
- **Result (Resolved)**: Settles all parts of Problem (3):
  1. **Ancilla-assisted braiding no-go:** Even with arbitrary finite $a \ge 0$ ancillas initialized to $|0\rangle$, dense-encoded Ising braiding cannot simulate all $n$-qubit Clifford gates for any $n \ge 3$ — neither in the standard register convention (even $H_1$ is impossible for every $a \ge 0$ and output placement $D'$) nor under arbitrary input/output wire placements $D, D'$ (even $C_{\mathrm{GHZ}} = H_0 \mathrm{CNOT}_{2,0} \mathrm{CNOT}_{1,0}$ is impossible for every $a \ge 0$), proved via Majorana weight invariance $w_\gamma(B P B^\dagger) = w_\gamma(P)$.
  2. **Braiding-only complexity classes:** Strictly weaker than Clifford classes — $\texttt{ISING-BRAID} \in \mathbf{L}$ (so $\mathbf{L}^{\texttt{ISING-BRAID}} = \mathbf{L} \subseteq \oplus\mathbf{L} = \mathbf{L}^{\texttt{STAB}}$), and unconditionally $\mathbf{LUSB} \subsetneq \mathbf{LUSC}$ and $\mathbf{PUSB} \subsetneq \mathbf{PUSC}$ (via Proposition 3.7 `prop:5050` and `PARITY`).
  3. **1-ancilla measurement-assisted equivalence:** With one $|0\rangle$ ancilla, interferometric measurements ($\mathcal{M}_1 = Z_j Z_n$, $\mathcal{M}_2 = X_n$) and conditional Pauli braids deterministically simulate every local Clifford generator in $O(n)$ operations and arbitrary-range $\mathrm{CNOT}_{c,t}$ in $O(n^2)$ operations; together with deferred measurement, $\mathbf{L}^{\texttt{ISING-MEAS-PAULI}} = \mathbf{L}^{\texttt{STAB}} = \oplus\mathbf{L}$, $\mathbf{PUSI} = \mathbf{PUSC} = \mathbf{AFF} \cap \mathbf{P}$, and $\mathbf{LUSI} = \mathbf{LUSC}$.
- **Checks**: Blind adversarial audit (`AUDIT.md`, label `written proof, internally audited`), Gate 3 novelty checks (`watch-1`, `watch-5`, `watch-6`; ECCC `TR26-347` clear, `arXiv:2611.13347v1` sole version), `verify.py` (`OVERALL: PASS`), and exact $\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{16})$ zero-float checker `notes/check-check-1.py` (`MAJORANA_AND_NOGO: PASS`, `ISING_BRAID_TRACKER: 50/50`, `ONE_ANCILLA_GADGET_EXACT: 16/16 + CZ`, `PASS`, `AST_FLOATS: []`, `FLOAT_CALLS: []`).
- **Artifacts**: `targets/dobson-2611.13347-ising-clifford/PROOF.md`, `targets/dobson-2611.13347-ising-clifford/AUDIT.md`, `targets/dobson-2611.13347-ising-clifford/verify.py`, `targets/dobson-2611.13347-ising-clifford/notes/check-check-1.py`, `targets/dobson-2611.13347-ising-clifford/notes/check-check-1.md`, `targets/dobson-2611.13347-ising-clifford/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-1.md`, `targets/dobson-2611.13347-ising-clifford/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-5.md`, `targets/dobson-2611.13347-ising-clifford/notes/novelty-gate3-watch-6.md`.
- **What is not established**:
  - Internal adversarial audit and finite exact $\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{16})$ verification (`50/50` tracker tests and `16/16` gadget branches) are not external peer review or a Lean formalization.
